[`FeatureFlagOverrideMode`](#featureflagoverridemode) is `Off` (the kill switch), `On`, `Percentage`,
or `Targeting`
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideMode.cs:12-28`),
translated into a `FeatureDefinition` by [`FeatureFlagDefinitions`](#featureflagdefinitions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagDefinitions.cs:33`).
They live in the owning service's own database behind
[`IFeatureFlagOverrideStore`](#ifeatureflagoverridestore), implemented by
[`EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore`](#effeatureflagoverridestore)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/FeatureFlags/EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore.cs:33`),
in a `FeatureFlagOverrides` table
([`FeatureFlagOverrideModelBuilderExtensions`](#featureflagoverridemodelbuilderextensions),
`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideModelBuilderExtensions.cs:18`)
that enters the model only when the marker
[`FeatureFlagOverrideModelGate`](#featureflagoverridemodelgate) is registered, so a host that never
opts in keeps a byte-identical model and sees no migration
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideModelGate.cs:3-11`).
Reads go through [`IFeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot`](#ifeatureflagoverridesnapshot), whose
[`FeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot`](#featureflagoverridesnapshot)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot.cs:46`)
holds the whole override table as one entry in the host's
[`ICacheService`](group-09-caching.md#icacheservice) for `OverrideCacheSeconds` (default 30,
`FeatureFlagOverrideSettings.cs:24`), keyed by both the application name and the data source so two
services sharing one Redis never read each other's snapshot (`FeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot.cs:27-32`),
and invalidated by prefix on a write so every replica of a two-level-cache host stops addressing the
old snapshot (`:18-24`, `:70-71`). The administration half is
[`IFeatureFlagAdministrationService`](#ifeatureflagadministrationservice), implemented by the internal
[`FeatureFlagAdministrationService`](#featureflagadministrationservice)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagAdministrationService.cs:30`,
`ListAsync`, `SetOverrideAsync`, and `ClearOverrideAsync` at lines 45, 62, and 86), which validates a
name against the [`FeatureFlagCatalog`](#featureflagcatalog)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagCatalog.cs:17`, built from
the registered [`FeatureFlagAssembly`](#featureflagassembly) records at line 9 through
[`FeatureFlagRegistry`](#featureflagregistry), which reads every `public const string` on a static
`*Features` class into a [`FeatureFlagDescriptor`](#featureflagdescriptor),
`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagRegistry.cs:17`, `:35`, and `:46`,
each declared with its [`FeatureFlagAttribute`](#featureflagattribute) and
[`FeatureFlagLifetime`](#featureflaglifetime),
`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagAttribute.cs:32`). It is exposed
over HTTP by [`FeatureFlagsControllerBase`](#featureflagscontrollerbase)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Administration/FeatureFlagsControllerBase.cs:38`,
gated on `AdministrationPermissions.ManageFeatures` at line 37: `GET` at line 49, `PUT {name}` at
line 68, `DELETE {name}` at line 93). The wire vocabulary is the Shared records
[`SetFeatureFlagOverrideRequest`](#setfeatureflagoverriderequest)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/SetFeatureFlagOverrideRequest.cs:18`),
[`FeatureFlagOverrideResponse`](#featureflagoverrideresponse) (`FeatureFlagOverrideResponse.cs:14`),
[`FeatureFlagStatusResponse`](#featureflagstatusresponse) (`FeatureFlagStatusResponse.cs:15`), and the
[`FeatureFlagValueKind`](#featureflagvaluekind) enum (`FeatureFlagValueKind.cs:10`)
([ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html)).

**The newer edge surface: opt-in actions, declarative caching, view-as, and the composed host.**
Several smaller pieces extend the edge without changing a host that ignores them. Two marker
attributes opt an entity controller in to extra list actions:
[`SupportsCursorPagingAttribute`](#supportscursorpagingattribute)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Pagination/SupportsCursorPagingAttribute.cs:14`, the
keyset list served by the query service's `GetPageByCursorAsync`, `:4-11`) and
[`SupportsChangeFeedAttribute`](#supportschangefeedattribute)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Pagination/SupportsChangeFeedAttribute.cs:14`, the
change feed served by `GetChangesSinceAsync`, `:4-11`). Both are enforced by the internal
[`OptInActionAttribute`](#optinactionattribute)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Pagination/OptInActionAttribute.cs:17`), an
`IActionModelConvention` that removes the base action from any controller that did not opt in, so it
has no route and no API description (`:6-13`). Output caching gains two declarative forms:
[`PublicCachedReadsAttribute`](#publiccachedreadsattribute)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/PublicCachedReadsAttribute.cs:42`, an
`IControllerModelConvention`) marks the inherited base reads anonymous and output-cached under one
named policy and names the by-id route, so a controller no longer overrides a read only to add
attributes (`:9-21`), and [`EvictOutputCacheAttribute`](#evictoutputcacheattribute)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/EvictOutputCacheAttribute.cs:36`, an
`IAsyncResultFilter`) evicts its tags after a 2xx write and before the body is written, evicts nothing
on a non-2xx, a GET or HEAD, or an unhandled exception, and logs rather than fails when eviction throws
(`:12-27`). Rate limiting gains [`RateLimitPartitions`](#ratelimitpartitions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/RateLimiting/RateLimitPartitions.cs:11`), partition
factories for a host's own policies built on the framework's limiters (`:7-9`), and the in-memory
fixed window now runs through [`RemainingWindowFixedWindowRateLimiter`](#remainingwindowfixedwindowratelimiter)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Build/RateLimiting/RemainingWindowFixedWindowRateLimiter.cs:31`), one internal
source file linked into each package that runs such a window, which rewrites the 429's `Retry-After`
hint from the whole window length to the time actually remaining (`:6-22`), handing back a
[`RejectedLease`](#rejectedlease) that carries only that hint (`:133-137`). Read-only support access
([ADR-137](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/137-read-only-view-as.html)) has its HTTP half in
[`ViewAsGuardMiddleware`](#viewasguardmiddleware)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Middleware/ViewAsGuardMiddleware.cs:42`): for a
principal carrying `act_sub` it refuses every method other than GET, HEAD, and OPTIONS with 403, except
the action marked by [`ViewAsEndEndpointAttribute`](#viewasendendpointattribute) (`:245`) and an
endpoint marked read-only, and it re-checks that the grant is still live on every request, answering
401 otherwise (`:13-33`). Unlike the soft-deleted-user gate it fails **closed**, because an outage then
costs one support session rather than a write in someone else's name (`:35-39`). A service that does
not hold the grant store checks the shared-cache [`ViewAsEndedMarker`](#viewasendedmarker)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Middleware/ViewAsEndedMarker.cs:32`) instead, written
only by the end endpoint (`:16-26`). The controller tower grows to match. Under
`Controllers/Administration`, [`AuditHistoryControllerBase`](#audithistorycontrollerbase)
(`AuditHistoryControllerBase.cs:48`, `GET {entity}/{key}` at line 93 behind
`AdministrationPermissions.ViewAuditTrail` at line 47) and
[`ViewAsGrantsAdminControllerBase`](#viewasgrantsadmincontrollerbase)
(`ViewAsGrantsAdminControllerBase.cs:40`, behind `ManageUsers` at line 39) join the users and roles
bases; [`MachineClientsControllerBase`](#machineclientscontrollerbase)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/MachineClients/MachineClientsControllerBase.cs:39`,
behind `ManageClients` at line 38: list, create, and `rotate`, `promote`, and `revoke` at lines 48,
68, 93, 115, and 140) manages service credentials that
[`MachineTokenControllerBase`](#machinetokencontrollerbase) (`MachineTokenControllerBase.cs:40`)
exchanges for a token through one anonymous POST behind `auth-ip` (lines 45-48). Three more
self-service sign-in bases follow the same app-owns-the-command pattern as the recovery bases:
[`SignInLinkControllerBase<TSendCommand, TRedeemCommand>`](#signinlinkcontrollerbasetsendcommand-tredeemcommand)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/SignInLinks/SignInLinkControllerBase.cs:35`,
anonymous `sign-in-link` and `sign-in-link/redeem` behind `auth-ip`, lines 80-83 and 108-111),
[`PasskeysControllerBase`](#passkeyscontrollerbase)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Passkeys/PasskeysControllerBase.cs:41`,
`[Authorize]` registration and management from line 62, anonymous `signin/options` and `signin`
behind `auth-ip` at lines 229-232 and 254-257), and
[`TwoFactorControllerBase<TBeginCommand, TConfirmCommand, TDisableCommand, TRegenerateCommand>`](#twofactorcontrollerbasetbegincommand-tconfirmcommand-tdisablecommand-tregeneratecommand)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/TwoFactor/TwoFactorControllerBase.cs:60`,
status, enrollment, confirmation, recovery codes, and disable at lines 92, 119, 150, 190, and 219,
every action plain `[Authorize]` rather than a permission, `:28`). External sign-in now also accepts
generic OIDC providers: each [`OidcProviderSettings`](#oidcprovidersettings)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Authentication/OidcProviderSettings.cs:20`) is read
from `OAuth:Oidc:<Name>` (`:8-10`), the configured set is held by
[`OidcProviderRegistry`](#oidcproviderregistry) (`OidcProviderRegistry.cs:10`, registered only when at
least one provider exists, `:4-7`), and [`OidcClaimNormalizer`](#oidcclaimnormalizer)
(`OidcClaimNormalizer.cs:21`) maps the validated ID token onto the app's claim shape, keyed by a stable
`oid` or `{iss}|{sub}` and never by the mutable email (`:6-18`). On the outbound side,
[`OutboundAddressPolicy`](#outboundaddresspolicy)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Http/OutboundAddressPolicy.cs:11`) holds an
operator-supplied URL to public addresses, at validation time and again at connect time, so a stored
webhook target cannot reach the host's own network (`:7-9`, the IPv6 allow-list at `:35-38`). Finally,
the separate `MMCA.Common.ServiceHost` package composes all of this for an extracted service:
[`ServiceHostBuilderExtensions`](#servicehostbuilderextensions)'s `AddMmcaServiceHost`
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.ServiceHost/ServiceHostBuilderExtensions.cs:65`) runs the
start-up list every extracted host repeated, in the one correct order, taking vendor calls only as
callbacks so the package stays cloud-neutral (`:21-24`, ADR-059); what differs between hosts arrives
through [`MmcaServiceHostOptions`](#mmcaservicehostoptions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.ServiceHost/MmcaServiceHostOptions.cs:16`, defaulting to an
HTTP/2 cleartext endpoint at line 42 and
[`ServiceHostAuthentication`](#servicehostauthentication)`.ForwardedJwtBearer` at line 52), and the
returned [`MmcaServiceHost`](#mmcaservicehost) (`MmcaServiceHost.cs:11`) is what
[`ServiceHostApplicationExtensions`](#servicehostapplicationextensions)'s `UseMmcaServiceHostAsync`
(`ServiceHostApplicationExtensions.cs:44`) takes to apply the post-`Build()` pipeline, with the
internal [`ServiceHostOpenApi`](#servicehostopenapi) (`ServiceHostOpenApi.cs:10`) and
[`ServiceHostProfiling`](#servicehostprofiling) (`ServiceHostProfiling.cs:11`, the MiniProfiler step)
as its helpers. [Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness], [Rubric §11, Security], and [Rubric §12,
Performance & Scalability] again, each piece opt-in.

**Per-module controller visibility.** Back on the feature-gating thread, at a coarser grain,
[`ModuleControllerFeatureProvider`](#modulecontrollerfeatureprovider)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/ModuleControllerFeatureProvider.cs:28`) removes a
disabled module's controllers from MVC discovery entirely, snapshotting the disabled names once (lines
36-39), returning immediately when none are disabled (lines 41-44), and matching a `.{ModuleName}.`
token against the controller's assembly name or namespace (lines 60-81, the dots on both sides
preventing a `Catalogue` false positive on `Catalog`), so a module switched off in configuration
cannot have its routes mapped (they would otherwise 500, since the module's DI services were never
registered). Together these are the feature-flag story extended to the HTTP edge and part of the
[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness] "one codebase, many deployment shapes" design.

**Well-known endpoints, database initialization, and the extraction edge.** Several types here exist
only so a module can be lifted out of the monolith into its own service (ADRs
[004](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/004-authentication-dual-fetch.html),
[007](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/007-grpc-extraction.html),
[008](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/008-service-extraction-topology.html)).
[`JwksEndpointExtensions`](#jwksendpointextensions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/Endpoints/JwksEndpointExtensions.cs:15`) serves
`/.well-known/jwks.json` (path constant at line 20, mapped at line 33) from
[`IJwksProvider`](group-08-auth.md#ijwksprovider) so extracted services validate tokens against the
issuer's public keys instead of a shared secret, and
[`OidcDiscoveryEndpointExtensions`](#oidcdiscoveryendpointextensions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/Endpoints/OidcDiscoveryEndpointExtensions.cs:22`)
serves the minimal discovery document the JWT middleware fetches when `AddForwardedJwtBearer` sets an
authority: it returns 404 when `Jwt:Issuer` is not configured (lines 62-66), derives `jwks_uri` from
that configured issuer rather than from the inbound request (line 76) so issuer and JWKS URI stay
origin-aligned, and disables the camelCase naming policy (line 45) because RFC 8414 field names are
snake_case and `jwksUri` would not be recognized. `AddForwardedJwtBearer` itself resolves
`RequireHttpsMetadata` in three steps, explicit argument, then the
`Authentication:JwtBearer:RequireHttpsMetadata` key (constant at
`WebApplicationBuilderExtensions.Authentication.cs:25`), then "true outside Development"
(`WebApplicationBuilderExtensions.Authentication.cs:64-66`); a resolved `false` outside Development is honored,
because an internal-ingress h2c authority genuinely has no HTTPS metadata, but it is never silent:
[`InsecureJwtMetadataWarningStartupFilter`](#insecurejwtmetadatawarningstartupfilter)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/Auth/InsecureJwtMetadataWarningStartupFilter.cs:15`)
is registered exactly then (`WebApplicationBuilderExtensions.Authentication.cs:68-72`) and logs one startup warning
naming the key (`InsecureJwtMetadataWarningStartupFilter.cs:21` with the message at `:26-28`). It is an
`IStartupFilter` rather than a log line at registration time for a reason worth remembering: while the
service collection is being built the logging providers are not configured yet, so a warning written
there is dropped (`InsecureJwtMetadataWarningStartupFilter.cs:7-13`).
[`JwtForwardingDelegatingHandler`](#jwtforwardingdelegatinghandler)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Http/JwtForwardingDelegatingHandler.cs:17`) copies
the caller's inbound `Authorization` header onto outgoing HTTP calls, unless one was already set (lines
27-30) and no-oping when there is no ambient `HttpContext` (lines 32-36), so distributed authorization
flows through a service-to-service hop without any handler threading the token by hand: the HTTP twin
of [`JwtForwardingClientInterceptor`](group-13-grpc-contracts.md#jwtforwardingclientinterceptor).
[`DatabaseInitializationExtensions`](#databaseinitializationextensions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/DatabaseInitializationExtensions.cs:28`)
initializes every physical data source the host owns: `InitializeDatabaseAsync` (line 51) validates the
strategy **before** touching anything (`EnsureKnownStrategy`, called at line 63 and declared at line
290, which accepts only `Migrate` or `None` and otherwise throws naming the valid values through
`UnknownStrategy` at line 302), warms the
[`IEntityDataSourceRegistry`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ientitydatasourceregistry) so
entity-to-database routing is deterministic before the first repository call (lines 70-72), runs
`EnsureCreated` for the migration-less Cosmos, PostgreSQL, and SQLite sources up front (lines 76-106,
skipping a PostgreSQL or SQLite source that *does* configure a migrations assembly, because creating
its tables without an `__EFMigrationsHistory` row leaves every migration both pending and
un-appliable), then, under a raised five-minute command timeout so a replica queued behind another's
EF migrations lock does not die at the 30-second default (`InitializationCommandTimeout`, line 37,
applied at line 112 through `RaiseCommandTimeouts`, line 257), applies the configured strategy per
migrated source (lines 118-128), where `None` is the production guard that throws with a per-source
breakdown of pending migrations (line 124 into `ThrowIfPendingMigrationsAsync` at line 359; [ADR-030](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/030-startup-sole-migrator.html),
[ADR-006](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/006-database-per-service.html)), repeats the same
strategy per tenant that keeps its own copy of a source, each in a fresh scope with its tenant set
(line 130 into `InitializeTenantDatabasesAsync` at line 196,
[ADR-073](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/073-multi-tenancy-model.html)), and finishes by running
the enabled modules' seeders on the default scope only (line 137). Five smaller startup helpers round
out the host: [`OpenApiEndpointExtensions`](#openapiendpointextensions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/Endpoints/OpenApiEndpointExtensions.cs:24`) maps the
per-version OpenAPI document (`MapCommonOpenApi`, lines 34-38) and the optional Scalar reference UI
(`MapCommonScalarUi`, lines 52-56), both outside Production only,
[`ApiParameterDescriptorBackfillProvider`](#apiparameterdescriptorbackfillprovider)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/OpenApi/ApiParameterDescriptorBackfillProvider.cs:43`)
fills in the placeholder descriptor MVC leaves null on an unbound route token (lines 65-71) so a
URL-segment-versioned or `{tenant}`-templated route cannot turn document generation into a 500, running
last by ordering itself at `int.MinValue` (line 46) and registered exactly once through
`TryAddEnumerable` however many helpers a host calls
(`WebApplicationBuilderExtensions.cs:56` and `:103`, through the private helper at `:124-126`), the
paired [`StronglyTypedIdSchemaTransformer`](#stronglytypedidschematransformer)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/OpenApi/StronglyTypedIdSchemaTransformer.cs:24`) and
[`StronglyTypedIdParameterTransformer`](#stronglytypedidparametertransformer) (same file, line 74),
both registered by `AddCommonOpenApi` through a `ConfigureAll` that reaches every versioned document
(`WebApplicationBuilderExtensions.cs:115-119`) and both inert in a host that declares no wrappers: the
schema half clears the object schema the default generator produces for a one-property struct and
rewrites it as the wrapped primitive's type and format (`:38-41`, mapped at `:46` and `:51`) so the
document matches the bare-primitive wire shape, and the operation half does the same for route and
query parameters, which the API explorer describes as plain strings because they bind through a
`TypeConverter` (`:74-107`,
[ADR-115](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/115-strongly-typed-identifiers-opt-in.html)),
[`SignalRExtensions`](#signalrextensions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/SignalRExtensions.cs:12`) maps
[`NotificationHub`](group-10-notifications.md#notificationhub) at its configured path when push
notifications are enabled (lines 22-27), [`MiniProfilerExtensions`](#miniprofilerextensions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/MiniProfilerExtensions.cs:11`) registers
MiniProfiler with Entity Framework profiling when `ApplicationSettings.UseMiniProfiler` is set (lines
16-25), and [`AppAssociationEndpointExtensions`](#appassociationendpointextensions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/Endpoints/AppAssociationEndpointExtensions.cs:15`) with
[`AppAssociationOptions`](#appassociationoptions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/Endpoints/AppAssociationOptions.cs:9`) serve the
Android Digital Asset Links and Apple App Site Association documents (paths at lines 18 and 24, mapped
at lines 42 and 46) that let a mobile OS hand this host's https links to the installed native app
([ADR-043](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/043-mobile-deep-links-and-native-oauth-callback.html)).
[`ExternalAuthExtensions`](#externalauthextensions)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Authentication/ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:30`)
completes the OAuth half of authentication, staying entirely inert when no provider is configured
(lines 47-52), each of Google, GitHub, and Apple gated on its `OAuth:{Provider}:ClientId` being present
(lines 60-73) and throwing at startup when the matching client secret is missing (lines 98-100 and
110-112); Apple has no static secret, so its handler mints one (line 130) and startup throws instead on
a missing `TeamId`, `KeyId`, or `PrivateKeyPem` (lines 131-139). Apple also never puts the user's name
in its ID token: it posts it once, as the `user` form field of the first authorization's callback, and
never again for that Apple ID, so the Apple scheme hooks `OnCreatingTicket` to
[`AppleUserNameClaims`](#appleusernameclaims) (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:214-216`). That internal
static helper
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Authentication/AppleUserNameClaims.cs:18`) reads the
already buffered callback form (`AddFromCallbackFormAsync`, line 28, the field name held as
`UserFormField` at line 21) and copies `name.firstName` and `name.lastName` into
`ClaimTypes.GivenName` and `ClaimTypes.Surname` (`AddFromUserField`, line 47, the claims added at
lines 75-76), leaving any claim the identity already carries untouched (line 86) and adding nothing
for a missing or malformed field (lines 49-52 and 70-73), because the name is best effort and must
never fail the sign-in. Without it the account is created with the "User User" placeholders and the
real name cannot be recovered on a later sign-in (`AppleUserNameClaims.cs:7-16`). Taken together these are the [Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness], [Rubric §11, Security],
and [Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability] concerns that let the same controller code run identically
in a monolith and in a fleet of extracted services behind a YARP gateway.

**Where this group sits.** Everything above is the outermost ring. It depends downward on the CQRS
pipeline and query services (Groups 03 and 05), the auth and caching infrastructure (Groups 08 and
09), the persistence factories (Group 07), the integration-event contracts it consumes (Group 04), and
the module system that discovers controllers and drives health checks (Group 14 via
[`ModuleLoader`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#moduleloader)), and it rests on the Result and
domain primitives (Groups 01 and 02). Nothing inside the framework depends on it: the app hosts and
the gRPC transport group (Group 13) call into it. [`AssemblyReference`](#assemblyreference) and
[`ClassReference`](#classreference)
(`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/AssemblyReference.cs:8` and `:20`) are the scanning
anchors that let those callers point at this assembly without naming an incidental type. Read the
group as the framework's HTTP grammar: the reusable edge every downstream service inherits so its own
code stays modules and domain logic, never plumbing.

### AssemblyReference
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/AssemblyReference.cs:8` · Level 0 · class (static)

- **What it is**: a tiny static class that exposes the `MMCA.Common.API` assembly handle and its simple name, so convention-based scanners have a stable, refactor-safe anchor into this layer.
- **Depends on**: `System.Reflection` (BCL) only (`AssemblyReference.cs:1`).
- **Concept introduced (assembly-marker types for convention scanning).** Scrutor-based DI registration and the architecture fitness rules both need a stable "anchor" type to say *scan the assembly that contains this*. Rather than reaching for `typeof(SomeIncidentalClass).Assembly`, a dedicated `AssemblyReference` makes the intent explicit and survives type moves. `[Rubric §2, Design Patterns]` assesses whether recurring structural problems are solved with named, reusable patterns; here the same two-type marker shape repeats in every layer and module assembly, which is exactly this pattern applied uniformly. `[Rubric §33, Developer Experience]` assesses how easy the framework is to build on; centralizing one assembly handle per package means every scan call references one obvious token.
- **Walkthrough**: two `public static readonly` fields. `Assembly` (`AssemblyReference.cs:11`) is `typeof(AssemblyReference).Assembly`, resolved once at type initialization. `AssemblyName` (`AssemblyReference.cs:14`) is `Assembly.GetName().Name` with a `?? string.Empty` fallback for logging and diagnostics.
- **Why it's built this way**: a purpose-built anchor decouples scanning from any incidental type. The pattern is duplicated in every layer so each assembly is self-describing without a cross-layer reference back to a single "well-known" class.
- **Where it's used**: module handler/validator/mapper scanning (see [ModuleLoader](group-14-module-system-composition.md#moduleloader)) and the architecture tests' package-assembly pinning.

### ClassReference
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/AssemblyReference.cs:20` · Level 0 · class

- **What it is**: an empty, instantiable class in the `MMCA.Common.API` assembly, used where a generic constraint or a `typeof(...)` needs a concrete reference *type* from this layer rather than an `Assembly` instance.
- **Depends on**: nothing. See [AssemblyReference](#assemblyreference) for the full concept; `ClassReference` is its type-shaped sibling.
- **Concept**: covered under [AssemblyReference](#assemblyreference). Where `AssemblyReference.Assembly` answers "which assembly", `ClassReference` answers "give me a `class` token from that assembly" for APIs whose generic parameter is constrained to a reference type (`where T : class`).
- **Walkthrough**: the whole type is `public class ClassReference;` (`AssemblyReference.cs:20`), a body-less class declaration whose doc comment (`:17-19`) states the intent. It carries no members; its identity is the entire point.
- **Why it's built this way**: some registration and scanning helpers take a marker *type parameter* instead of an `Assembly`; a dedicated empty class keeps those call sites from accidentally binding to a real domain or controller type.
- **Where it's used**: generic registration helpers that need a per-assembly type anchor from the API layer.

### AppleUserNameClaims
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Authentication` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Authentication/AppleUserNameClaims.cs:18` · Level 0 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: an internal static helper that recovers the user's given name and surname during a Sign in with Apple callback and adds them to the external identity as `ClaimTypes.GivenName` and `ClaimTypes.Surname` claims.
- **Depends on**: the ASP.NET Core OAuth ticket context (`OAuthCreatingTicketContext`), `System.Security.Claims`, and `System.Text.Json`. No first-party dependencies. It is plugged into the Apple handler by [ExternalAuthExtensions](#externalauthextensions) (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:216`).
- **Concept introduced (best-effort claim enrichment from a one-time payload).** Apple's ID token never carries the user's name. Apple posts it once, as JSON in the callback form's `user` field, and only on the first authorization; every later sign-in sends an empty field (`AppleUserNameClaims.cs:21`, `:45`). `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses how external identity input is trusted; here the payload is untrusted form data, parsed defensively, and a failure never blocks sign-in.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `UserFormField = "user"` (`:21`) names the form field.
  - `AddFromCallbackFormAsync(OAuthCreatingTicketContext)` (`:28`) is the `OnCreatingTicket` handler. It returns early when there is no identity or the request is not form-encoded (`:30-33`), then reads the already buffered form (`:35`) and delegates to `AddFromUserField` with the scheme's `ClaimsIssuer` (`:36`).
  - `AddFromUserField(ClaimsIdentity, string?, string?)` (`:47`) returns on a blank value, parses the JSON, requires an object with a `name` object, and reads `firstName` and `lastName` (`:54-68`). A `JsonException` is swallowed (`:70-73`): the name is best effort and the controller's placeholders apply instead.
  - `AddIfMissing` (`:84`) adds a claim only when the value is non-blank and the identity does not already carry that claim type, so an existing claim is never overwritten (`:84-91`); `ReadString` (`:79`) trims string values and ignores non-strings.
- **Why it's built this way**: the sign-in must never fail because a cosmetic field is missing or malformed, and the one-time nature of the payload means the only chance to capture it is inside the callback, which is why it hooks `OnCreatingTicket` rather than the controller.
- **Where it's used**: only by the Apple branch of [ExternalAuthExtensions](#externalauthextensions) (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:216`); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Authentication/AppleUserNameClaimsTests.cs`.

### OidcClaimNormalizer
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Authentication` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Authentication/OidcClaimNormalizer.cs:21` · Level 0 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: an internal static helper that rebuilds the principal a generic OpenID Connect handler produces from a validated ID token into the claim shape the OAuth controller already reads, so a new OIDC provider needs no app change (`OidcClaimNormalizer.cs:5-8`).
- **Depends on**: `System.Security.Claims` only (`OidcClaimNormalizer.cs:1`). It is called from the `OnTokenValidated` event that [ExternalAuthExtensions](#externalauthextensions) wires for every OIDC scheme (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:140-144`).
- **Concept introduced (provider-neutral identity normalization).** Each OIDC issuer names its claims a little differently, and the handler is configured to keep the raw names (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:128-130`). This class maps them onto one fixed shape and drops everything else. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses how external identity input is trusted; two choices here are trust decisions. The provider key is never the email, which a user or an administrator can change (`OidcClaimNormalizer.cs:10-13`), and `email_verified` passes through only when the operator configured the provider with `TrustEmail` and the token asserts it, so an app that links an external login to an existing account by email does so only for an issuer the operator vouched for (`:14-16`).
- **Walkthrough**
  - `EmailVerifiedClaimType = "email_verified"` (`OidcClaimNormalizer.cs:24`) names the one non-standard claim the app reads.
  - `Normalize(ClaimsPrincipal, string providerName, bool trustEmail)` (`:31`) starts an empty claim list (`:33`), adds `ClaimTypes.NameIdentifier` from `ProviderKey` when one can be built (`:35-39`), then copies email, given name, surname, and name, each from the raw OIDC claim name with the mapped `ClaimTypes` name as a fallback (`:41-44`). It adds `email_verified=true` only when `trustEmail` is set and the token's value parses as `true` (`:46-51`). The result is a new principal whose authentication type is the provider name (`:53`), which the controller passes on as the login provider (`:17-18`).
  - `ProviderKey` (`:56`) returns `{tid}|{oid}` when both claims are present (`:58-63`); for Entra ID `sub` is pairwise per application while `oid` is the stable user id inside the tenant (`:10-12`). Otherwise it uses `sub` (or `NameIdentifier`) and returns `null` when there is none (`:65-69`), qualifies it with the `iss` claim, falling back to the subject claim's `Issuer` when `iss` was not kept and the issuer is not the default (`:71-78`), and returns `{iss}|{sub}`, or `null` when no issuer is known (`:80`).
  - `First` (`:83-86`) returns the first non-blank value across a list of claim types, and `AddIfPresent` (`:88-94`) adds a claim, issued by the provider name, only when the value is non-blank.
- **Why it's built this way**: keying on `{iss}|{sub}` rather than the bare `sub` keeps two issuers from colliding on the same subject value, and building a fresh principal (rather than adding to the handler's) guarantees the controller sees only claims this class vouches for.
- **Where it's used**: only by the OIDC branch of [ExternalAuthExtensions](#externalauthextensions) (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:142`). See [ADR-036](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/036-external-oauth-login.html) for the external-login decision.

### OidcProviderSettings
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Authentication` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Authentication/OidcProviderSettings.cs:20` · Level 0 · record (internal sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the validated configuration of one generic OIDC provider, a positional record of `Name`, `Authority`, `ClientId`, `ClientSecret`, `Scopes`, `DisplayName`, `Domains`, and `TrustEmail` (`OidcProviderSettings.cs:20-28`), plus the static reader that builds the list from configuration.
- **Depends on**: `IConfiguration`, `FrozenSet`, and a source-generated `Regex` (`OidcProviderSettings.cs:148-149`). No first-party dependencies.
- **Concept introduced (fail-fast, configuration-driven provider registration).** Any OIDC provider joins by adding an `OAuth:Oidc:<Name>` section (`SectionPath`, `OidcProviderSettings.cs:31`); no code changes. `[Rubric §11, Security]` and `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` both apply: a misconfigured section throws an `InvalidOperationException` that names the offending key at startup rather than producing a half-working scheme, and a section without `ClientId` stays inert, the same gate the built-in providers use (`:43-52`).
- **Walkthrough**
  - `ReadAll(IConfiguration)` (`:53`) is null-tolerant about the section (`:55-56`) and reads each child whose `ClientId` is set (`:60`). It tracks which provider claimed each email domain and throws when a second provider lists a domain already claimed, because each domain may map to one provider only (`:58`, `:63-69`).
  - `Read` (`:77`) takes the section key as the name. It rejects a reserved name (`:82-86`): `google`, `github`, and `apple` (the built-in providers) and `complete`, `exchange`, and `discover` (the controller's own literal routes, which would shadow the challenge), compared ignoring case (`:35-41`). It also rejects a name that fails `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`, because the name becomes a URL path segment (`:88-92`, `:148`).
  - `Authority` and `ClientSecret` are required through `Required` (`:94-95`, `:131-137`). Scopes default to `openid profile email` when none are configured, and `openid` is inserted first when a configured list omits it (`:33`, `:97-109`). Domains are normalized, blanks dropped, and de-duplicated ignoring case (`:111-115`). `DisplayName` falls back to the name (`:117`, `:126`), and `TrustEmail` is `true` only when it parses as such (`:118`).
  - `NormalizeDomain` (`:145-146`) trims and drops a leading `@`; case is kept because every comparison is ordinal-ignore-case (`:139-142`).
- **Why it's built this way**: validating eagerly at registration means an operator learns about a typo in a deployment, not at a user's first sign-in, and the reserved-name list keeps a configured provider from silently hijacking a built-in route.
- **Where it's used**: read by [ExternalAuthExtensions](#externalauthextensions) (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:58`) and held by [OidcProviderRegistry](#oidcproviderregistry). See [ADR-036](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/036-external-oauth-login.html).

### OidcProviderRegistry
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Authentication` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Authentication/OidcProviderRegistry.cs:10` · Level 1 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: an internal lookup over the configured [OidcProviderSettings](#oidcprovidersettings) list (primary-constructor parameter, `OidcProviderRegistry.cs:10`), registered as a singleton only when at least one OIDC provider is configured (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:91-100`).
- **Depends on**: [OidcProviderSettings](#oidcprovidersettings) for the provider list and its `NormalizeDomain` rule (`OidcProviderRegistry.cs:34`).
- **Concept introduced (home-realm discovery by email domain).** A sign-in page can ask for an email address and route the user to the provider whose `Domains` claim that address's domain, rather than showing one button per provider. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` is relevant: both lookups return `null` instead of throwing, so the controller decides the response.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `Find(string? name)` (`OidcProviderRegistry.cs:15-18`) returns the provider whose name equals the route segment, ignoring case, or `null` for a blank name or no match.
  - `FindByEmail(string? email)` (`:26`) takes the domain after the last `@` and returns `null` for a missing `@`, an empty local part, or an empty domain (`:28-32`). It then normalizes the domain and returns the provider whose `Domains` contains it, exact match ignoring case (`:34-37`); a subdomain does not match its parent (`:20-23`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the registry is absent in a host with no OIDC configuration, so the controller resolves it optionally and the generic routes stay inert.
- **Where it's used**: [OAuthControllerBase](#oauthcontrollerbase) resolves it from the request services (`OAuthControllerBase.cs:188`) and calls `Find` for the generic challenge route (`:160`) and `FindByEmail` for discovery (`:181`).

### ExternalAuthExtensions
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Authentication` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Authentication/ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:30` · Level 2 · class (static)

- **What it is**: a static class that registers the external OAuth provider schemes (Google, GitHub, Apple), one OpenID Connect scheme per configured `OAuth:Oidc:<Name>` section, and the short-lived cookie scheme that carries the external principal from the provider callback to the app's OAuth controller. It is the counterpart wiring that `AddCommonAuthentication` (JWT-only) deliberately leaves out.
- **Depends on**: the ASP.NET Core authentication, HTTP, configuration, DI, and OpenID Connect protocol surfaces (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:1-5`), plus the provider handlers reached through `AddGoogle`, `AddGitHub`, `AddApple`, and `AddOpenIdConnect` (`:163`, `:175`, `:190`, `:113`). First-party, it partners with the app's OAuth controller subclassing [OAuthControllerBase](#oauthcontrollerbase), whose `ExtractClaims` consumes the schemes registered here (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:11-12`, `:184`), reads [OidcProviderSettings](#oidcprovidersettings), registers [OidcProviderRegistry](#oidcproviderregistry), hands each OIDC principal to [OidcClaimNormalizer](#oidcclaimnormalizer), and names the same signal the UI's [ConfigurationOAuthUISettings](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#configurationoauthuisettings) uses to decide which buttons to render (`:16-18`).
- **Concept introduced (config-gated, additive auth registration).** The single `public const string ExternalLoginScheme = "ExternalLogin"` (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:36`) is shared with the OAuth controller so the sign-in scheme name can never drift between the two halves of the flow. The important property of this class is that it is *additive and inert*: `AddCommonAuthentication` registers only the JWT bearer scheme, so without this call the OAuth controller would `Challenge` and read schemes that are not in the authentication pipeline and the flow would fail at runtime (`:14-16`), yet a host with no `OAuth` configuration section keeps the JWT-only default completely untouched. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses how authentication, secrets, and trust boundaries are handled; each provider is gated on its `ClientId` being present, a missing companion secret throws at startup (`:166-168`, `:178-180`, `:199-207`), and a misconfigured OIDC section fails startup naming its key (`:45-47`, `:57-58`) rather than silently half-configuring an auth scheme. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` is relevant because the opt-in posture mirrors `AddPermissions` ([ADR-020](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/020-permission-based-authorization.html)), and the class doc says so explicitly (`:17-20`).
- **Walkthrough**
  - The public surface is one C# `extension(IServiceCollection services)` block (`ExternalAuthExtensions.cs:38`), matching the DI convention described in the [primer](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to).
  - `AddExternalAuthProviders(IConfiguration configuration)` (`:50`) reads the `OAuth` section (`:52`), pulls `Google:ClientId`, `GitHub:ClientId`, and `Apple:ClientId` (`:53-55`), and reads every OIDC provider eagerly through `OidcProviderSettings.ReadAll` (`:57-58`). When all three client ids are empty and no OIDC provider is configured it returns the collection untouched (`:62-68`), which is what keeps environments without OAuth secrets (most tests, local dev) exactly as `AddCommonAuthentication` left them (`:60-61`).
  - When at least one is configured it calls `services.AddAuthentication()` with no argument (`:72`). The comment above it is the load-bearing detail (`:70-71`): the parameterless overload does not reset the default scheme set by `AddCommonAuthentication`, it just yields a builder to append schemes onto.
  - It then adds the `ExternalLogin` cookie (`:74`) and each configured built-in provider in turn, each behind its own non-empty check (`:76-89`). When OIDC providers exist it adds one scheme per provider and registers a singleton [OidcProviderRegistry](#oidcproviderregistry), which the controller's generic challenge route and home-realm discovery read (`:91-100`), before returning `services` for chaining (`:102`).
  - `AddOidcProvider` (`:112-145`) registers an OpenID Connect scheme named after the provider, using its display name (`:113`): authorization code flow with PKCE (`:118-119`), signing into `ExternalLoginScheme` (`:120`) with callback `/auth/callback/<Name>` (`:121`). The sign-out callback and remote sign-out paths are per provider, because the handler defaults are shared and two OIDC providers would otherwise claim the same path (`:123-126`). `MapInboundClaims = false`, `SaveTokens = false`, and `GetClaimsFromUserInfoEndpoint = false` (`:130-132`) keep raw claim names (`sub`, `iss`, `tid`, `oid`, `email_verified`) for the event and keep the ID token out of the cookie, since nothing downstream reads it (`:128-129`). The scope list is replaced by the provider's (`:134-138`), and `OnTokenValidated` swaps the principal for [OidcClaimNormalizer](#oidcclaimnormalizer)'s output (`:140-144`).
  - `AddExternalLoginCookie` (`:151-160`) configures the bridge cookie: name `mmca_external_login` (`:154`), `HttpOnly` (`:155`), `SameSite=Lax` (`:158`, sufficient because the OAuth round trip returns as a top-level GET navigation, which avoids the `Secure` plus cross-site cost that `SameSite=None` imposes, `:156-157`), and a 10-minute expiry (`:159`).
  - `AddGoogleProvider` (`:162-172`) and `AddGitHubProvider` (`:174-187`) each set the client id, require the matching `ClientSecret` or throw (`:166-168`, `:178-180`), set `SignInScheme = ExternalLoginScheme`, pin a fixed `CallbackPath` (`/auth/callback/google` at `:170`, `/auth/callback/github` at `:182`), and set `SaveTokens = true`. GitHub additionally requests the `user:email` scope (`:185`) because it does not return the email on the default scope and the controller's `ClaimTypes.Email` lookup would otherwise fail (`:183-184`).
  - `AddAppleProvider` (`:189-217`) is the one that differs structurally among the built-in providers. Its `ClientId` is the Apple *Services ID*, not the app bundle id (`:192-194`), and Apple has no static client secret at all: the handler mints a short-lived ES256 JWT from the developer's private key, so `GenerateClientSecret = true` (`:198`) and `TeamId` (`:199`), `KeyId` (`:202`), and `PrivateKeyPem` (`:205`) are each required-or-throw. The PEM is handed over as a callback returning a `ReadOnlyMemory<char>` (`:208`). Scheme, callback path (`/auth/callback/apple`, `:211`), and `SaveTokens` match the other two. It also wires `options.Events.OnCreatingTicket = AppleUserNameClaims.AddFromCallbackFormAsync` (`:216`), because the handler reads only the ID token, which never carries the name; Apple posts the name once, in the callback's `user` form field, on the first authorization (`:214-215`). See [AppleUserNameClaims](#appleusernameclaims).
- **Why it's built this way**: the cookie is intentionally short-lived and single-purpose. It exists only to bridge the provider callback to the controller's `CompleteAsync`, which signs it out the moment the local JWT pair is minted (`:148-149`). Splitting the OAuth scheme registration from `AddCommonAuthentication` keeps the JWT-only default free of provider secrets, and gating each provider independently means one provider's configuration never disturbs another's. Every provider, built-in or OIDC, signs into the same cookie, so the controller's completion path is shared. See [ADR-036](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/036-external-oauth-login.html) for the external-login decision.
- **Where it's used**: called from the host composition of a service that exposes social login; pairs with the app's `OAuthController` (subclass of [OAuthControllerBase](#oauthcontrollerbase)). Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Authentication/ExternalAuthExtensionsTests.cs` and `ExternalAuthOidcTests.cs`.

### ModuleControllerFeatureProvider
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/ModuleControllerFeatureProvider.cs:28` · Level 2 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: an `IApplicationFeatureProvider<ControllerFeature>` that removes controllers belonging to *disabled* modules from MVC's controller discovery, so a module turned off via configuration exposes no routes.
- **Depends on**: [ModulesSettings](group-14-module-system-composition.md#modulessettings) (the config-bound enabled/disabled map, `ModuleControllerFeatureProvider.cs:4`) and the MVC application-parts surface (`IApplicationFeatureProvider<ControllerFeature>`, `ControllerFeature`, `ApplicationPart`, `:2-3`), plus `System.Reflection` for the `TypeInfo` it inspects (`:1`).
- **Concept introduced (module-aware controller discovery).** MVC discovers controllers by scanning referenced assemblies. When a host references a module's `API` assembly transitively but an operator has disabled that module (`Modules:{Name}:Enabled=false`), MVC would still map its controllers, and every request to them would 500 because the module's DI services were never registered (`ModuleControllerFeatureProvider.cs:19-25`). `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` assesses whether modules can be composed and decomposed cleanly; this provider is one boundary that lets a module be switched off without deleting code or breaking the host, complementing the disabled-module stub registrations in the module system. `[Rubric §29, Resilience, Reliability & Business Continuity]` is relevant: the enable/disable decision is enforced once at the edge rather than checked inside each controller.
- **Walkthrough**: the primary-constructor parameter is `ModulesSettings modulesSettings` (`ModuleControllerFeatureProvider.cs:28-29`). `PopulateFeature` (`:33`) first snapshots the disabled module names once (`:36-39`) so it does not re-scan the settings dictionary per controller, returns early if none are disabled (`:41-44`), then materializes the matches and removes every controller matched by `IsDisabledModuleController` (`:46-53`). The private static matcher (`:60`) reads the controller's assembly simple name and namespace, each with a `?? string.Empty` fallback (`:64-65`), and, for each disabled module, tests whether either contains the token `.{ModuleName}.` (`:72`). Wrapping the module name in dots is deliberate: it matches `.Catalog.` inside `MMCA.Store.Catalog.API` or its `.Controllers` namespace while avoiding false positives from substrings like "Catalogue" (`:69-71`). The comparison is `OrdinalIgnoreCase` on both the assembly name and the namespace (`:74-75`), and the loop falls through to `false` when nothing matches (`:81`).
- **Why it's built this way**: matching on the dotted token handles both the `MMCA.{Repo}.{Module}.API` convention and the legacy `{Prefix}.Modules.{Module}.*` convention without maintaining a registry of controller types (`:14-17`). Removing controllers at feature-provider time is earlier than routing, so a disabled module is invisible rather than returning a runtime error.
- **Where it's used**: registered by [DependencyInjection](#dependencyinjection)'s `AddAPI(modulesSettings)` via `ConfigureApplicationPartManager` (`DependencyInjection.cs:72-73`), but only when a non-null `ModulesSettings` is supplied (`DependencyInjection.cs:70`); pairs with the module system's disabled-stub registrations so cross-module interfaces stay resolvable.

### DependencyInjection
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/DependencyInjection.cs:26` · Level 11 · class (static)

- **What it is**: the primary DI entry point for the `MMCA.Common.API` layer. Using a C# `extension(IServiceCollection services)` block (`DependencyInjection.cs:28`) it adds six methods to `IServiceCollection`: `AddAPI`, `AddErrorLocalization`, `AddErrorResources<TResource>`, `AddCommonExceptionHandlers`, `AddServerAuthSessionCookie`, and `AddModuleHealthChecks`.
- **Depends on**: a broad slice of the API layer plus feature management and localization (`DependencyInjection.cs:1-19`). Notable first-party types wired here: the Shared-layer [EnumerationJsonConverterFactory](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#enumerationjsonconverterfactory) (aliased, `:19`, because the Shared and API namespaces both surface converters), and `StronglyTypedIdJsonConverterFactory` (ADR-115 strongly typed identifiers, serializing wrapper types as their bare primitive), [UnhandledResultFailureFilter](#unhandledresultfailurefilter), [OversizeFormResourceFilter](#oversizeformresourcefilter) (from `MMCA.Common.API.Uploads`, `:15`), [IdempotencyFilter](#idempotencyfilter) and [IdempotencySettings](#idempotencysettings), [OwnerOrAdminFilter](group-08-auth.md#owneroradminfilter) and its `OwnerOrAdminFilterOptions`, [ModuleControllerFeatureProvider](#modulecontrollerfeatureprovider), [CurrentUserTargetingContextAccessor](#currentusertargetingcontextaccessor) and [DisabledFeatureHandler](#disabledfeaturehandler), [IErrorLocalizer](#ierrorlocalizer)/[ErrorLocalizer](#errorlocalizer), [ErrorResources](#errorresources)/[ErrorResourceSource](#errorresourcesource), the exception handlers ([OperationCanceledExceptionHandler](#operationcanceledexceptionhandler), [DomainExceptionHandler](#domainexceptionhandler), [DbUpdateExceptionHandler](#dbupdateexceptionhandler), [ValidationExceptionHandler](#validationexceptionhandler), [GlobalExceptionHandler](#globalexceptionhandler)), [CookieTokenReader](group-08-auth.md#cookietokenreader) and [ICookieSessionRefresher](group-08-auth.md#icookiesessionrefresher)/[CookieSessionRefresher](group-08-auth.md#cookiesessionrefresher), the session-cookie options and store ([SessionCookieSettings](group-08-auth.md#sessioncookiesettings), [ISessionCookieStore](group-08-auth.md#isessioncookiestore)/[SessionCookieStore](group-08-auth.md#sessioncookiestore), from `MMCA.Common.API.SessionCookies`, `:14`), and [ModuleLoader](group-14-module-system-composition.md#moduleloader)/[ModulesSettings](group-14-module-system-composition.md#modulessettings). Externals: `Microsoft.FeatureManagement` (including its `IDisabledFeaturesHandler` contract), `Microsoft.Extensions.Localization`, ASP.NET Core MVC/ProblemDetails/HealthChecks.
- **Concept introduced (layered DI wiring at the API edge).** `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]` assesses whether each layer registers only its own concerns; this class wires controllers, JSON/XML formatters, filters, feature management, exception handlers, and health checks, all API-layer edges, and reaches down to Application only for `ModulesSettings`/`ModuleLoader` (`:16-17`). `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` and `[Rubric §17, DevOps]` both apply through `AddModuleHealthChecks` (`:220`), which projects module state into `/health` checks tagged `module` so `/health?tag=module` reports each module's status (`:212-213`). `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` is relevant: every parameter is optional and defaulted so a host wires only what it needs.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `AddAPI(ModulesSettings? modulesSettings = null, IConfiguration? configuration = null)` (`DependencyInjection.cs:45`) registers controllers with `ReturnHttpNotAcceptable = false`, the [UnhandledResultFailureFilter](#unhandledresultfailurefilter) global filter, and the [OversizeFormResourceFilter](#oversizeformresourcefilter) at its own `FilterOrder`, so an oversize form body answers 413 rather than model binding's 400 (`:47-54`). It adds two JSON converters (`:55-67`) and the XML DataContract formatters (`:68`); `Currency` is not among them because it carries its own converter in Shared (`:30-31`). The first converter is the load-bearing one for enumerations: concrete enumerations do not inherit the base class's `[JsonConverter]` attribute because `System.Text.Json` resolves it with `inherit: false`, so the factory is registered once here and every `Enumeration<T>` serializes by `Name` across the whole API surface (`:57-60`). The second, `StronglyTypedIdJsonConverterFactory` (`:62-66`), does the same job for ADR-115 strongly typed identifiers, so wrapper types serialize as the bare primitive they wrap without the attribute being repeated on every wrapper a consumer declares; in a host that declares no wrappers the factory converts nothing. It then conditionally registers [ModuleControllerFeatureProvider](#modulecontrollerfeatureprovider) when `modulesSettings` is non-null (`:70-74`), conditionally binds [IdempotencySettings](#idempotencysettings) from the config section with data-annotation validation on start (`:76-82`), and registers the scoped [IdempotencyFilter](#idempotencyfilter) and [OwnerOrAdminFilter](group-08-auth.md#owneroradminfilter) (`:87-88`). Scoped is driven by `OwnerOrAdminFilter`, which takes the scoped [ICurrentUserService](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice); `IdempotencyFilter` takes only a logger and reads the singleton [ICacheService](group-09-caching.md#icacheservice) from the request's services (`:84-86`). Right after that it binds `OwnerOrAdminFilterOptions` with data-annotation validation but deliberately **not** `ValidateOnStart` (`:90-95`): `BypassRole` is required with no default, the framework knows no role names, so validating at startup would fail every host that never applies the filter; validating on first resolve puts the message in front of the host that actually uses it. Feature management comes next (`:97-110`) and is worth reading closely: `AddHttpContextAccessor()` is called first because it is `TryAdd`-based and therefore safe to repeat, then `AddFeatureManagement().WithTargeting<CurrentUserTargetingContextAccessor>()` registers the feature manager plus the built-in Percentage/TimeWindow/Targeting filters **and** supplies the targeting audience from the current request's principal, which is what makes a percentage rollout sticky per user instead of random per request; the accessor is a singleton, which is exactly why it reads `IHttpContextAccessor` rather than the scoped `ICurrentUserService`. Finally the singleton [DisabledFeatureHandler](#disabledfeaturehandler) is bound to `IDisabledFeaturesHandler` (`:110`) and `AddErrorLocalization()` is called (`:113`).
  - `AddErrorLocalization()` (`:125`) registers ASP.NET localization (`:127`), the singleton [IErrorLocalizer](#ierrorlocalizer) via `TryAddSingleton` so a host can substitute its own (`:128`), and the framework's own [ErrorResources](#errorresources) source (`:129-133`). The framework source is registered inline as an [ErrorResourceSource](#errorresourcesource) with `IsFramework = true` (`:132`) rather than through `AddErrorResources<ErrorResources>()`, so the localizer can consult it last: a module's entry for a code wins over the framework's (`:121-122`). `AddErrorResources<TResource>()` (`:144`) adds a module's resource anchor as another [ErrorResourceSource](#errorresourcesource) built from an `IStringLocalizerFactory` (`:146-147`). This is the [ADR-027](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/027-multi-locale-i18n.html) edge error-localization extension point, keyed by `Error.Code`, and modules add their translations additively (`:119-122`).
  - `AddCommonExceptionHandlers()` (`:157`) registers ProblemDetails (adding a `requestId` extension from `TraceIdentifier`, `:159-161`) then five `IExceptionHandler`s in specificity order (`:162-166`): `OperationCanceled`, `DomainException`, `DbUpdate`, `Validation`, and [GlobalExceptionHandler](#globalexceptionhandler) as the catch-all. ASP.NET Core invokes them in registration order and stops at the first that handles the exception, hence most-specific first and the 500 fallback last (`:153-154`).
  - `AddServerAuthSessionCookie(string apiBaseAddress)` (`:182`) wires the SSR-prerender auth path: it guards the address (`:184`), then adds `HttpContextAccessor`, memory cache, the scoped [CookieTokenReader](group-08-auth.md#cookietokenreader) (`:186-188`), a named `HttpClient` pointed at the internal API base address (`:190-191`), and the [CookieSessionRefresher](group-08-auth.md#cookiesessionrefresher) as a **singleton** (`:194`). The singleton is load-bearing: its in-flight map must be shared across requests for single-flight refresh to work (`:193`). It then registers the [SessionCookieSettings](group-08-auth.md#sessioncookiesettings) options with no configuration binding, so SameSite and the claims-only browser token keep their default behavior unless the same-origin API proxy in `MMCA.Common.UI.Web` tightens them on opt-in ([ADR-131](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/131-same-origin-api-proxy.html)), and the singleton [ISessionCookieStore](group-08-auth.md#isessioncookiestore)/[SessionCookieStore](group-08-auth.md#sessioncookiestore) via `TryAddSingleton` (`:196-199`). Last it registers `TimeProvider.System` via `TryAddSingleton` (`:201-204`): the refresher judges access-token expiry against the injected clock, and a Blazor Web host that calls only this method has no `AddServices` registration to supply one, while `TryAdd` never displaces a clock the host registered itself. The doc comment is explicit that the address is the internal endpoint, not the browser-facing one (`:178-179`).
  - `AddModuleHealthChecks(ModuleLoader moduleLoader)` (`:220`) adds one health check per module, `Healthy` for each enabled module (`:224-230`) and `Degraded` for each disabled one (`:232-239`), named `module-{Name}` and tagged `module`. It must run after [ModuleLoader](group-14-module-system-composition.md#moduleloader)'s `DiscoverAndRegister` (`:216-217`).
- **Why it's built this way**: bundling the API-edge concerns behind small, defaulted extension methods lets each host opt into exactly the surface it needs (a JWT-only test host skips `AddServerAuthSessionCookie`; a monolith with no disabled modules passes a null `modulesSettings`; a host with no idempotency configuration passes a null `configuration` and keeps the defaults). The exception-handler ordering, the oversize-form filter's 413, the enumeration and strongly-typed-id converter factories, the `OwnerOrAdminFilterOptions` validate-on-resolve (not on-start) choice, the targeting accessor's singleton lifetime, the refresher's singleton lifetime, and the `TryAdd` fallback clock for hosts that call only `AddServerAuthSessionCookie` are the non-obvious, correctness-critical choices, and each carries an inline comment. Error localization is registered automatically by `AddAPI` so modules only add their own resources additively ([ADR-027](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/027-multi-locale-i18n.html)).
- **Where it's used**: called from every service host's composition (`Program.cs` of the ADC/Store/Helpdesk API hosts and the integration-test hosts) to wire the shared API layer; `AddApplicationDecorators()` still runs last in the overall sequence (see `MMCA.Common/CLAUDE.md` DI ordering note).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the relative ordering of `AddAPI` against `AddInfrastructure`/`AddApplication` in a given host is not fixed by this file; only `AddApplicationDecorators()` last is load-bearing.

### ApiVersionReportingResultFilter
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Caching` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/ApiVersionReportingResultFilter.cs:21` · Level 0 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: an MVC `IResultFilter` that writes the api-version report headers (`api-supported-versions`, `api-deprecated-versions`) before a result writes its body, so the output cache stores them with the entry (`ApiVersionReportingResultFilter.cs:8-12`).
- **Depends on**: `Asp.Versioning` (`IReportApiVersions`, `ApiVersionMetadata`), `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Http`, and `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.Filters` (`ApiVersionReportingResultFilter.cs:1-4`). No first-party dependencies.
- **Concept introduced (report before the body so the cache snapshot sees it).** The versioning library reports from a `Response.OnStarting` callback, but the output cache snapshots response headers when the body starts, before those callbacks run, so a cache hit replayed the stored headers without the version report (`:14-17`). `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether the contract a client observes is stable; this filter keeps the version headers present on cached and uncached responses alike. Versioning itself is the decision recorded in [ADR-046](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/046-http-api-versioning.html), and the cache that makes the ordering matter is the one configured by [OutputCacheOptionsExtensions](#outputcacheoptionsextensions).
- **Walkthrough**: `OnResultExecuting` (`:28-44`) returns early if the response has already started (`:31-34`). Otherwise it reads `ApiVersionMetadata` from the matched endpoint (`:36`) and the host's `IReportApiVersions` from `RequestServices` (`:37`); if either is missing it returns (`:38-41`), else it calls `reporter.Report(response, metadata.Map(reporter.Mapping))` (`:43`). `OnResultExecuted` is an empty body (`:47-49`).
- **Why it's built this way**: reporting ahead of the body puts the headers in the cache snapshot, and the library's own `OnStarting` callback then finds them present and adds nothing (`:17-18`). Resolving `IReportApiVersions` from DI, rather than writing the headers directly, means a host with `ReportApiVersions` off still reports nothing (`:18-19`).
- **Where it's used**: added to the global MVC filter collection by `WebApplicationBuilderExtensions` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/WebApplicationBuilderExtensions.cs:60`), right after `AddApiParameterDescriptorBackfill()` in the versioning setup. See [WebApplicationBuilderExtensions](#webapplicationbuilderextensions).

### ConcurrencyConflictMetrics
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Concurrency` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Concurrency/ConcurrencyConflictMetrics.cs:24` · Level 0 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: the OpenTelemetry counter for optimistic-concurrency conflicts: how often a save lost to a moved row version and the request was answered with a conflict, tagged by the entity type that conflicted (`ConcurrencyConflictMetrics.cs:6-11`).
- **Depends on**: `System.Diagnostics.Metrics` (`Meter`, `Counter<long>`) and `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore` for `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` (`ConcurrencyConflictMetrics.cs:1-2`). No first-party dependencies. Its two recording sites are [SupportsIfMatchAttribute](#supportsifmatchattribute) and [DbUpdateExceptionHandler](#dbupdateexceptionhandler).
- **Concept**: the same "meter a mechanism an operator cannot otherwise see" idea introduced at [IdempotencyMetrics](#idempotencymetrics) and applied to cache eviction at [OutputCacheMetrics](#outputcachemetrics), here applied to optimistic concurrency ([ADR-035](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/035-optimistic-concurrency.html)). A conflict is a correct outcome for the request that lost, so nothing fails visibly; the counter is what shows contention. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` assesses whether such a signal is exported. The type doc settles two points worth carrying (`:12-23`).
  - **Counted once, at the two places a conflict becomes a response** (`:14-17`): [SupportsIfMatchAttribute](#supportsifmatchattribute) answers 412 under an explicit `If-Match` and marks the exception handled, and [DbUpdateExceptionHandler](#dbupdateexceptionhandler) answers 409 for every other endpoint. A given exception reaches exactly one of the two, so each conflict is counted once.
  - **No alert ships on it** (`:20-21`): a sustained rate on one entity type points at contended rows (two editors, or a background writer racing user edits), which is a design question rather than an incident.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `MeterName = "MMCA.Common.Persistence"` (`:27`) and a single static `Meter` built from it (`:35`). `UnknownEntityType = "unknown"` (`:30`) is the tag value when the exception names no entry; the tag name itself, `entity_type`, is private (`:33`).
  - `ConflictCounter` (`:37-40`) is a `Counter<long>` named `persistence.concurrency.conflict` with unit `{conflict}`.
  - `RecordConflict(DbUpdateConcurrencyException)` (`:46-47`) adds one, tagged with `EntityTypeName(exception)`.
  - `EntityTypeName` (`:55-56`) returns `UnknownEntityType` when `exception.Entries` is empty, otherwise the CLR name of the first entry's type read from the EF model (`Entries[0].Metadata.ClrType.Name`), so a lazy-loading proxy reports its entity rather than the generated subclass (`:49-52`). The tag is bounded by the host's entity vocabulary.
- **Why it's built this way**: `internal static` keeps the instrument out of the package's public surface while the filter and the handler in the same assembly record it. As with [OutputCacheMetrics](#outputcachemetrics), the meter name is duplicated as a literal in `MMCA.Common.Aspire` because that package has no reference to `MMCA.Common.API`, and a host exports the counter by registering the meter, which the Aspire service defaults do (`:8-10`).
- **Where it's used**: twice. [SupportsIfMatchAttribute](#supportsifmatchattribute) records before replacing the exception with a 412 (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Concurrency/SupportsIfMatchAttribute.cs:186`), and [DbUpdateExceptionHandler](#dbupdateexceptionhandler) records only for the `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` kind, leaving unique-key and foreign-key failures uncounted so they do not hide the contention signal (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Middleware/DbUpdateExceptionHandler.cs:34-39`).

### EvictOutputCacheAttribute
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Caching` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/EvictOutputCacheAttribute.cs:36` · Level 0 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: an attribute that is also an MVC result filter. Placed on a write action or on a whole controller, it evicts the output-cache tags it names after the write succeeds, instead of a hand-written eviction call before every `return` (`EvictOutputCacheAttribute.cs:11-14`).
- **Depends on**: `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.Filters.IAsyncResultFilter`, `IStatusCodeActionResult` for the result's own status, `IOutputCacheStore`, and `ILogger` with a `NullLogger` fallback (`EvictOutputCacheAttribute.cs:1-7`). The eviction itself is the best-effort `TryEvictTagsAsync` verb of [OutputCacheEvictionExtensions](#outputcacheevictionextensions) (`:114`).
- **Concept introduced (declarative, outcome-aware cache eviction).** A public read cached by [PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy](#publicendpointoutputcachepolicy) stays stale until a write drops its tag, so every successful mutation has to evict. Doing that by hand means one eviction call per success branch, and a branch that forgets it serves stale data for a full TTL. The class doc settles three decisions (`:15-34`).
  - **When it evicts** (`:16-22`): as a result filter it runs after the action filters, so it sees the final outcome, after [SupportsIfMatchAttribute](#supportsifmatchattribute) rewrote a conflict to 412 and after an idempotent replay was produced. A 2xx result evicts every tag BEFORE the response body is written; a non-2xx result, a GET or HEAD, and an unhandled action exception (which skips result filters) evict nothing.
  - **Best effort** (`:23-28`): the write has already committed, so a cache-store fault is a Warning, never a failed request, and a host with no `IOutputCacheStore` registered evicts nothing. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses whether a post-commit follow-up degrades gracefully; this is the contract [ADR-096](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/096-best-effort-side-effects.html) states.
  - **Once per request** (`:29-33`): tags already evicted are recorded in `HttpContext.Items`, so a class-level and a method-level instance naming the same tag evict it once. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses whether a hot path avoids redundant round trips; the dedupe keeps a stacked declaration from doubling store calls. The caching model this serves is [ADR-040](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/040-authenticated-output-caching-for-public-reads.html).
- **Walkthrough**
  - `[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Class | AttributeTargets.Method, AllowMultiple = true, Inherited = true)]` (`:35`). `EvictedTagsItemKey` (`:42`) is the `HttpContext.Items` slot holding the per-request set of evicted tags.
  - The constructor takes `params string[] tags` (`:52`), throws `ArgumentNullException` on null (`:54`), `ArgumentException` when the array is empty (`:56-57`) or a tag is null, empty or whitespace (`:59-60`), and copies the array (`:62`). `Tags` (`:66`) exposes them read-only, in order.
  - `OnResultExecutionAsync` (`:75`) null-guards both arguments, evicts when `ShouldEvict` says so (`:80-83`), then always awaits `next()` (`:85`), so the eviction lands before the body is written.
  - `ShouldEvict` (`:89`) returns false for GET and HEAD (`:92-93`), then reads the status from the result's own `IStatusCodeActionResult.StatusCode`, falling back to the response status (`:95`), and accepts 200 through 299 (`:96`).
  - `EvictAsync` (`:100`) filters the tags through the request's evicted set, keeping only those `HashSet.Add` accepts (`:102-103`), returns when none are pending, returns when no `IOutputCacheStore` resolves (`:107-109`), resolves `ILogger<EvictOutputCacheAttribute>` or the null logger (`:111-112`), and calls `store.TryEvictTagsAsync(logger, pending)` (`:114`).
  - `EvictedTagsOf` (`:118`) returns the set already stored under `EvictedTagsItemKey` (`:120-121`) or creates an ordinal `HashSet<string>` and stores it (`:123-125`).
- **Why it's built this way**: a result filter, not an action filter, is the one place that sees the status after every action-level rewrite and still runs before the body, which is what lets a 412 from the conditional-write filter evict nothing. Resolving the store and the logger from `RequestServices` instead of taking them by constructor keeps the attribute usable as a plain attribute with no DI registration.
- **Where it's used**: the generic write base relies on it: [CrudEntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType, TCreateRequest, TUpdateRequest>](#crudentitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype-tcreaterequest-tupdaterequest) documents that a derived controller declares its tags with this attribute (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/CrudEntityControllerBase.cs:42`). ADC's Conference controllers carry it at class level, for example `ActivitiesController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Activities/ActivitiesController.cs:42`) and `SessionAssetsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/SessionAssets/SessionAssetsController.cs:62`), and at method level with several tags, for example `EventsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Events/EventsController.cs:195`) and the lifecycle transitions of `EventLifecycleController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Events/EventLifecycleController.cs:61`). Framework coverage is `EvictOutputCacheAttributeTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Caching/EvictOutputCacheAttributeTests.cs`), and consumers assert their declarations through `EvictOutputCacheAssertions` in the testing package (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing/Support/EvictOutputCacheAssertions.cs`).

### OutputCacheMetrics
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Caching` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/OutputCacheMetrics.cs:16` · Level 0 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: the OpenTelemetry instrument set for the output-cache eviction consumer. Today it is exactly one counter, `cache.eviction.failed`, plus the helper that records it.
- **Depends on**: `System.Diagnostics.Metrics` (`Meter`, `Counter<long>`, `OutputCacheMetrics.cs:1`) only. Its single recording site is [OutputCacheEvictionHandler](#outputcacheevictionhandler).
- **Concept**: the same "meter a degradable mechanism" idea introduced at [IdempotencyMetrics](#idempotencymetrics), applied to a different failure. Cross-service cache eviction ([OutputCacheEvictionHandler](#outputcacheevictionhandler)) is deliberately best-effort: an eviction that throws is logged and swallowed rather than rethrown, because rethrowing would redeliver the message and eventually dead-letter it. The consequence is that a failing eviction is invisible from the outside, since every request still succeeds and the stale entry simply serves until its TTL. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` assesses whether an operator can see that: the counter doc names itself the alert target for cross-service cache coherence, because a non-zero rate means this host is serving output-cached responses it was told to drop (`OutputCacheMetrics.cs:23-27`). It also records the cardinality argument, which is the discipline that keeps a tagged counter affordable: the `cache_tag` tag is bounded by the host's own tag vocabulary, a small fixed set declared in its output-cache policies.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `MeterName = "MMCA.Common.OutputCache"` (`OutputCacheMetrics.cs:19`) and a single static `Meter` built from it (`:21`). The type doc carries a warning worth reading once and remembering (`:10-14`): never create a second `Meter` with this name, because a duplicate instance publishes a parallel set of instruments under the same meter name and a listener enabling one of them silently misses measurements recorded on the other.
  - `EvictionFailed` (`:29-32`) is a `Counter<long>` named `cache.eviction.failed` with unit `{tag}`.
  - `RecordEvictionFailure(string cacheTag)` (`:36-37`) adds one with a `cache_tag` tag, so the instrument name and the tag name are spelled in exactly one place.
- **Why it's built this way**: `internal static` keeps the instruments out of the package's public surface while the handler in the same assembly records them. As with [IdempotencyMetrics](#idempotencymetrics), the meter name is duplicated as a literal in `MMCA.Common.Aspire` because that package has no reference to `MMCA.Common.API` (`:8-9`), and a host exports the instruments by registering the meter, which the Aspire service defaults (`ConfigureOpenTelemetry`) already do (`:6-8`).
- **Where it's used**: exactly once, in [OutputCacheEvictionHandler](#outputcacheevictionhandler)'s catch block (`OutputCacheEvictionHandler.cs:61`), paired with a Warning log naming the same tag.

### PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Caching` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy.cs:47` · Level 2 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: a custom ASP.NET Core `IOutputCachePolicy` for public, user-independent GET/HEAD endpoints that must stay cacheable even when the request carries an `Authorization` header. It replaces the built-in default policy, which refuses to serve or store a cached response for any authenticated request.
- **Depends on**: `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OutputCaching` (`IOutputCachePolicy`, `OutputCacheContext`) and `Microsoft.Extensions.Primitives` (`StringValues`) (`PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy.cs:1-8`), plus three first-party types resolved or called per request: [ITenantContext](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#itenantcontext) for the tenant-vary key, and [IPermissionRegistry](group-08-auth.md#ipermissionregistry) with [PermissionAuthorizationHandler](group-08-auth.md#permissionauthorizationhandler) for the permission bypass. It is registered by [OutputCacheOptionsExtensions](#outputcacheoptionsextensions).
- **Concept introduced (auth-header-tolerant output caching).** The framework UI attaches a Bearer token to *every* outgoing API request, including reads of `[AllowAnonymous]` endpoints whose payload is identical for every caller.
