Under the default policy those reads bypass the output cache for any signed-in user and land on the database each time (`PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy.cs:16-21`). `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses whether hot read paths avoid redundant work; this policy is a direct performance lever, letting public reads share one cached entry across authenticated and anonymous callers. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses trust boundaries; the class docs are explicit that a cached response is served verbatim to every subsequent caller, so it must be applied only to identity-independent payloads, and the `bypassRoles` mechanism exists precisely so a privileged role that receives an elevated payload (for example administrators seeing unpublished rows) is never served or stored from the shared cache, and `bypassPermissions` does the same when the elevated payload is gated by a permission rather than a fixed role, so any caller holding it by claim, by role or by stored grant skips the cache too (`:29-39`). A second SECURITY remark covers multi-tenancy (`:40-45`): the tenant-vary rule below is a backstop, not a license, an endpoint whose payload depends on the caller (rather than only on the tenant) still must not use this policy. This is the edge tier of the two-tier caching model ([ADR-026](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/026-caching-strategy.html)); the authenticated-read decision itself is [ADR-040](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/040-authenticated-output-caching-for-public-reads.html).
- **Walkthrough**: a `TenantVaryByKey = "t"` constant (`:53`) mirrors the `t:{tenantId}` prefix the caching CQRS decorators use, and four instance fields hold the rest of the config, `_expiration`, `_bypassRoles`, `_bypassPermissions`, `_tags` (`:55-58`). Three constructors chain to the last: the `params string[] tags` overload passes empty bypass-roles and bypass-permissions arrays (`:63-64`), the `(expiration, bypassRoles, tags)` overload passes an empty bypass-permissions array (`:73-74`), and the full `(expiration, bypassRoles, bypassPermissions, tags)` constructor (`:92`) guards its inputs (`ThrowIfLessThanOrEqual(expiration, TimeSpan.Zero)` and null checks on all three arrays, `:94-97`). All three interface methods are explicitly implemented, so they are reachable only through `IOutputCachePolicy`. `CacheRequestAsync` (`:106`) computes `attemptOutputCaching` as "is a GET/HEAD request" AND "is not a bypassed caller" (`:112-113`), enables output caching, sets `AllowCacheLookup`/`AllowCacheStorage` to that flag, deliberately turns request coalescing off with `AllowLocking = false` (`:122`), sets the expiration (`:123`), and (matching the built-in default) varies the cache key by every query-string parameter via `CacheVaryByRules.QueryKeys = "*"` (`:127`). Locking is off because with it on, concurrent misses for one key wait on the first request's execution, which runs under that request's `RequestAborted` token: one client disconnecting would cancel the shared query and every waiter would answer 500 with a `TaskCanceledException`; the cost is that simultaneous misses each run the query once (`:118-121`). It then resolves the tenant from `context.HttpContext.RequestServices` and, when one is present, stamps it into `CacheVaryByRules.VaryByValues[TenantVaryByKey]` (`:133-137`), an unresolved tenant, single-tenant host or background call, adds nothing; then copies the eviction tags in (`:139-140`). `ServeFromCacheAsync` (`:146`) is a no-op returning `ValueTask.CompletedTask`. `ServeResponseAsync` (`:150`) refuses to store any response that set a cookie or returned a non-200 status (`:156-160`), the same guard the built-in default applies. Two private helpers close it out. `IsCacheableRequest` (`:165-166`) accepts GET or HEAD. `IsBypassedCaller(HttpContext)` (`:172`) first returns true when `Array.Exists(_bypassRoles, user.HasRole)` (`:175`), returns false when no bypass permissions are configured (`:180-183`), and otherwise resolves the optional `IPermissionRegistry` from `RequestServices` (`:187`) and asks `PermissionAuthorizationHandler.Holds(user, registry, permission)` for each bypass permission (`:188`), the same evaluation the permission handler grants the elevated payload by (`:185-186`). `HasRole` is deliberate, not `ClaimsPrincipal.IsInRole`: `IsInRole` only matches the identity's own role claim type, so against a provider that emits bare `role`/`roles` claims the permission handler would grant the elevated payload while this bypass check missed it, and that response would be stored under the shared public key (`:168-171`).
- **Why it's built this way**: it mirrors the built-in default policy minus exactly one behavior, the authenticated-request bail-out, so its caching, query-key variance, and cookie/status guards stay identical to what developers already expect. Bypass roles and bypass permissions get the default behavior back (no lookup, no storage), which keeps elevated payloads out of the shared cache without disabling caching for everyone. Evaluating a bypass permission through the permission handler's own `Holds`, rather than a second claim check, means a role granted the permission later (including a stored grant) is bypassed too, so the cache can never answer a caller the endpoint would have given the elevated payload. Stamping the resolved tenant into the vary key means a multi-tenant host's shared cache entry per path+query never crosses tenants, since the same path and query mean different rows per tenant (`:129-132`). A raw `IOutputCachePolicy` implementation inherits none of the default policy's behavior, so every guard is re-implemented here.
- **Where it's used**: registered as a named policy by [OutputCacheOptionsExtensions](#outputcacheoptionsextensions) and referenced from controller actions via `[OutputCache(PolicyName = ...)]` or, for the base reads of an entity controller, through [PublicCachedReadsAttribute](#publiccachedreadsattribute). Store's Catalog service registers three such policies with no bypass roles, the products one at 60 seconds because an effective price moves on the clock (`MMCA.Store/Source/Services/MMCA.Store.Catalog.Service/Program.cs:59-69`); ADC's Conference service registers eleven, most of them with an admin bypass-roles array (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:174-216`), including two short 60-second policies, `NowNextPolicy` (`:202`) and `BookmarkCountsPolicy` (`:216`). Its sessions policy is the one that constructs the policy directly with a bypass permission, `ConferencePermissions.SessionAssetsManage`, because `SessionAssetsController`'s read answers a holder of that permission with materials the public cannot see (`:181-192`). Framework coverage is `PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicyTests` and `PublicEndpointOutputCachePermissionBypassTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Caching/`). The tags passed here are the same strings [OutputCacheEvictionHandler](#outputcacheevictionhandler) evicts by.

### OutputCacheOptionsExtensions
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Caching` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/OutputCacheOptionsExtensions.cs:6` · Level 2 · class (static)

- **What it is**: registration helpers that add named output-cache policies backed by [PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy](#publicendpointoutputcachepolicy) onto ASP.NET Core's `OutputCacheOptions`.
- **Depends on**: `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OutputCaching` (`OutputCacheOptions`, `OutputCacheOptionsExtensions.cs:1`) and [PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy](#publicendpointoutputcachepolicy).
- **Concept**: this is a thin fluent facade over `OutputCacheOptions.AddPolicy`, using a C# `extension(OutputCacheOptions options)` block (`OutputCacheOptionsExtensions.cs:8`) so the policy registration reads as a first-class option on the options object. See the [DI registration extension(T) convention](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to). `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` is relevant: the helper gives callers a self-documenting, named entry point instead of hand-constructing the policy at each call site.
- **Walkthrough**: two overloads of `AddPublicEndpointPolicy`. The first (`OutputCacheOptionsExtensions.cs:20-21`) takes `name`, `expiration`, and `params string[] tags` and registers `new PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy(expiration, tags)`. The second (`:34-35`) adds a `string[] bypassRoles` parameter before the `params string[] tags` and forwards to the three-argument policy constructor, for endpoints whose payload is identical for every caller except one privileged role. Both are expression-bodied and return `void`, mutating the options in place.
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping the policy construction behind a named helper means the "apply only to `[AllowAnonymous]`, identity-independent endpoints" guidance travels with the API surface (see the doc comments at `:10-16` and `:23-29`) instead of being re-derived at each registration.
- **Where it's used**: called during host composition where the app configures `AddOutputCache(...)`; the registered `name` is then referenced by `[OutputCache(PolicyName = ...)]` on controller actions. Store's Catalog service uses the two-argument form (`MMCA.Store/Source/Services/MMCA.Store.Catalog.Service/Program.cs:59-69`); ADC's Conference service mostly uses the bypass-roles form (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:276-307`).

### OutputCacheEvictionHandler
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Caching` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/OutputCacheEvictionHandler.cs:32` · Level 4 · class (sealed, partial)

- **What it is**: the integration-event handler that drops named tags from **this host's** output cache when another service says the underlying data changed. It is the consumer half of cross-service output-cache coherence.
- **Depends on**: [IIntegrationEventHandler<in TIntegrationEvent>](group-04-events-outbox.md#iintegrationeventhandlerin-tintegrationevent) closed over [OutputCacheEvictionRequested](group-04-events-outbox.md#outputcacheevictionrequested) (`OutputCacheEvictionHandler.cs:35`), ASP.NET Core's `IOutputCacheStore`, `ILogger`, and [OutputCacheMetrics](#outputcachemetrics) for the failure counter (`:1-4`).
- **Concept introduced (cache coherence across a service boundary, as a best-effort hint).** [PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy](#publicendpointoutputcachepolicy) makes a public read cheap by caching it for minutes at a time. The cost of that is staleness when the data changes, and in a multi-service topology the change usually happens **somewhere else**: a write in one service invalidates a cached read in another. The framework's answer is an ordinary integration event, so nothing about this handler is broker-specific. `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]` assesses whether cross-service reactions travel as events rather than as direct calls; the class doc is explicit that no broker type appears here, so the handler is equally reachable from the in-process dispatcher (`:8-16`). Two failure decisions carry the design.
  - **Per-tag best effort** (`:18-24`): each tag is evicted independently and a failure is logged and counted rather than rethrown. Rethrowing would hand the message back to the retry policy and redeliver it, re-evicting every tag that already succeeded, and would eventually dead-letter a message whose only consequence is a cache entry that expires on its own TTL anyway. The doc gives the one-line principle worth remembering: **a failed eviction is a staleness window, not a lost fact.** `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses whether degraded dependencies degrade the system gracefully instead of failing it; this is the contract [ADR-096](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/096-best-effort-side-effects.html) generalizes.
  - **Cancellation is not swallowed** (`:25-28`): an `OperationCanceledException` from host shutdown propagates, so the broker receive pipeline sees the shutdown rather than a completed message. This is the same `when (ex is not OperationCanceledException)` discipline used across the framework's cache-touching code.
- **Walkthrough**: the primary constructor takes `IOutputCacheStore outputCacheStore` and `ILogger<OutputCacheEvictionHandler> logger` (`:32-34`). `HandleAsync(OutputCacheEvictionRequested integrationEvent, CancellationToken)` (`:38`) null-guards the event (`:42`), then loops the tags (`:44`), skipping blank entries (`:46-49`). Each tag goes through `outputCacheStore.EvictByTagAsync(tag, cancellationToken)` inside its own `try` (`:51-53`) followed by a Debug log (`:54`). The catch (`:56-63`) is deliberately broad, filtered only by `when (ex is not OperationCanceledException)`; its comment notes that CA1031 stays at error but does not flag a filtered catch, and S2221 is a suggestion, because an eviction store that throws must not turn a coherence hint into a dead-lettered message (`:58-60`). It records the failure on [OutputCacheMetrics](#outputcachemetrics) (`:61`) and logs a Warning naming the tag and stating that responses carrying it stay cached until their own TTL expires (`:62`, template at `:72-75`).
- **Why it's built this way**: `partial` plus two `[LoggerMessage]` declarations (`:67-75`) gives source-generated, allocation-free logging at Debug for the success path and Warning for the failure path. Keeping the handler free of any broker type is what lets the same class serve both the in-process dispatcher and the broker consumer, which is the extraction property the module system is built around ([ADR-007](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/007-grpc-extraction.html), [ADR-008](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/008-service-extraction-topology.html)).
- **Where it's used**: registered by [OutputCacheEvictionExtensions](#outputcacheevictionextensions)`.AddOutputCacheEvictionHandler()`. The broker half is `RegisterOutputCacheEvictionConsumer()` inside the host's `AddBrokerMessaging` configuration, which wires the generic `IntegrationEventConsumer<OutputCacheEvictionRequested>` onto this handler; ADC's Conference service calls both (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:280` and `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/ConferenceBrokerConsumers.cs:31`); on the far side, ADC's Engagement bookmark path names this handler as the receiver of the `OutputCacheEvictionRequested` it causes to be published (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application/UserSessionBookmarks/DomainEventHandlers/UserSessionBookmarkCacheEvictionHandler.cs`). The tags it evicts are the ones passed to [OutputCacheOptionsExtensions](#outputcacheoptionsextensions)`.AddPublicEndpointPolicy`. Framework coverage is `OutputCacheEvictionHandlerTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Caching/OutputCacheEvictionHandlerTests.cs`).

### SupportsIfMatchAttribute
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Concurrency` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Concurrency/SupportsIfMatchAttribute.cs:58` · Level 4 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: an attribute that is also an action filter and an application-model convention. It makes a write action conditional: the optimistic-concurrency token comes from the HTTP `If-Match` header, the header is **mandatory**, and a conflict outcome is answered with `412 Precondition Failed` rather than `409 Conflict`. As a convention it also documents those answers in the API description.
- **Depends on**: [ConcurrencyETag](group-08-auth.md#concurrencyetag) (the header name, the wildcard, and the tag decoder), [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error), [ErrorHttpMapping](#errorhttpmapping) and [IErrorLocalizer](#ierrorlocalizer) for the problem body, [ConcurrencyConflictMetrics](#concurrencyconflictmetrics) for the conflict counter, plus `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.Filters.IAsyncActionFilter`, `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.ApplicationModels` (`IActionModelConvention`, `IControllerModelConvention`), `IApiResponseMetadataProvider`, `ProblemDetailsFactory`, and `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.DbUpdateConcurrencyException` (`SupportsIfMatchAttribute.cs:1-12`).
- **Concept introduced (conditional writes, and why two conflict statuses are not one).** The type doc (`:16-56`) is the whole design, in five decisions.
  - **The token never touches the request model** (`:22-27`, `:60-65`): the filter decodes the header and puts the bytes in `HttpContext.Items` under `TokenItemKey`, and the action reads them from there. A request body therefore never carries a concurrency token, and no request record has to loosen its immutability to receive one.
  - **The header is required** (`:28-34`). A guarded mutation without a usable token would be a last-write-wins write, so a request that states no precondition is refused with `428 Precondition Required` and the action never runs. A malformed tag is a `400 Bad Request`, because the server cannot tell what the caller meant. `*` counts as no precondition, since it names no particular version.
  - **Why 412 and not 409** (`:33-41`): RFC 9110 reserves 412 for a precondition the client stated in a conditional request header, which is precisely what `If-Match` is, while 409 stays the answer for a conflict the client did not condition on. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether status codes carry their standard meaning rather than a local one. The doc also names the consequence worth carrying, because the rewrite keys on the **outcome** rather than on an error code: an endpoint whose 409 means something else (a duplicate key, say) reports that conflict as 412, with the original problem details, error codes included, intact.
  - **The attribute IS the filter** (`:44-48`): unlike [IdempotentAttribute](#idempotentattribute) this needs no scoped service, so it implements `IAsyncActionFilter` directly and requires no DI registration by the host.
  - **The attribute owns its response metadata** (`:49-55`): as an action or controller model convention it appends Problem Details `412` and `428` response types after the action's own, each only when neither the action nor its controller already declares that status. `Apply(ActionModel)` (`:96`) collects the declared statuses from every `IApiResponseMetadataProvider` filter on the action and its controller (`:100-104`) and adds a `ProducesResponseTypeAttribute(typeof(ProblemDetails), status)` for each missing one of `PreconditionStatusCodes` (`:69-70`, `:106-109`); `Apply(ControllerModel)` (`:117`) runs that for every action of a controller carrying the attribute at class level (`:121-124`). `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` again: the published contract states the answers the filter can produce without each action repeating them.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Method | AttributeTargets.Class)]` (`:48`); `TokenItemKey` (`:57`) is the `HttpContext.Items` slot. `RequiredToken(HttpContext)` (`:68-76`) is the action-side accessor: it returns the decoded `byte[]`, or throws `InvalidOperationException` naming the attribute when the slot is empty. That is deliberate (`:64-67`): reaching the action with no token means the attribute is missing, which is a wiring mistake rather than a client error.
  - `OnActionExecutionAsync` (`:79`) guards its arguments (`:81-82`), calls `TryApplyIfMatch` (`:84`), and returns **without running the action** when it is false (`:84-88`), because the 428 or the 400 is already on the context. Otherwise it awaits `next()` (`:90`) and passes the outcome to the rewrite (`:92`).
  - `TryApplyIfMatch` (`:104`) reads the header (`:106-107`), then refuses with `PreconditionRequiredResult` when the value is blank or is the wildcard (`:109-114`), refuses with `MalformedIfMatchResult` when `ConcurrencyETag.TryParse` cannot decode it (`:116-120`), and otherwise stores the bytes in `HttpContext.Items` and returns true (`:122-123`).
  - `RewriteConflictToPreconditionFailed` (`:130`) handles two shapes. A `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` is first recorded on [ConcurrencyConflictMetrics](#concurrencyconflictmetrics) (`:137`), then replaced with the 412 result and marked `ExceptionHandled = true` (`:132-141`). Marking it handled is what stops [DbUpdateExceptionHandler](#dbupdateexceptionhandler) from mapping it to a 409 like any other `DbUpdateException`, and it is also why the conflict is counted here: the global handler never sees it (`:134-136`). An `ObjectResult` already carrying 409 has both its own `StatusCode` and its `ProblemDetails.Status` moved to 412, leaving the body otherwise intact (`:145-152`); a bare `StatusCodeResult` 409 is swapped for a 412 (`:154-156`); anything else falls through untouched (`:158-159`).
  - The three responses are built by one private helper, `Problem` (`:206-222`), which asks the registered `ProblemDetailsFactory` for the body so the response carries the same diagnostic extensions (`traceId`) as the rest of the API, falls back to a hand-built `ProblemDetails` when no factory is registered, and attaches the framework's standard `errors` extension through [ErrorHttpMapping](#errorhttpmapping)`.BuildErrorsExtension` with the optional [IErrorLocalizer](#ierrorlocalizer) (`:218-219`). The three call sites differ only in status, title, detail and error: 428 `Concurrency.PreconditionRequired` (`:164-173`), 400 `Concurrency.MalformedIfMatch` (`:176-185`), and 412 `Concurrency.PreconditionFailed` (`:188-197`), each detail line showing a sample tag or telling the caller to re-read and retry.
- **Why it's built this way**: making the attribute its own filter keeps adoption to a single line on an action with no host wiring, which matters for a framework whose consumers upgrade in lockstep ([ADR-035](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/035-optimistic-concurrency.html)). Routing the token through `HttpContext.Items` instead of through the bound model is what removed reflection from the request path entirely: there is no per-type property lookup, and request records stay `init`-only as the immutability fitness rules require.
- **Where it's used**: applied across all three consumer apps on writes whose stale-view risk is real, each action reading the token with `SupportsIfMatchAttribute.RequiredToken(HttpContext)` a few lines below the attribute. In ADC: the event lifecycle transitions (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Events/EventLifecycleController.cs:59`, `:86`), the hand-written PUT actions of `EventsController` (`.../Controllers/Events/EventsController.cs:228`), `SessionsController` (`.../Controllers/Sessions/SessionsController.cs:310`), `SpeakersController` (`.../Controllers/Speakers/SpeakersController.cs:372`) and the other Conference controllers (activities, sponsors, categories, session assets, partners, questions), plus Engagement's `SessionQuestionsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.API/Controllers/SessionQuestionsController.cs:136`, `:160`, `:186`) and `LivePollsController` (`.../Controllers/LivePollsController.cs:86`, `:120`). In Store: `OrdersController` (`MMCA.Store/Source/Modules/Sales/MMCA.Store.Sales.API/Controllers/OrdersController.cs:299`, `:359`), `OrderFulfillmentController` (`.../OrderFulfillmentController.cs:55`, `:88`, `:134`), `GuestOrdersController` (`.../GuestOrdersController.cs:165`) and `InventoryItemsController` (`.../InventoryItemsController.cs:96`, `:129`) in Sales, the Catalog controllers (for example `MMCA.Store/Source/Modules/Catalog/MMCA.Store.Catalog.API/Controllers/ProductVariantsController.cs:100`), and Identity's `CustomersController` (`MMCA.Store/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.Store.Identity.API/Controllers/CustomersController.cs:178`, `:204`, `:230`). In Helpdesk: `TicketsController` (`MMCA.Helpdesk/Source/Modules/Tickets/MMCA.Helpdesk.Tickets.API/Controllers/TicketsController.cs:116`, `:144`, `:212`). ADC pins the convention with `ConditionalWriteConventionTests` fitness tests that assert the attribute is present on the endpoints that need it (`MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API.Tests/Conventions/ConditionalWriteConventionTests.cs:31`, and an Engagement twin at `MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.API.Tests/Controllers/ConditionalWriteConventionTests.cs:13`). The read half of the round trip is [EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype)`.SetConcurrencyETag` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/EntityControllerBase.cs:417-425`), which gives the client the tag to send back.

### OutputCacheEvictionExtensions
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Caching` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/OutputCacheEvictionExtensions.cs:27` · Level 5 · class (static, two extension blocks)

- **What it is**: two things that travel together. It is the one-method DI registration for [OutputCacheEvictionHandler](#outputcacheevictionhandler) (the container half of the cross-service eviction path), and it is the multi-tag eviction helper pair a mutating controller reaches for right after a write.
- **Depends on**: [OutputCacheEvictionHandler](#outputcacheevictionhandler), [IIntegrationEventHandler<in TIntegrationEvent>](group-04-events-outbox.md#iintegrationeventhandlerin-tintegrationevent), [OutputCacheEvictionRequested](group-04-events-outbox.md#outputcacheevictionrequested), [BestEffort](group-03-querying-specifications.md#besteffort), and `ServiceCollectionDescriptorExtensions.TryAddEnumerable` (`OutputCacheEvictionExtensions.cs:1-8`).
- **Concept introduced (two eviction verbs for two call sites).** ASP.NET Core's `IOutputCacheStore` evicts one tag per call, but a single write usually invalidates several (a speaker edit drops both `conference:speakers` and `conference`). Rather than a private per-controller helper wrapping a run of `EvictByTagAsync` calls (`:17-21`), the framework offers one call that names every tag, in two flavors that differ only in what they do when the cache store is unreachable.
  - `EvictTagsAsync` propagates the failure. A caller that considers a failed eviction part of its own success uses this one.
  - `TryEvictTagsAsync` is the best-effort sibling: a Redis outage or a transient network fault becomes one Warning plus one [BestEffort](group-03-querying-specifications.md#besteffort) metric increment instead of an exception (`:60-66`). The reasoning is worth internalizing: **the mutation this follows has already committed**, so surfacing the failure would turn a successful write into a client-visible error while leaving the write in place, and an entry that could not be evicted expires on its own TTL anyway. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` covers exactly this post-commit-follow-up contract ([ADR-096](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/096-best-effort-side-effects.html)).
  - A second detail in `TryEvictTagsAsync` is easy to miss and load-bearing: eviction runs under `CancellationToken.None`, not the request token (`:67-70`, `:90`). The write has committed, so a client that disconnected mid-response must not abandon the cleanup.
- **Concept (an idempotent registration for a handler that must not run twice).** Two lifetime decisions are documented on `AddOutputCacheEvictionHandler` and both matter. The handler is registered as a **singleton**, matching the lifetime the module scanner gives every other integration-event handler, so a host that wires it by hand and a module that wires it through the scan agree. And it goes in through **`TryAddEnumerable`** rather than `AddSingleton`, so calling it twice (a host plus a module that both want the behavior) registers one handler rather than evicting every tag twice (`:102-108`). `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses whether a shared concern can be opted into safely from more than one place; a plain `Add` here would double every eviction, harmless in effect but doubling the store round trips and the failure counter.
- **Walkthrough**
  - A class-level `[SuppressMessage]` for CA1708 (`:23-26`) records a known analyzer trap: with multiple `extension(T)` blocks in one static class, CA1708 flags the compiler-generated grouping members as case-colliding, and no user-visible identifier differs only by case.
  - `EvictOperationPrefix = "output-cache-evict:"` (`:34`) is the prefix of the best-effort operation name. The tag is appended so a failure is attributable to the cache it could not clear, which is also why the doc insists call-site tags stay low-cardinality literals: the name becomes a metric tag (`:29-33`).
  - `extension(IOutputCacheStore store)` (`:36`) holds the two verbs. `EvictTagsAsync(CancellationToken cancellationToken, params string[] tags)` (`:49`) null-guards `store` and `tags` (`:51-52`) and awaits `EvictByTagAsync` for each tag in the order given (`:54-57`). `TryEvictTagsAsync(ILogger logger, params string[] tags)` (`:78`) adds a `logger` guard (`:80-82`) and routes each eviction through `BestEffort.ExecuteAsync` with the prefixed operation name, the caller's logger, the eviction as the action, and `CancellationToken.None` (`:86-90`).
  - `extension(IServiceCollection services)` (`:95`) holds `AddOutputCacheEvictionHandler()` (`:111`). It null-guards `services` (`:113`), calls `services.TryAddEnumerable(ServiceDescriptor.Singleton<IIntegrationEventHandler<OutputCacheEvictionRequested>, OutputCacheEvictionHandler>())` (`:115-117`), and returns the collection for chaining (`:119`). The doc records the one prerequisite (`:100-101`): `AddOutputCache()` must have been called, since it supplies the singleton `IOutputCacheStore` the handler evicts through.
- **Why it's built this way**: the class doc states the pairing rule plainly (`:12-16`): this is the DI half, `RegisterOutputCacheEvictionConsumer()` on the framework's `IBrokerConsumerBuilder` is the broker half (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Messaging/Consumers/IntegrationEventConsumerExtensions.cs:28`, shorthand for `RegisterIntegrationEventConsumer<OutputCacheEvictionRequested>()` at `:32`), and a host that wants the behavior calls both. Splitting them is what keeps the handler broker-agnostic, since the DI registration has no messaging dependency at all. Housing the eviction verbs in the same class keeps the write-side helper and the read-side coherence path in one file.
- **Where it's used**: ADC's Conference service calls `services.AddOutputCacheEvictionHandler()` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:313`) alongside the broker registration (`:352`). No consumer controller calls either verb directly: the one caller is [EvictOutputCacheAttribute](#evictoutputcacheattribute), which runs the best-effort form after a 2xx write (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/EvictOutputCacheAttribute.cs:114`), so ADC's Conference controllers and Store's Catalog controllers name their tags declaratively instead, for example `SpeakerCategoryItemsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Speakers/SpeakerCategoryItemsController.cs:51`), `ProductsController` (`MMCA.Store/Source/Modules/Catalog/MMCA.Store.Catalog.API/Controllers/ProductsController.cs:46`) and `CategoriesController` (`MMCA.Store/Source/Modules/Catalog/MMCA.Store.Catalog.API/Controllers/CategoriesController.cs:33`). Framework coverage: `OutputCacheEvictTagsTests` pins both verbs, including the `CancellationToken.None` rule and the swallow-versus-throw split (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Caching/OutputCacheEvictTagsTests.cs:59-113`), and `OutputCacheEvictionHandlerTests` pins the double-registration behavior (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Caching/OutputCacheEvictionHandlerTests.cs:107-114`).

### PublicCachedReadsAttribute
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Caching` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Caching/PublicCachedReadsAttribute.cs:42` · Level 8 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: a class-level attribute that is also an MVC controller-model convention. On an entity controller it marks the inherited base reads (both `GetAllAsync` overloads, `GetAllForLookupAsync` and `GetByIdAsync`) as anonymous and output-cached under one named policy, and names the by-id route, so a controller no longer overrides a base read only to add attributes (`PublicCachedReadsAttribute.cs:8-13`).
- **Depends on**: `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authorization` (`AllowAnonymousAttribute`, `IAllowAnonymous`, `IAuthorizeData`), `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.ApplicationModels` (`IControllerModelConvention`, `ControllerModel`, `ActionModel`, `SelectorModel`) and `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OutputCaching.OutputCacheAttribute` (`PublicCachedReadsAttribute.cs:1-3`), plus [EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype), whose read actions it recognizes (`:4`, `:45-50`). The named policy it points at is normally a [PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy](#publicendpointoutputcachepolicy) registered through [OutputCacheOptionsExtensions](#outputcacheoptionsextensions) (`:36-38`).
- **Concept introduced (declaring a public cached read surface once, at the controller).** A public read cached under [PublicEndpointOutputCachePolicy](#publicendpointoutputcachepolicy) needs two markers on every read action, `[AllowAnonymous]` and `[OutputCache(PolicyName = ...)]`, and the base reads live in the framework's generic controller, so without this attribute a consumer overrides each inherited read just to decorate it. The class doc settles three decisions (`:14-39`).
  - **What it adds** (`:15-22`): on every selector of a base read it appends the anonymous marker and the output-cache marker, each only when the selector does not already carry one, so an override's own `[OutputCache]` wins whatever its policy. The by-id read gets a route name unless one is already declared. Nothing is added to the action filters, so the API description changes only by the operationId a new route name produces. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses whether hot read paths avoid redundant work; this makes the cached shape the one-line default for an entity's public reads, the authenticated-read caching model of [ADR-040](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/040-authenticated-output-caching-for-public-reads.html).
  - **What it leaves alone** (`:23-27`): the export, every write, the opt-in cursor and change-feed reads, and every action the application declares itself keep exactly the authorization and caching the controller gives them.
  - **What it refuses** (`:28-34`): an override of a base read is still a base read and is marked like an inherited one, so an override that declares its own `[Authorize]` or permission attribute without `[AllowAnonymous]` makes the convention throw when the application model is built, because the anonymous marker would silently replace the requirement. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether access-control changes are explicit; this turns an accidental widening of a gated read into a startup failure rather than a public endpoint. A read that needs a gate belongs on a controller without the attribute.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Class, AllowMultiple = false, Inherited = true)]` (`:41`). `ReadActionNames` (`:45-50`) holds the three base read names via `nameof` on the open `EntityControllerBase<,,>`; both `GetAllAsync` overloads share one name.
  - The constructor takes `policyName` (`:57`) and rejects null, empty or whitespace with `ArgumentException.ThrowIfNullOrWhiteSpace` (`:59`). `PolicyName` (`:64`) exposes it. `ByIdRouteName` (`:71`) is an optional `init` property: null derives `Get{EntityName}ById`, and empty or whitespace is rejected when the convention runs.
  - `Apply(ControllerModel)` (`:83`) null-guards the model, finds the closed `EntityControllerBase<,,>` in the controller's base chain or throws `InvalidOperationException` naming the controller (`:87-89`), throws when `ByIdRouteName` is set but blank (`:91-95`), and derives the route name from the first generic argument, the entity type (`:97`). For every action `IsBaseRead` accepts (`:99`) it runs `EnsureNoDeclaredAuthorization` (`:101`), notes whether the action is `GetByIdAsync` (`:103`), and marks each selector, passing the route name only for the by-id read (`:105-108`).
  - `MarkSelector` (`:118`) adds `AllowAnonymousAttribute` when no `IAllowAnonymous` is present (`:120-123`), adds `OutputCacheAttribute { PolicyName = PolicyName }` when no `OutputCacheAttribute` is present (`:125-128`), and sets the attribute route's name only when a name is wanted and the route model has none (`:130-133`).
  - `EnsureNoDeclaredAuthorization` (`:146`) throws when the action's own attributes carry an `IAuthorizeData` and no `IAllowAnonymous` (`:148`); authorization declared on the controller class is deliberately left alone, the usual shape of gated writes beside public reads (`:136-141`).
  - `FindEntityControllerBase` (`:156`) walks `BaseType` until it meets a generic type whose definition is `EntityControllerBase<,,>` (`:158-161`). `IsBaseRead` (`:171`) takes the method's `GetBaseDefinition().DeclaringType` (`:173`), so an override still counts as a base read, and requires that declaring type to be a closed `EntityControllerBase<,,>` and the method name to be one of `ReadActionNames` (`:175-177`).
- **Why it's built this way**: a controller-model convention runs once when the application model is built, so the markers become ordinary endpoint metadata with no per-request cost, and a misuse (a non-entity controller, a blank route name, a gated override) fails at startup rather than at the first request. Writing to `EndpointMetadata` rather than to the action filters keeps the published API description unchanged. Adding a marker only when absent lets an individual override opt out of the shared policy by declaring its own `[OutputCache]`. An unknown policy name is not checked here; it fails at request time exactly as a hand-written `[OutputCache(PolicyName = ...)]` does (`:36-38`).
- **Where it's used**: fourteen ADC Conference controllers carry it at class level, each beside a class-level [EvictOutputCacheAttribute](#evictoutputcacheattribute) for the write side, for example `SessionsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Sessions/SessionsController.cs:44`), `EventsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Events/EventsController.cs:48`), `SpeakerCategoryItemsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Speakers/SpeakerCategoryItemsController.cs:48`) and `SponsorsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Sponsors/SponsorsController.cs:39`). Framework coverage is `PublicCachedReadsAttributeTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Caching/PublicCachedReadsAttributeTests.cs`), and ADC asserts its declarations through `PublicCachedReadAssertions` (`MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API.Tests/Helpers/PublicCachedReadAssertions.cs`). The generic entity-controller decision it builds on is [ADR-099](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/099-generic-write-side-entity-commands.html).

### ServiceInfoResponse
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:51` · Level 0 · record (sealed, nested)

- **What it is**: the v1.0 (minimal) payload returned by the service-info discovery endpoint: just the service name and the API version.
- **Depends on**: nothing beyond the BCL; a nested positional `record` inside [ServiceInfoControllerBase](#serviceinfocontrollerbase).
- **Concept**: the deprecated shape in a versioned-contract pair. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether an API can evolve without breaking callers; this record is the "before" shape that v1.0 clients keep receiving unchanged while v2.0 clients get the superset ([ADR-046](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/046-http-api-versioning.html)).
- **Walkthrough**: `ServiceInfoResponse(string Service, string ApiVersion)` (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:51`), returned by `GetV1()` populated with the concrete service name and the literal `"1.0"` (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:42`).
- **Where it's used**: produced by [ServiceInfoControllerBase](#serviceinfocontrollerbase)`.GetV1()`; superseded by [ServiceInfoV2Response](#serviceinfov2response).

### ServiceInfoV2Response
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:54` · Level 0 · record (sealed, nested)

- **What it is**: the v2.0 (evolved) service-info payload, a strict superset of [ServiceInfoResponse](#serviceinforesponse) that additionally advertises the supported and deprecated version lists.
- **Depends on**: nothing beyond the BCL; a nested positional `record` inside [ServiceInfoControllerBase](#serviceinfocontrollerbase).
- **Concept**: the additive-evolution half of the versioned pair. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: adding fields (not renaming or removing them) is the backward-compatible way to grow a contract, so a v1.0 caller who never sees the new fields is unaffected.
- **Walkthrough**: `ServiceInfoV2Response(string Service, string ApiVersion, IReadOnlyList<string> SupportedVersions, IReadOnlyList<string> DeprecatedVersions)` (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:54-58`). The two extra members surface the `Supported`/`Deprecated` arrays the controller holds (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:32-33`), so the body itself documents the version landscape, the same facts the `api-supported-versions` / `api-deprecated-versions` headers carry.
- **Where it's used**: produced by [ServiceInfoControllerBase](#serviceinfocontrollerbase)`.GetV2()` (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:47-48`).

### DisabledFeatureHandler
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.FeatureManagement` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/FeatureManagement/DisabledFeatureHandler.cs:14` · Level 0 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the one-method handler that decides what a `[FeatureGate]`-protected controller action returns when its feature flag is off. Instead of ASP.NET Core's default (a bare 404 with no body), it emits an RFC 9457 Problem Details payload so a disabled feature reads the same as any other framework error.
- **Depends on**: `IDisabledFeaturesHandler` and `FeatureGateAttribute` from `Microsoft.FeatureManagement.Mvc` (NuGet); `ProblemDetails`, `ObjectResult`, and `StatusCodes` from ASP.NET Core. No first-party dependencies.
- **Concept introduced: feature gating at the HTTP edge.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether every response, success or refusal, follows one uniform contract; this handler makes the disabled-feature path match the [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase)`.HandleFailure` shape rather than leaking a framework default. `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven Design]` covers concerns applied uniformly across endpoints, and feature flags are exactly that. Note the split: this class gates *controller actions* decorated with `[FeatureGate]`, while [FeatureGateCommandDecorator<TCommand, TResult>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#featuregatecommanddecoratortcommand-tresult) gates *CQRS handlers* one layer deeper. The two surfaces cover the two entry points into a gated capability, which is precisely the dual-surface enforcement [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html) records.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleDisabledFeatures(features, context)` (`DisabledFeatureHandler.cs:17`) sets `context.Result` to an `ObjectResult` wrapping a `ProblemDetails` with `Status = 404` and a fixed title/detail ("Feature not available", `DisabledFeatureHandler.cs:19-24`), and also sets the outer `StatusCode = 404` (`DisabledFeatureHandler.cs:26`) so the response code and the body agree. It returns `Task.CompletedTask` (`DisabledFeatureHandler.cs:29`): the work is synchronous, there is nothing to await.
- **Why it's built this way**: the payload deliberately does not name the disabled feature. An anonymous caller learns only that the endpoint is unavailable, not which flag is off, so the flag set is not enumerable from outside. The `features` parameter is available but unused for that reason.
- **Where it's used**: registered as the app's `IDisabledFeaturesHandler` singleton inside `AddAPI` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/DependencyInjection.cs:110`, immediately after `services.AddFeatureManagement().WithTargeting<CurrentUserTargetingContextAccessor>()` on `DependencyInjection.cs:108-109`); invoked by `Microsoft.FeatureManagement.Mvc` whenever a `[FeatureGate]` action is hit with its flag disabled. In the framework itself that covers [DataExportControllerBase<TQuery>](#dataexportcontrollerbasetquery) while `PrivacyFeatures.DataExport` is off (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Privacy/DataExportControllerBase.cs:60`) and the three notification controllers gated on `NotificationFeatures.PushNotifications` (`Controllers/Notifications/DevicesController.cs:23`, `NotificationInboxController.cs:27`, `NotificationsController.cs:28`).

### ServiceInfoControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:30` · Level 1 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: an anonymous, read-only discovery controller that proves the API-versioning machinery works across more than one version. The same `/ServiceInfo` route is served by v1.0 (deprecated) and v2.0, selected via the `api-version` header.
- **Depends on**: `Asp.Versioning` (`MapToApiVersion`) and ASP.NET Core MVC (`ControllerBase`); returns [ServiceInfoResponse](#serviceinforesponse) and [ServiceInfoV2Response](#serviceinfov2response), both nested in this file.
- **Concept introduced: header-based API versioning as a first-class contract.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether an API can carry multiple versions concurrently and signal deprecation; this controller demonstrates the whole loop: two versions on one route, one marked deprecated, and `ReportApiVersions = true` (set in `AddCommonApiVersioning` on [WebApplicationBuilderExtensions](#webapplicationbuilderextensions), `WebApplicationBuilderExtensions.cs:47`, inside the extension member declared at `WebApplicationBuilderExtensions.cs:39`) so responses carry `api-supported-versions` / `api-deprecated-versions` headers (class doc, `ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:6-14`). [ADR-046](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/046-http-api-versioning.html) makes the point that a versioning claim which only ever ships `v1.0` is untestable; this endpoint is what makes it testable.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `Supported = ["1.0", "2.0"]` and `Deprecated = ["1.0"]` (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:32-33`) are the static version lists the v2 payload echoes.
  - `ServiceName` (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:36`) is an abstract property the sealed per-service subclass supplies, because class-level routing/versioning attributes are not reliably inherited (remarks, `ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:15-29`): the subclass carries `[ApiController]`, `[Route("[controller]")]`, `[AllowAnonymous]`, and the two `[ApiVersion]` attributes.
  - `GetV1()` (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:41`) is `[HttpGet]` plus `[MapToApiVersion("1.0")]` (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:39-40`) and returns the minimal [ServiceInfoResponse](#serviceinforesponse).
  - `GetV2()` (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:47`) is `[MapToApiVersion("2.0")]` (`ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:46`) and returns the superset [ServiceInfoV2Response](#serviceinfov2response) with the supported/deprecated lists.
- **Why it's built this way**: the type is abstract with an abstract `ServiceName` so each extracted service reuses the identical versioning surface while stamping its own identity, keeping the "build the monolith now, extract a service later" path uniform (`[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]`). The endpoint is anonymous and reached on the service host directly; gateways do not route it (class doc, `ServiceInfoControllerBase.cs:12-13`).
- **Where it's used**: subclassed by each service's sealed `ServiceInfoController`, for example ADC's Conference service (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/ServiceInfoController.cs:20`) and Store's Catalog service (`MMCA.Store/Source/Modules/Catalog/MMCA.Store.Catalog.API/Controllers/ServiceInfoController.cs:20`). Because the controller ships in the framework, the fitness contract that exercises it is shared too: [ServiceInfoVersioningContractTestsBase<TFixture>](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#serviceinfoversioningcontracttestsbasetfixture) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing/Conformance/ServiceInfoVersioningContractTestsBase.cs:20`), and a repo subclasses it supplying only its fixture.

### IEntityControllerBase<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/IEntityControllerBase.cs:14` · Level 2 · interface

- **What it is**: the contract every read-only entity controller implements, four GET-shaped methods for all-entities, paged, lookup, and by-id retrieval.
- **Depends on**: [IBaseDTO<TIdentifierType>](#ibasedtotidentifiertype) (constraint), [CollectionResult<T>](group-01-result-error-handling.md#collectionresultt), [PagedCollectionResult<T>](group-01-result-error-handling.md#pagedcollectionresultt), [BaseLookup<TIdentifierType>](#baselookuptidentifiertype), and [QueryFilterModelBinder](#queryfiltermodelbinder) for the filter parameter.
- **Concept introduced: the generic entity-controller contract.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses uniform endpoint conventions across every entity, and this interface is the guarantee that all read controllers expose the same four GET shapes. `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: it is deliberately the read-only slice, kept separate from the create/delete slice ([IAggregateRootEntityControllerBase<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType, TCreateRequest>](#iaggregaterootentitycontrollerbasetentitydto-tidentifiertype-tcreaterequest)) so a child-collection controller can implement reads without inheriting mutation endpoints (Interface Segregation).
- **Walkthrough**: the type constrains `TEntityDTO : IBaseDTO<TIdentifierType>` and `TIdentifierType : notnull` (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:17-18`). The members:
  - `GetAllAsync` unpaged (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:26`), with `fields` projection (`[FromQuery]`) and the two eager-load flags (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:27-30`).
  - the paged `GetAllAsync` overload (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:43`), adding `sortColumn`/`sortDirection`, `[Range(1, int.MaxValue)]`-guarded `pageNumber`/`pageSize` (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:49-50`), and a `Dictionary<string, (string Operator, string Value)>` of filters bound by [QueryFilterModelBinder](#queryfiltermodelbinder) (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:51`).
  - `GetAllForLookupAsync` for id/label dropdown data (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:58`).
  - `GetByIdAsync` (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:69`), whose `includeFKs` defaults to `true` for the single-entity case (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:71`) while the collection endpoints default it to `false` (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:28` and `IEntityControllerBase.cs:44`).
- **Why it's built this way**: expressing the surface as an interface lets architecture tests and OpenAPI tooling reason about the contract independently of the concrete generic base, and lets the two-level controller hierarchy layer capabilities without collapsing reads and writes into one type. Note what is deliberately **absent**: the CSV `ExportAsync` action added to the generic base is a base-class method only, and this interface still declares exactly four members (`IEntityControllerBase.cs:26`, `:43`, `:58`, `:69`). Adding a fifth would be a breaking change for any consumer implementing the interface explicitly instead of inheriting the base, and a default interface member would hide that break behind a runtime surprise ([ADR-078](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/078-csv-export-endpoint.html)).
- **Where it's used**: implemented by [EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) and extended by [IAggregateRootEntityControllerBase<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType, TCreateRequest>](#iaggregaterootentitycontrollerbasetentitydto-tidentifiertype-tcreaterequest).

### IAggregateRootEntityControllerBase<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType, TCreateRequest>
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/IAggregateRootEntityControllerBase.cs:15` · Level 3 · interface

- **What it is**: the read-write extension of [IEntityControllerBase<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#ientitycontrollerbasetentitydto-tidentifiertype): it adds `CreateAsync` and `DeleteAsync` for aggregate-root entities.
- **Depends on**: [IEntityControllerBase<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#ientitycontrollerbasetentitydto-tidentifiertype) (base interface), [IBaseDTO<TIdentifierType>](#ibasedtotidentifiertype) and [ICreateRequest](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icreaterequest) (constraints).
- **Concept**: the write half of the segregated controller contract introduced by [IEntityControllerBase<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#ientitycontrollerbasetentitydto-tidentifiertype). `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: only aggregate roots get a create/delete surface (`TCreateRequest : ICreateRequest`, `IAggregateRootEntityControllerBase.cs:22`), so child-collection controllers that implement only the read interface never expose mutation they should not own. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: create returns the created DTO with a 201, delete returns 204, a consistent verb-to-status contract.
- **Walkthrough**: extends the read interface (`IAggregateRootEntityControllerBase.cs:19`) and adds two members: `CreateAsync([Required] TCreateRequest request, ...)` returning the created DTO with 201 (`IAggregateRootEntityControllerBase.cs:28-30`), and `DeleteAsync(TIdentifierType id, ...)` returning 204 No Content (`IAggregateRootEntityControllerBase.cs:36-38`). Like its base interface it declares no update member: PUT is added one rung higher by [CrudEntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType, TCreateRequest, TUpdateRequest>](#crudentitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype-tcreaterequest-tupdaterequest) as a class member only, for the same "changing a shipped interface breaks every explicit implementer" reason.
- **Where it's used**: implemented by [AggregateRootEntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType, TCreateRequest>](#aggregaterootentitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype-tcreaterequest).

### ApiControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/ApiControllerBase.cs:23` · Level 5 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the base class of the entity and use-case API controllers (the OAuth and service-info bases derive `ControllerBase` directly, as the class doc at `ApiControllerBase.cs:16-21` states). It carries the `[ApiController]` behavior, the shared `HandleFailure` that turns domain errors into RFC 9457 Problem Details responses (adding a `Retry-After` header to a 429 that knows its wait), and the concurrency-token plumbing every controller that serves or conditionally writes a row reuses: `SetConcurrencyETag` and the two `HandleConditionalCommand*Async` helpers.
- **Depends on**: [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error), [ErrorType](group-01-result-error-handling.md#errortype), [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), [ErrorHttpMapping](#errorhttpmapping) (and through it [ErrorTypeSeverity](group-01-result-error-handling.md#errortypeseverity)), [IErrorLocalizer](#ierrorlocalizer) (resolved optionally from `RequestServices`), [RetryAfterHeader](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#retryafterheader) (`Format`), [ConcurrencyETag](group-08-auth.md#concurrencyetag), [SupportsIfMatchAttribute](#supportsifmatchattribute) (`RequiredToken`), and [ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult); `System.Reflection` plus a `ConcurrentDictionary` for the per-type token-property cache.
- **Concept introduced: centralized error-to-HTTP mapping.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether every endpoint fails the same way; `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]` covers keeping the HTTP-translation concern in the presentation layer rather than the domain. This is the boundary where a [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) failure from the Application/Domain layers becomes an HTTP status: the domain never knows about status codes, this base owns that mapping. The `[ApiController]` attribute (`ApiControllerBase.cs:22`) enables automatic model-state validation, binding-source inference, and `ProblemDetails` serialization.
- **Concept introduced: the status code is ranked, not positional.** `[Rubric §11, Security]` and `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` both bear on this. An aggregate failure built by `Result.Combine` carries several errors at once, and if the response status came from `errors[0]` then the *order* in which handlers appended errors would decide whether a caller saw 403 or 400. `HandleFailure` therefore hands the whole list to `ErrorHttpMapping.GetStatusCode(IReadOnlyList<Error>)` (`ApiControllerBase.cs:62`, mapping at `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Middleware/ErrorHttpMapping.cs:54-55`), which resolves the **most severe** [ErrorType](group-01-result-error-handling.md#errortype) present through [ErrorTypeSeverity](group-01-result-error-handling.md#errortypeseverity). The ranking is spelled out on the method doc (`ApiControllerBase.cs:35-44`): `Unexpected` (500), then `Unauthorized` (401), `Forbidden` (403), `TooManyRequests` (429), `Conflict` (409), `NotFound` (404), `UnprocessableEntity` (422), and finally `Invariant`/`Validation`/`Failure` (400). Equal ranks keep the earliest error, so a same-rank list behaves exactly as a positional selection would. The ranking itself lives in `MMCA.Common.Shared` on purpose (`ErrorHttpMapping.cs:49-50`), so the gRPC edge classifies the same aggregate identically. Every error still travels in the response; only the status is ranked.
- **Concept introduced: a 429 that says how long to wait.** `[Rubric §11, Security]` and `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`. A refusal such as a sign-in lockout ([ADR-029](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/029-authentication-brute-force-protection.html)) knows its own duration, carried on `Error.RetryAfter`. `SetRetryAfter` (`ApiControllerBase.cs:87`) acts only when the ranked status is 429 (`:89-92`), takes the longest `RetryAfter` among the `TooManyRequests` errors (`:94-96`, because retrying before the longest would only be refused again), and writes `Response.Headers.RetryAfter` through `RetryAfterHeader.Format` (`:97-100`) when that delay is positive, so the header uses the same whole, rounded-up seconds the rate limiters send (`:79-86`). Any other status, or a 429 with no hint, sends no header.
- **Concept introduced: one place that turns a row version into a precondition.** `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]` and `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` ([ADR-035](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/035-optimistic-concurrency.html)). A client gets the concurrency token as a weak `ETag` on a read and hands it back as `If-Match` on the next write (see [SupportsIfMatchAttribute](#supportsifmatchattribute)); this base owns both halves so they cannot drift apart.
  - `SetConcurrencyETag(object? dto)` (`:125`) is `protected`, so a custom read action, a hand-built projection, or a conditional write answering with the refreshed value emits the identical header instead of re-implementing the reflection and the base64 format (`:118-123`). A null row returns at once (`:127-128`), a row with no or an empty token gets no header (`:130-131`, because a fabricated tag would invite preconditions the write side cannot honour, `:112-117`), and otherwise it writes `ConcurrencyETag.Format(rowVersion)` under `ConcurrencyETag.ETagHeaderName` (`:133`).
  - `ReadRowVersion` (`:239`) reads the token off the row's RUNTIME shape: a shaped dictionary (a `fields=` projection) is probed under `rowVersion`, then `RowVersion` (`:241-246`); a typed value goes through `RowVersionProperties.GetOrAdd(dto.GetType(), FindRowVersionProperty)` (`:248`). That static `ConcurrentDictionary<Type, PropertyInfo?>` (`:30`) resolves each runtime type once, since a type's shape cannot change at runtime (`:25-29`), and `FindRowVersionProperty` (`:255-260`) accepts only a public instance, readable, non-indexer `byte[]` property named exactly `RowVersion`. Keying on the runtime type is what lets the helper live on this non-generic base rather than on a DTO-typed one.
  - `HandleConditionalCommandAsync<TCommand, TResult>` (`:153`, `TResult : Result` at `:158`) and `HandleConditionalCommandWithResponseAsync<TCommand, TResponse>` (`:184`) run a conditional write end to end. Each null-guards its handler and command builder eagerly (`:160-161`, `:190-191`), reads the token `SupportsIfMatchAttribute.RequiredToken(HttpContext)` decoded (`:163`, `:193`; an action without `[SupportsIfMatch]` throws `InvalidOperationException` and the handler never runs, `:151-152`, `:182-183`), builds the command from it, and hands off to a private awaited half (`:164`, `:194`; the split keeps the argument checks eager, `:197`, `:215`). `RunConditionalCommandAsync` (`:198`) dispatches (`:205`), maps a failure through `HandleFailure` (`:206-207`), awaits the optional `onSuccess` follow-up such as a cache eviction (`:209-210`), and answers 204 (`:212`). `RunConditionalCommandWithResponseAsync` (`:216`) unwraps the value with `TryGetValue` (`:223-224`), awaits `onSuccess` with it (`:226-227`), emits the refreshed token through `SetConcurrencyETag(value)` (`:229`), and answers 200 with the value (`:230`), so the client already holds the precondition for its next write ([ADR-144](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/144-offline-writes-and-delta-reads.html) relies on that round trip).
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleFailure(IEnumerable<Error> errors)` (`ApiControllerBase.cs:49`) is `protected virtual`:
  - Null/empty guard (`ApiControllerBase.cs:51-59`): with no errors it returns a 500 "Unknown error", treating an empty failure as a programming mistake rather than a domain outcome.
  - Ranked status (`ApiControllerBase.cs:62`, with the intent stated in the comment just above it at `ApiControllerBase.cs:61`).
  - Builds a `ProblemDetails` with that status and a fixed title/detail (`ApiControllerBase.cs:64-69`), attaches `Extensions["errors"]` via `ErrorHttpMapping.BuildErrorsExtension` (`ApiControllerBase.cs:72`), optionally localized through an [IErrorLocalizer](#ierrorlocalizer) resolved with `GetService` (`ApiControllerBase.cs:71`, so a host without localization simply passes `null`), sets the `Retry-After` header when it applies (`ApiControllerBase.cs:74`), then returns `StatusCode(statusCode, problemDetails)` (`ApiControllerBase.cs:76`).
- **Why it's built this way**: one `virtual` method instead of a `switch` in every action removes duplication and makes the response shape uniform ([ADR-013](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/013-result-pattern.html) for why failures are values rather than exceptions in the first place); keeping it `virtual` lets a subclass ([EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype)) wrap it with logging without reimplementing the mapping. The `ErrorHttpMapping` members are `internal static` (`ErrorHttpMapping.cs:41`, `:50`, `:61`), which is what lets [UnhandledResultFailureFilter](#unhandledresultfailurefilter) reuse the same ranked status mapping and the same `errors` extension array for a failed `Result` that an action returned without calling `HandleFailure` (`UnhandledResultFailureFilter.cs:36` and `:47`). The two bodies are deliberately *not* identical: the filter labels its `ProblemDetails` `Title`/`Detail` "Unhandled result failure" / "The action returned a Result.Failure that was not mapped to an HTTP error response." (`UnhandledResultFailureFilter.cs:42-43`) against the base's "Operation failed" / "One or more errors occurred." (`ApiControllerBase.cs:67-68`), so a response that fell through the filter is distinguishable from one the controller mapped on purpose. Localization is the [ADR-027](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/027-multi-locale-i18n.html) extension point, keyed by `Error.Code` and leaving `Code`/`Type`/`Source`/`Target` verbatim so clients can still branch on them (`ErrorHttpMapping.cs:60-63` for the contract, `ErrorHttpMapping.cs:62-70` for the projection that honours it).
- **Where it's used**: the root of the entity and use-case controller hierarchy. [EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype), [AuthControllerBase](#authcontrollerbase), [PasswordResetAuthControllerBase<TForgotPasswordCommand, TResetPasswordCommand>](#passwordresetauthcontrollerbasetforgotpasswordcommand-tresetpasswordcommand), [DataExportControllerBase<TQuery>](#dataexportcontrollerbasetquery), and every module controller derive from it directly or transitively; [OAuthControllerBase](#oauthcontrollerbase) and [ServiceInfoControllerBase](#serviceinfocontrollerbase) do not. The architecture rule `ControllersInheritApiControllerBase` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing.Architecture/Rules/Api/ArchitectureRules.Controllers.cs:66`) fails a module controller that derives raw `ControllerBase` unless it is listed as exempt. The conditional-write helpers are used by ADC's `EventLifecycleController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Events/EventLifecycleController.cs:72`, `HandleConditionalCommandAsync`) and `QuestionsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Questions/QuestionsController.cs:67`, `HandleConditionalCommandWithResponseAsync`) among others, and `SetConcurrencyETag` directly by ADC's `SpeakersController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Speakers/SpeakersController.cs:301`). Framework coverage is `ApiControllerBaseTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Controllers/ApiControllerBaseTests.cs`) and `ApiControllerBaseConditionalCommandTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Controllers/ApiControllerBaseConditionalCommandTests.cs`).

### EmailConfirmationControllerBase<TSendCommand, TConfirmCommand>
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/EmailConfirmationControllerBase.cs:43` · Level 6 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the abstract base for the two anonymous email-confirmation endpoints, `POST send-email-confirmation` and `POST confirm-email` ([ADR-116](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/116-identity-completions-opt-in.html) is the identity-completions decision it implements; the route names are at `EmailConfirmationControllerBase.cs:78` and `:106`). The app subclass supplies the route prefix, the API version and two one-line command factories; the base owns the HTTP shape.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (base, for `HandleFailure`), [ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) (one handler per action, both returning [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result)), [ICommandWithRequest<out TRequest>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandwithrequestout-trequest) (the generic constraints, `:46-47`), [SendEmailConfirmationRequest](group-08-auth.md#sendemailconfirmationrequest) and [ConfirmEmailRequest](group-08-auth.md#confirmemailrequest) (the bodies), [IdempotentAttribute](#idempotentattribute), and the `RateLimitPolicyAuthIp` constant on [WebApplicationBuilderExtensions](#webapplicationbuilderextensions).
- **Concept introduced: a sibling controller instead of a longer inheritance chain.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses uniform endpoint conventions; `[Rubric §1, SOLID]` covers keeping the base closed and the variation in small abstract members. The app's own `AuthController` already occupies the single-inheritance chain ([AuthControllerBase](#authcontrollerbase) to [UserAccountAuthControllerBase<TChangePasswordCommand, TChangePreferencesCommand>](#useraccountauthcontrollerbasetchangepasswordcommand-tchangepreferencescommand)), so confirmation ships as a separate controller routed to the same `Auth` prefix, which reaches the gateway through the existing `/Auth` route (`:13-20`). The commands stay app-side, the same split [PasswordResetAuthControllerBase<TForgotPasswordCommand, TResetPasswordCommand>](#passwordresetauthcontrollerbasetforgotpasswordcommand-tresetpasswordcommand) uses: the derived controller supplies each through `CreateSendCommand` (`:61`) and `CreateConfirmCommand` (`:69`), and the base reads the request back only through `ICommandWithRequest<TRequest>` (`:35-38`).
- **Concept introduced: anonymous by necessity, and non-enumerating.** `[Rubric §11, Security]` is the angle. Both actions are `[AllowAnonymous]` because the link is followed from a mail client with no session, and an unconfirmed account may be unable to sign in at all (`:23-27`). Send answers 202 on every well-formed request: the handler treats an unknown address and an already-confirmed one as success, so the response never reveals which addresses hold accounts, and only a malformed payload reaches 400 through the request validator (`:30-33`, `:78-84`). Both carry `[EnableRateLimiting(RateLimitPolicyAuthIp)]` (`:81`, `:109`) and declare the 429 (`:84`, `:113`), for the same reason login and recovery do ([ADR-019](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/019-rate-limiting.html), [ADR-029](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/029-authentication-brute-force-protection.html)).
- **Walkthrough**
  - Primary constructor (`:43-45`) exposes the two handlers as `protected` `SendHandler` (`:50`) and `ConfirmHandler` (`:53`).
  - `SendEmailConfirmationAsync` (`:85`): `[HttpPost("send-email-confirmation")]`, `[Idempotent]`, `[AllowAnonymous]` (`:78-80`); dispatches `CreateSendCommand(request)` (`:89-91`) and returns `Accepted()` on success, `HandleFailure(result.Errors)` otherwise (`:93-95`).
  - `ConfirmEmailAsync` (`:114`): `[HttpPost("confirm-email")]`, `[Idempotent]`, `[AllowAnonymous]` (`:106-108`); the link carries the address and token in the URI fragment, so the page reads them client-side and posts them here and neither reaches a server log or a `Referer` header (`:98-101`). It returns `NoContent()` on success (`:122-124`) and declares 400, 401 and 429 (`:111-113`).
- **Why it's built this way**: unlike the credential-issuing endpoints in [AuthControllerBase](#authcontrollerbase), neither action mints a token pair, so both opt in with `[Idempotent]` ([ADR-017](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/017-request-idempotency.html)): a retried send or confirm replays the stored answer instead of re-running the handler. Both methods are `virtual` so an app can wrap them. Errors go through the shared `HandleFailure`, so a failed confirm is the same RFC 9457 shape as every other endpoint.
- **Where it's used**: subclassed by ADC's `EmailConfirmationController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/EmailConfirmationController.cs:29`). Both actions are listed by name in the framework's anonymous-endpoint architecture gate (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Architecture/MMCA.Common.Architecture.Tests/Api/AnonymousEndpointTests.cs:42-43`), so their `[AllowAnonymous]` is an approved exception. Framework coverage is `EmailConfirmationControllerBaseTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Controllers/Auth/EmailConfirmationControllerBaseTests.cs`).

### OAuthControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/OAuthControllerBase.cs:56` · Level 6 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the base controller for external sign-in (Google, GitHub, Apple, and any configured generic OIDC provider). It runs the challenge/callback/complete/exchange dance so a browser or native head can log in through a provider and receive a local JWT pair without ever exposing tokens in a redirect URL, and it answers the home-realm discovery question "which organization provider owns this email domain".
- **Depends on**: [IAuthenticationService](group-08-auth.md#iauthenticationservice) (`ExternalLoginAsync`), [ICacheService](group-09-caching.md#icacheservice) (`SetAsync`, `GetFromSharedStoreAsync`, `RemoveAsync`), `IConfiguration`, an optional [IDistributedLock](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#idistributedlock) (`TryAcquireAsync`), [ExternalAuthExtensions](#externalauthextensions) (the scheme constant, `OAuthControllerBase.cs:68`), [OidcProviderRegistry](#oidcproviderregistry) (`Find`, `FindByEmail`, resolved optionally from `RequestServices`), [OidcDiscoveryResponse](group-08-auth.md#oidcdiscoveryresponse), [WebApplicationBuilderExtensions](#webapplicationbuilderextensions) (`RateLimitPolicyAuthIp`), [NonIdempotentAttribute](#nonidempotentattribute), [AuthenticationResponse](group-08-auth.md#authenticationresponse), [OAuthCodeExchangeRequest](group-08-auth.md#oauthcodeexchangerequest), and [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error); the Google, GitHub, and Apple OAuth packages and `System.Security.Cryptography`.
- **Concept introduced: the code-exchange OAuth completion pattern.** `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses how credentials move through the system; the design's whole point is that the redirect after a successful provider login carries only a single-use opaque code, never the access/refresh tokens, so tokens never land in the address bar, browser history, the `Referer` header, or upstream access logs (`OAuthControllerBase.cs:245-247`). `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]`: the base is hoisted from the app hosts so every service reuses the identical flow, with the sealed subclass supplying only `[Route("auth/oauth")]` and versioning, and the burn check reads the shared cache store so the single-use guarantee holds across replicas. A subclass can go further and pass an `IDistributedLock` to the four-argument primary constructor (`:56-60`), which makes the read-then-burn one critical section across replicas (`:304-323`); `AddCaching` registers a Redis-backed lock when an `IConnectionMultiplexer` is present, and the three-argument constructor (`:102`) leaves the lock `null`, so single-use then holds per replica only. See [ADR-036](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/036-external-oauth-login.html).
- **Concept introduced: generic OIDC providers and home-realm discovery.** `[Rubric §11, Security]` and `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`. Beyond the three named providers, any provider configured under `OAuth:Oidc:<Name>` (registered by `ExternalAuthExtensions.AddExternalAuthProviders`, `:144-145`) is reachable through one parameterized route. `OidcLogin` (`:158`, `[HttpGet("{provider}")]` and `[AllowAnonymous]` at `:154-155`) looks the name up ignoring case through `OidcProviders?.Find(provider)` and either challenges with the provider's registered name or answers 404 (`:160-161`). The literal `google`, `github`, and `apple` routes take precedence and those names are reserved, so an OIDC provider can never shadow them, and MAUI heads reach it through the same `auth/oauth/{provider}` URL the external auth broker already builds (`:146-148`). `DiscoverOidcProvider` (`:179`; `[HttpGet("discover")]`, `[AllowAnonymous]`, and `[EnableRateLimiting(WebApplicationBuilderExtensions.RateLimitPolicyAuthIp)]` at `:174-176`) maps the domain of an email to the provider whose `Domains` list claims it through `FindByEmail` (`:181`) and answers an `OidcDiscoveryResponse(Name, DisplayName)` or 404 (`:182-184`), so a sign-in page can offer one "Sign in with your organization" step instead of a button per identity provider. The answer reveals only which configured provider owns a domain, never whether an account exists, and it is throttled per client IP like the other anonymous auth endpoints (`:164-171`). The registry comes from `OidcProviders` (`:188`), an optional `GetService` lookup, because `AddExternalAuthProviders` registers it only when at least one OIDC provider is configured (`:187`); a host with none answers 404 on both actions.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `OAuthExchangeCodePrefix` (`OAuthControllerBase.cs:73`) and a 2-minute `OAuthExchangeCodeLifetime` (`:87`) namespace and time-box the server-side token stash; the short TTL matches the single redirect-then-POST round trip. A second constant, `ClientStateItemKey = "clientState"` (`:77`), names a separate `AuthenticationProperties.Items` slot for the client's own opaque per-attempt value, deliberately not called `"state"` because that name belongs to the OAuth handler's own protocol value (`:75-76`). The redeem lock uses a 10-second lease and a 5-second wait (`RedeemLockTimeToLive`, `RedeemLockWait`, `:92-93`), matching the password-reset token service: the critical section is one cache read and one remove. `UIBaseUrlConfigKey = "OAuth:UIBaseUrl"` (`:66`) is public so the AppHost can set the same key through `WithUiLinks` as `OAuth__UIBaseUrl` (`:62-65`).
  - `GoogleLogin` (`:117`), `GitHubLogin` (`:127`), and `AppleLogin` (`:140`) each take an optional `state` query parameter alongside `returnUrl` and carry `[AllowAnonymous]` directly (`:116`, `:126`, `:139`); all three, like `OidcLogin`, call `ChallengeProvider` (`:456`), which stashes both `returnUrl` and the client's `state` under `ClientStateItemKey` in `AuthenticationProperties.Items` and sets `RedirectUri = "/auth/oauth/complete"` (`:458-470`). Apple needs no special action here: its callback arrives as a cross-site form POST (`response_mode=form_post` is forced by the name/email scopes) that the middleware handles at `/auth/callback/apple` like any other provider (`:132-134`).
  - `CompleteAsync` (`:207`) carries `[AllowAnonymous]` (`:205`). It reads the UI base URL through `UIBaseUrlConfigKey` (`:209`), and after the middleware handles the provider callback it reads the external cookie (`:210`), redirects to `/login?error=oauth_failed` when the ticket did not survive (`:212-217`), reads both the stashed `returnUrl` and `clientState` via `ReadChallengeState` (`:222`, `:258-259`), extracts provider claims (`ExtractClaims`, `:225`), calls `ExternalLoginAsync` to find or create the local user and mint tokens (`:232-233`), signs out the temporary external cookie (`:243`), then mints a 32-byte hex `exchangeCode` (`:248`), stashes the token pair in the cache under it (`:249-250`), and redirects with the code and, when the client sent one, the echoed `state` (`:252`, URL built by `BuildSuccessRedirectUrl` at `:261-275`).
  - `ReadChallengeState` (`:258-259`) is a safe-lookup helper (`GetString` is `TryGetValue` under the hood): the challenge normally stashes both `returnUrl` and `clientState`, but a ticket minted without them (custom challenge, provider round-trip edge) falls back to `"/"` and `null` instead of throwing on the `Items` indexer.
