See [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html).
- **Walkthrough**
  - Constants: `AlwaysOnFilter = "AlwaysOn"` (`:22`), `TargetingFilter = "Microsoft.Targeting"` (`:25`), private `WholeGroup = "100"` (`:28`).
  - `ToDefinition` (`:33`) switches on the mode: `On` becomes one `AlwaysOn` filter with empty parameters (`:35-39`); `Percentage` a targeting filter with no users or groups and the stored percentage (`:40`); `Targeting` a targeting filter with the stored users, groups and percentage (`:41`); `Off` a definition with no filters (`:42`); an unknown mode also evaluates to off (`:44-45`). A null percentage becomes `0`.
  - `Targeting` (`:89`) builds the filter's parameters as the configuration section the targeting filter binds from: `Audience:DefaultRolloutPercentage` (`:96`), `Audience:Users:{i}` (`:101`), and per group `Audience:Groups:{i}:Name` with a `RolloutPercentage` of `WholeGroup` (`:106-107`). The doc comment's point (`:84-88`): the binding is the filter's own, not a copy of its settings shape.
  - `Describe` (`:51`) returns `Off` for a null or `Disabled` definition (`:53-56`) or one with no named filters (`:58-65`); `On` when every filter is always-on, or any is and the requirement type is `Any` (`:68-71`); otherwise `Conditional` with the filter names (`:74`). `IsAlwaysOn` accepts `AlwaysOn` or `On`, case-insensitively (`:77-79`).
- **Where it's used**: [StoredOverrideFeatureDefinitionProvider](#storedoverridefeaturedefinitionprovider) calls `ToDefinition` for every overridden flag; [FeatureFlagAdministrationService](#featureflagadministrationservice) calls both methods to report configured and effective state.

### FeatureFlagCatalog
> MMCA.Common.Infrastructure · `MMCA.Common.Infrastructure.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagCatalog.cs:17` · Level 3 · class (internal, sealed)

- **What it is**: the set of flags this host declares, computed once from every registered [FeatureFlagAssembly](#featureflagassembly). Only these names can carry a stored override (`FeatureFlagCatalog.cs:11-15`).
- **Depends on**: [FeatureFlagAssembly](#featureflagassembly), [FeatureFlagRegistry](#featureflagregistry), [FeatureFlagDescriptor](#featureflagdescriptor).
- **Concept introduced (declared flags as the override allow-list).** `[Rubric §34, Architecture Governance & Documentation]`: flags are declared by `[FeatureFlag]` constants on `*Features` classes, and this catalog makes that declaration the boundary for runtime overrides, so an operator cannot create an override for a name no code reads.
- **Walkthrough**: `_flags` is a `Lazy` (`:19-22`) over `FeatureFlagRegistry.Describe` on the distinct assemblies (`:20`), de-duplicated by flag name case-insensitively (`:21`). `Flags` exposes it, ordered by name per the doc (`:24-25`). `TryFind` (`:31`) trims the input (`:33`) and matches case-insensitively, returning the declared spelling (`:34-35`).
- **Where it's used**: registered with `TryAddSingleton` by `AddReloadableFeatureFlags` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/DependencyInjection.FeatureFlags.cs:72`); consumed by [FeatureFlagAdministrationService](#featureflagadministrationservice) for listing and for rejecting undeclared names.

### StoredOverrideFeatureDefinitionProvider
> MMCA.Common.Infrastructure · `MMCA.Common.Infrastructure.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/StoredOverrideFeatureDefinitionProvider.cs:31` · Level 3 · class (internal, sealed, partial)

- **What it is**: the `IFeatureDefinitionProvider` that `AddReloadableFeatureFlags` layers over the configured one: a flag with a stored override answers with the override's definition, every other flag is answered by configuration unchanged (`StoredOverrideFeatureDefinitionProvider.cs:8-13`).
- **Depends on**: `Microsoft.FeatureManagement.IFeatureDefinitionProvider` (implemented and wrapped), [IFeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot](#ifeatureflagoverridesnapshot), [FeatureFlagDefinitions](#featureflagdefinitions), [FeatureFlagOverrideResponse](#featureflagoverrideresponse), `ILogger`.
- **Concept introduced (runtime flag changes with no restart).** `[Rubric §2, Design Patterns]`: a **decorator** on the definition provider; the FeatureGate decorators keep calling `IFeatureManager`, which asks this provider on every evaluation, so a stored override takes effect on the next evaluation after the snapshot is invalidated (`:15-18`). `[Rubric §29, Resilience, Reliability & Business Continuity]`: the override layer is never more fragile than the configuration under it.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `Configured` (`:40`) exposes the wrapped provider, which is what "the configured value" means on the administration surface.
  - `GetFeatureDefinitionAsync` (`:43`) looks the name up in the loaded overrides (`:45`) and returns either the configured definition or `FeatureFlagDefinitions.ToDefinition(stored)` (`:47-49`).
  - `GetAllFeatureDefinitionsAsync` (`:53`) streams the configured definitions, substituting overridden ones (`:58-63`), then yields any override for a name configuration never mentions (`:65-69`).
  - `Find` (`:72`) compares names case-insensitively, like the configuration keys.
  - `OverridesOrNoneAsync` (`:81`) catches any non-cancellation exception from the snapshot (`:87`), logs one Warning (`:89`, message at `:94`) and returns an empty list (`:90`), so a transient database error or an unapplied migration degrades to configuration instead of failing every feature-gated command and query (`:75-80`).
- **Where it's used**: installed by `services.TryDecorate<IFeatureDefinitionProvider>` in `AddReloadableFeatureFlags`, which throws if no provider is registered yet ("Call it after AddAPI() (or AddFeatureManagement())") (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/DependencyInjection.FeatureFlags.cs:77-85`). [FeatureFlagAdministrationService](#featureflagadministrationservice) unwraps it through `Configured`.

### FeatureFlagAdministrationService
> MMCA.Common.Infrastructure · `MMCA.Common.Infrastructure.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagAdministrationService.cs:30` · Level 4 · class (internal, sealed, partial)

- **What it is**: the implementation of [IFeatureFlagAdministrationService](#ifeatureflagadministrationservice): list every declared flag with its configured and effective state, set an override, clear one.
- **Depends on**: [FeatureFlagCatalog](#featureflagcatalog), [IFeatureFlagOverrideStore](#ifeatureflagoverridestore), [IFeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot](#ifeatureflagoverridesnapshot), `IFeatureDefinitionProvider`, [FeatureFlagDefinitions](#featureflagdefinitions), [FeatureFlagStatusResponse](#featureflagstatusresponse), [SetFeatureFlagOverrideRequest](#setfeatureflagoverriderequest), [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error).
- **Concept introduced**: none new; it composes the catalog, store and snapshot taught in their own sections. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: the operator view shows both what configuration says and what actually applies.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `ConfiguredProvider` (`:41-42`) is the layered provider's `Configured` when the registered provider is a [StoredOverrideFeatureDefinitionProvider](#storedoverridefeaturedefinitionprovider), else the registered provider itself.
  - `ListAsync` (`:45`) reads overrides from the store, not the snapshot, so the operator sees the rows as they are rather than one replica's cache (`:47-49`), then describes every catalog flag (`:52-56`).
  - `SetOverrideAsync` (`:62`) returns a not-found [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) `FeatureFlag.Undeclared` for an undeclared name (`:69-72`, `:103-106`), writes through the store under the declared spelling (`:74`), propagates a store failure (`:75-78`), invalidates the snapshot (`:80`) and returns the new status (`:82`).
  - `ClearOverrideAsync` (`:86`) follows the same shape (`:88-99`).
  - `InvalidateAsync` (`:111`) swallows a non-cancellation failure with a Warning (`:117`, message at `:108`): the write is already committed, and other evaluations may serve the previous snapshot until it expires.
  - `DescribeAsync` (`:123`) computes the configured state from `ConfiguredProvider` (`:125-126`) and the effective kind from the override when there is one (`:128-130`), returning a [FeatureFlagStatusResponse](#featureflagstatusresponse) with the descriptor's lifetime, owner and remove-by date (`:132-140`).
- **Where it's used**: registered with `TryAddScoped<IFeatureFlagAdministrationService, FeatureFlagAdministrationService>()` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/DependencyInjection.FeatureFlags.cs:75`); the HTTP surface is [FeatureFlagsControllerBase](#featureflagscontrollerbase).

### FeatureFlagOverrideModelBuilderExtensions
> MMCA.Common.Infrastructure · `MMCA.Common.Infrastructure.Persistence.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideModelBuilderExtensions.cs:15` · Level 5 · class (public, static)

- **What it is**: the EF Core mapping of [FeatureFlagOverride](#featureflagoverride) to the `FeatureFlagOverrides` table, as a `ModelBuilder` extension method.
- **Depends on**: [FeatureFlagOverride](#featureflagoverride) (its length constants), `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.ModelBuilder`.
- **Concept introduced**: none new; gated table mapping is taught at [FeatureFlagOverrideModelGate](#featureflagoverridemodelgate). `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]`: the mapping pins storage choices that keep a row's meaning stable.
- **Walkthrough**
  - Public constants `TableName = "FeatureFlagOverrides"` (`:18`) and `NameColumn = "Name"` (`:21`); private `ModeMaxLength = 16` (`:24`).
  - `ApplyFeatureFlagOverrideConfiguration(schema = "dbo")` (`:34`) maps to the table (`:40`) keyed on `Id` (`:41`), which is stored in the `Name` column, non-unicode, at `FeatureFlagOverride.NameMaxLength`, never generated (`:43-48`). `Mode` is stored as its string name so a reordered enum cannot silently change what a row means (`:50-54`). `TargetUsers`, `TargetGroups` and the required `Reason` take the entity's max lengths (`:56-58`); the computed `Name`, `Users` and `Groups` are ignored (`:60-62`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the doc requires calling it BEFORE the soft-delete, tenant and concurrency-token passes so the override is treated like any other auditable entity (`:26-30`); [ApplicationDbContext](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#applicationdbcontext) does so, first in `OnModelCreating` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/DbContexts/ApplicationDbContext.cs:532-538`).
- **Where it's used**: `ApplicationDbContext.OnModelCreating` when the gate is open (above); [EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore](#effeatureflagoverridestore) references it.

### FeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot
> MMCA.Common.Infrastructure · `MMCA.Common.Infrastructure.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot.cs:46` · Level 5 · class (internal, sealed)

- **What it is**: the cached, whole-table copy of the stored overrides that every flag evaluation reads, implementing [IFeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot](#ifeatureflagoverridesnapshot).
- **Depends on**: [ICacheService](group-09-caching.md#icacheservice), `IServiceScopeFactory`, `IOptions<`[FeatureFlagOverrideSettings](#featureflagoverridesettings)`>`, optional `IHostEnvironment`, [IFeatureFlagOverrideStore](#ifeatureflagoverridestore) (resolved per load).
- **Concept introduced (cross-replica invalidation by key prefix).** `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`: evaluation must not hit the database each time, so the table is read once per lifetime and shared. `[Rubric §29, Resilience, Reliability & Business Continuity]`: a write invalidates by prefix through `ICacheService.RemoveByPrefixAsync`, which on a Redis-backed host replaces one generation token so every replica stops addressing the old snapshot; on a memory-only host `FeatureFlags:OverrideCacheSeconds` is the staleness bound for other replicas (`FeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot.cs:20-24`).
- **Walkthrough**
  - `KeyRoot = "featureflags:overrides:"` (`:53`); `_lifetime` is `OverrideCacheSeconds` (`:55`).
  - `Prefix` (`:62`) is `KeyRoot` + service name + `:` + data-source name + `:` (`BuildPrefix`, `:77-78`). Both parts are needed: services sharing one Redis on the default data-source name would otherwise share one snapshot (`:27-33`). `ResolveServiceName` (`:85`) uses `IHostEnvironment.ApplicationName`, else the entry assembly name, else `"host"` (`:93`).
  - `GetAsync` (`:65`) is `GetOrCreateAsync` on `Prefix + "all"` (`:66`) with `LoadAsync` as the factory, so the key and the invalidated prefix cannot disagree. `InvalidateAsync` (`:70-71`) removes by `Prefix`.
  - `LoadAsync` (`:99`) opens its own async scope (`:101`) because the store is scoped while this class is a singleton (`:35-37`), and returns an array so the two-level cache can serialize it (`:104-105`).
- **Where it's used**: registered with `TryAddSingleton<IFeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot, FeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot>()` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/DependencyInjection.FeatureFlags.cs:74`); read by [StoredOverrideFeatureDefinitionProvider](#storedoverridefeaturedefinitionprovider), invalidated by [FeatureFlagAdministrationService](#featureflagadministrationservice). The singleton-plus-scope shape follows [PermissionGrantCache](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#permissiongrantcache) (`:36-37`).

### EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore
> MMCA.Common.Infrastructure · `MMCA.Common.Infrastructure.Persistence.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/FeatureFlags/EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore.cs:33` · Level 10 · class (internal, sealed)

- **What it is**: the EF Core implementation of [IFeatureFlagOverrideStore](#ifeatureflagoverridestore): read all active overrides, upsert one, soft-clear one, in the owning service's own database.
- **Depends on**: [IDbContextFactory](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#idbcontextfactory), [IEntityDataSourceRegistry](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ientitydatasourceregistry), [IDataSourceResolver](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#idatasourceresolver), [DataSourceKey](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#datasourcekey), [ApplicationDbContext](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#applicationdbcontext), [FeatureFlagOverride](#featureflagoverride), [FeatureFlagOverrideResponse](#featureflagoverrideresponse), [FeatureFlagOverrideSettings](#featureflagoverridesettings), [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result).
- **Concept introduced**: none new; soft delete, audit stamping and data-source resolution are taught in the primer and in [G07](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#applicationdbcontext). `[Rubric §4, DDD]`: every write goes through the aggregate's `Create`, `Change` and `Clear`, which return `Result`.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `Context` (`:39`) resolves the context for `ResolveDataSourceKey()` on each access; `Overrides` is its `Set<FeatureFlagOverride>()` (`:41`).
  - `ResolveDataSourceKey` (`:139`) uses the registry's mapping for the entity when present (`:140`), else the configured `DataSourceName` verbatim with only the engine taken from the resolver's default (`:142-144`), the [EFPermissionGrantStore](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#efpermissiongrantstore) reasoning (`:137-138`).
  - `GetAllAsync` (`:44`) is a no-tracking query with an explicit `!IsDeleted` predicate, ordered by `Id` (`:48-53`); the predicate is redundant with the soft-delete filter but keeps a hand-mapped table correct (`:46-47`).
  - `SetAsync` (`:59`) loads the row ignoring query filters, so a soft-cleared row is revived rather than duplicated (`:67-70`). A new name goes through `FeatureFlagOverride.Create` and `AddAsync` (`:75-83`); an existing row through `Change` (`:87`). Domain failures return as `Result.Failure`; success saves through the factory (`:96`) and maps the entity (`:98`).
  - `ClearAsync` (`:102`) finds an active row (`:106-108`); none is success, the idempotent-clear promise (`:110-113`); otherwise `Clear()` and save (`:116-122`).
  - `ToResponse` (`:126`) reports `LastModifiedOn ?? CreatedOn` and `LastModifiedBy ?? CreatedBy` (`:134-135`).
- **Where it's used**: registered with `TryAddScoped<IFeatureFlagOverrideStore, EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore>()` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/DependencyInjection.FeatureFlags.cs:73`); called by [FeatureFlagAdministrationService](#featureflagadministrationservice) directly and by [FeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot](#featureflagoverridesnapshot) on each load. [ADR-073](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/073-multi-tenancy-model.html) mentions it.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: whether `GetAllAsync` would surface another tenant's rows depends on the tenant filter applied by `ApplicationDbContext`; not determinable from this file alone.

### IBaseDTO<TIdentifierType>
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.DTOs` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/DTOs/IBaseDTO.cs:9` · Level 0 · interface

- **What it is**: a one-property marker interface. Every DTO that carries an entity identifier exposes `TIdentifierType Id { get; init; }` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/DTOs/IBaseDTO.cs:13`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party, and nothing external beyond the generic constraint. It lives in `MMCA.Common.Shared`, the bottom layer, so a Blazor WebAssembly client and an EF-backed service can both reference it.
- **Concept introduced (the DTO and the marker/role interface).** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses DTOs decoupled from domain entities and stable wire contracts: a **DTO** (Data Transfer Object) is the shape that crosses the wire, deliberately separate from the domain entity. `IBaseDTO` lets generic machinery treat *any* DTO uniformly through its `Id`, which is what makes a single generic read service, a single generic controller base, and a single generic UI service possible instead of one hand-written trio per entity. This is also `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: a textbook **Interface Segregation** interface, with one member (the only thing a generic consumer needs), so clients never depend on more than they use.
- **Walkthrough**: generic over `TIdentifierType` with a `where TIdentifierType : notnull` constraint (`IBaseDTO.cs:10`); the single `Id` is `get; init;` (`IBaseDTO.cs:13`), settable at construction and immutable after. The `init`-not-`set` choice recurs across these contracts (see the primer on [immutability with required/init](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)). The doc comment (`IBaseDTO.cs:3-6`) names the two consumers it exists for: the generic query service and the controller bases.
- **Why it's built this way**: making the identifier type a generic parameter, rather than hard-coding `int`, lets a DTO match its entity's strongly-typed id alias (see [identifier aliases](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)), so a `Guid`-keyed aggregate and an `int`-keyed one share the same generic pipeline; the `notnull` constraint forbids `Id` being a nullable type, which keeps the generic code free of null checks on the one value it always needs.
- **Where it's used**: it is the constraint on every generic read/write pipeline in the framework, each declaring the same `where TEntityDTO : IBaseDTO<TIdentifierType>` line: [IEntityQueryService<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](group-03-querying-specifications.md#ientityqueryservicetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Interfaces/Mapping/IEntityQueryService.cs:21`), [IEntityDTOMapper<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Interfaces/Mapping/IEntityDTOMapper.cs:16`), the generic create handler [CreateEntityHandlerBase<TCreateRequest, TEntity, TIdentifierType, TEntityDTO>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#createentityhandlerbasetcreaterequest-tentity-tidentifiertype-tentitydto) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/UseCases/Crud/CreateEntityHandlerBase.cs:50`), the controller hierarchy ([EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) at `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/EntityControllerBase.cs:41`), and the UI's [EntityServiceBase<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#entityservicebasetentitydto-tidentifiertype) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Api/EntityServiceBase.cs:39`). Implemented directly by [BaseLookup<TIdentifierType>](#baselookuptidentifiertype) and by every module DTO in ADC, Store, and Helpdesk (for example `MMCA.Helpdesk/Source/Modules/Tickets/MMCA.Helpdesk.Tickets.Shared/Tickets/TicketDTO.cs:10`).

### IConcurrencyAware
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.DTOs` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/DTOs/IConcurrencyAware.cs:15` · Level 0 · interface

- **What it is**: the contract for **read** DTOs that expose the current optimistic-concurrency token (`byte[] RowVersion`). The API renders that token as the response `ETag`, and the client echoes it back in the `If-Match` header of its next write.
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party. It is the wire-side half of a pair whose persistence-side half is [IWriteRepository<TEntity, TIdentifierType>](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iwriterepositorytentity-tidentifiertype)`.SetOriginalRowVersion`; the header encoding of the same bytes is [ConcurrencyETag](group-08-auth.md#concurrencyetag), which names this interface as the byte-array side of the translation (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/Http/ConcurrencyETag.cs:7`).
- **Concept introduced (optimistic concurrency, header-only).** `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]` assesses deliberate persistence: transactions, migrations, soft-delete, audit, and **concurrency control**. The token is a version stamp that changes on every save of a row: on SQL Server it is a server-generated `rowversion`, and on PostgreSQL and SQLite it is stamped on every save instead (`IConcurrencyAware.cs:17`). A read DTO carries the current `RowVersion` so the client can state a precondition on the next write, and the persistence layer can then refuse a conflicting concurrent edit instead of silently overwriting it. The doc comment (`IConcurrencyAware.cs:3-7`) spells out the full round trip: DTO to `ETag` to `If-Match` to `SetOriginalRowVersion`. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` applies too, because the precondition is expressed in HTTP's own vocabulary rather than in a body field.
- **Walkthrough**: one property, `byte[] RowVersion { get; init; }` (`IConcurrencyAware.cs:19`), and it is **not nullable**. The remark (`IConcurrencyAware.cs:9-13`) is the reason: the token is the `If-Match` header's whole content, so it is never optional. A DTO read from a persisted aggregate always has one (`AuditableBaseEntity.RowVersion` is non-null), and a write that states no precondition is refused with `428 Precondition Required` rather than falling back to last-write-wins. The same remark states the other half of the current design: **update requests carry no token**, because the precondition travels in the header alone. Note the `[SuppressMessage("Performance", "CA1819")]` on `IConcurrencyAware.cs:18`: exposing a `byte[]` property normally trips the "properties should not return arrays" analyzer rule, but it is required to round-trip the EF token, and the suppression is *justified inline* (`[Rubric §15, Best Practices]`: suppressions are tracked and explained, not blanket-disabled).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-035](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/035-optimistic-concurrency.html) puts the precondition on the resource read rather than on each request model, so a module adds conditional writes by making its read DTO concurrency-aware plus tagging the action, and no request record has to loosen its immutability to receive a token. Keeping the contract in `Shared` means the same interface is visible to the Blazor client that must echo the tag and to the Infrastructure repository that consumes the bytes.
- **Where it's used**: implemented by the read DTOs across the apps: ADC's `SessionDTO` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared/Sessions/SessionDTO.cs:15`) and `LivePollDTO` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Shared/LivePolls/LivePollDTO.cs:8`), Store's `OrderDTO` (`MMCA.Store/Source/Modules/Sales/MMCA.Store.Sales.Shared/Orders/OrderDTO.cs:9`) and `InventoryItemDTO` (`MMCA.Store/Source/Modules/Sales/MMCA.Store.Sales.Shared/Inventory/InventoryItemDTO.cs:9`), and Helpdesk's `TicketDTO` (`MMCA.Helpdesk/Source/Modules/Tickets/MMCA.Helpdesk.Tickets.Shared/Tickets/TicketDTO.cs:10`). On the server, [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase)`.SetConcurrencyETag` writes the `ETag` response header (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/ApiControllerBase.cs:125-134`), called from [EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) (`EntityControllerBase.cs:383`) and [CrudEntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType, TCreateRequest, TUpdateRequest>](#crudentitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype-tcreaterequest-tupdaterequest) (`CrudEntityControllerBase.cs:113`). It does not test for this interface: it reads a public `byte[] RowVersion` property off the row's runtime shape (or the `rowVersion` entry of a field-projected dictionary row), cached per type (`ApiControllerBase.cs:26-30`, `:112-116`), so a shaped response keeps its tag. On the client, [EntityServiceBase<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#entityservicebasetentitydto-tidentifiertype)`.ConcurrencyTagOf` pattern-matches the same interface to build the outgoing `If-Match` value, treating an empty array as no token (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Api/EntityServiceBase.cs:193-197`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the "update requests carry no token" half is a convention this interface cannot enforce by itself; it is pinned by the shipped fitness rule `UpdateRequests_ShouldNotImplement_IConcurrencyAware` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing.Architecture/Bases/Domain/ConcurrencyConventionTestsBase.cs:14`), which each consumer app subclasses.

### ServiceHostAuthentication
> MMCA.Common.ServiceHost · `MMCA.Common.ServiceHost` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.ServiceHost/ServiceHostAuthentication.cs:6` · Level 0 · enum

- **What it is**: the two-value switch that tells the composed service host how it validates bearer tokens: `ForwardedJwtBearer = 0` (`ServiceHostAuthentication.cs:12`) for every service that receives tokens, and `TokenIssuer = 1` (`ServiceHostAuthentication.cs:18`) for the one Identity service that mints them.
- **Depends on**: nothing; it is read by [ServiceHostBuilderExtensions](#servicehostbuilderextensions) and carried on [MmcaServiceHostOptions](#mmcaservicehostoptions).
- **Concept (issuer versus relying party).** `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses authentication and token handling. A service that only consumes tokens validates them against the issuer's published signing keys (JWKS discovery against `Authentication:JwtBearer:Authority`, `ServiceHostAuthentication.cs:8-11`), while the issuer validates in process with its own `Jwt` settings and reads no authority at all (`:14-17`). The dual-fetch model itself is taught in the primer under [ADR-004](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/004-authentication-dual-fetch.html); this enum only picks which half a host runs.
- **Walkthrough**: the zero value is the forwarded mode, so a default-constructed options object is a relying party, and `MmcaServiceHostOptions.Authentication` defaults to it explicitly (`MmcaServiceHostOptions.cs:52`). `AddMmcaServiceHost` branches on it: `TokenIssuer` calls `AddCommonAuthentication`, anything else calls `AddForwardedJwtBearer` with the authority from `GetRequiredJwtAuthority()` and an audience from [JwtAudience](#jwtaudience)`.RequireConfigured` (`ServiceHostBuilderExtensions.cs:132-143`).
- **Why it's built this way**: a selector, rather than a callback, keeps the fail-closed audience check inside the package for every relying-party host, and it gives the architecture rule a literal to look for. `ArchitectureRules.ForwardedJwtBearerAudienceIsFailClosed` exempts a host file only when it names `ServiceHostAuthentication.TokenIssuer` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing.Architecture/Rules/Api/ArchitectureRules.ForwardedJwt.cs:10`, `:41`), surfaced through [ForwardedJwtAudienceTestsBase](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#forwardedjwtaudiencetestsbase).
- **Where it's used**: ADC's Identity service is the one host that selects `TokenIssuer` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:117`); ADC's Conference, Engagement and Notification services keep the default.

### ServiceHostOpenApi
> MMCA.Common.ServiceHost · `MMCA.Common.ServiceHost` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.ServiceHost/ServiceHostOpenApi.cs:10` · Level 0 · static class (internal)

- **What it is**: the internal helper that names the served OpenAPI document (`DocumentName = "v1"`, `ServiceHostOpenApi.cs:15`) and fails startup when the host never registered it.
- **Depends on**: ASP.NET Core's `IOpenApiDocumentProvider` and `IServiceProviderIsKeyedService` (external).
- **Concept**: cross-reference [OpenApiEndpointExtensions](#openapiendpointextensions) for the served document. `[Rubric §33, Developer Experience]` assesses whether a mistake is caught early with an actionable message; this type turns a silently summary-less document into a startup exception that names the missing line.
- **Walkthrough**: `EnsureHostRegisteredDocument` (`ServiceHostOpenApi.cs:24`) asks the root provider whether `IOpenApiDocumentProvider` is registered as a keyed service under `"v1"` (the registration `AddOpenApi` makes) and returns if so (`:26-30`); otherwise it throws `InvalidOperationException` telling the developer to call `builder.Services.AddOpenApi()` in the host's own `Program.cs` (`:32`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the message states the reason. The OpenAPI XML-comment generator attaches controller summaries only at an `AddOpenApi` call compiled in the host project, so the package cannot register the document on the host's behalf without losing them; it pins the version instead (see [ServiceHostBuilderExtensions](#servicehostbuilderextensions)). Versioned-API context: [ADR-046](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/046-http-api-versioning.html).
- **Where it's used**: `DocumentName` keys the OpenAPI 3.1 pin in `AddMmcaServiceHost` (`ServiceHostBuilderExtensions.cs:127-129`), and the check runs first in [ServiceHostApplicationExtensions](#servicehostapplicationextensions)`.UseMmcaServiceHostAsync` (`ServiceHostApplicationExtensions.cs:51`).

### BaseLookup<TIdentifierType>
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.DTOs` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/DTOs/BaseLookup.cs:8` · Level 1 · record class

- **What it is**: a minimal DTO for dropdown and autocomplete lookups: just `Id` and `Name`.
- **Depends on**: [IBaseDTO<TIdentifierType>](#ibasedtotidentifiertype) (Level 0), which it implements (`BaseLookup.cs:8`).
- **Concept (right-sized response shapes).** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether responses are shaped to their consumer rather than dumping full entities. Instead of returning a full entity DTO to populate a `<select>` element, the system returns `BaseLookup<T>`, carrying only the id and the display name; this cuts wire size and avoids coupling the UI to full entity shapes it does not need. Both `Id` (`BaseLookup.cs:12`) and `Name` (`BaseLookup.cs:15`) are `required`, so hand-written construction is compile-checked, and record equality gives value semantics for free.
- **Walkthrough**: the type itself is four lines, but its interesting half lives in the repository that projects into it. [EFReadRepository<TEntity, TIdentifierType>](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#efreadrepositorytentity-tidentifiertype)`.GetAllForLookupAsync` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/Repositories/EFReadRepository.cs:222`) takes the *name* of the display property and lets the database do the shaping. The selector is built once per (entity type, property name) pair into a cache (`:247-248`), binding `Id` and the named property into a `BaseLookup<TIdentifierType>` via `Expression.MemberInit` (`:262-268`). Note the consequence for `required`: an expression-tree `MemberInit` constructs the record without the compiler's required-member check, which is legal because `required` is a compile-time contract, not a runtime one. Above the repository the shape travels as a [CollectionResult<T>](group-01-result-error-handling.md#collectionresultt) through [EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype)`.GetAllForLookupAsync` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/EntityControllerBase.cs:321-336`).
- **Why it's built this way**: one shared lookup shape means the UI's generic select components bind to a single type regardless of which entity fills them, and projecting inside the SQL query (rather than materializing entities and mapping) keeps a lookup list cheap `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`; caching the compiled expression per property name keeps the reflection cost to the first call.
- **Where it's used**: returned by the lookup path at every layer: the read repository (`EFReadRepository.cs:222`), [IEntityQueryService<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](group-03-querying-specifications.md#ientityqueryservicetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype), the controller base (`EntityControllerBase.cs:321`), and the UI's [IEntityService<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#ientityservicetentitydto-tidentifiertype).

### MmcaServiceHost
> MMCA.Common.ServiceHost · `MMCA.Common.ServiceHost` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.ServiceHost/MmcaServiceHost.cs:11` · Level 4 · sealed class

- **What it is**: the handle `AddMmcaServiceHost` returns and `UseMmcaServiceHostAsync` takes back, carrying state from the registration phase to the application phase of startup.
- **Depends on**: [ModuleHostContext](#modulehostcontext) and `ILoggerFactory` (external).
- **Walkthrough**: the constructor is `internal` (`MmcaServiceHost.cs:13`), so only [ServiceHostBuilderExtensions](#servicehostbuilderextensions) can create one. `ModuleHost` (`:23`) is public: the same module host the `ConfigurePipeline` callback received (bound settings, the module loader, the discovery step). `BootstrapLoggerFactory` (`:29`) is internal: the factory the module loader's logger came from, kept alive for the life of the host and disposed on `ApplicationStopped`.
- **Why it's built this way**: the module loader keeps the logger it was given, so the factory cannot be disposed at the end of registration; returning it inside an opaque handle lets the application phase own its disposal without exposing it to the host.
- **Where it's used**: every ADC service host stores it as `var host = builder.AddMmcaServiceHost(...)` and passes it to `app.UseMmcaServiceHostAsync(host)` (for example `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:82`, `:376`).

### MmcaServiceHostOptions
> MMCA.Common.ServiceHost · `MMCA.Common.ServiceHost` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.ServiceHost/MmcaServiceHostOptions.cs:16` · Level 4 · sealed class

- **What it is**: the values and callbacks that differ between service hosts. Its defaults are the REST plus gRPC service profile: an Http2-only cleartext endpoint, forwarded JWT validation, no Redis-backed output cache (`MmcaServiceHostOptions.cs:11-15`).
- **Depends on**: [ServiceHostAuthentication](#servicehostauthentication), [MmcaApplicationPipelineBuilder](group-14-module-system-composition.md#mmcaapplicationpipelinebuilder), [ModuleHostContext](#modulehostcontext); externals `HttpProtocols`, `OutputCacheOptions`, `WebApplicationBuilder`, `IServiceCollection`, `Assembly`.
- **Concept (options object plus ordered callbacks).** `[Rubric §2, Design Patterns]` assesses deliberate pattern use: this is a Template Method expressed as data. The package owns the fixed registration order, and each `Action` property is a hook invoked at one named point in it, so a host supplies only what differs. `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` applies too: every extracted service starts the same way, which is what lets a module move out of the monolith without a hand-written startup ([ADR-059](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/059-module-contract-and-composition.html)).
- **Walkthrough**: three `required` members. `LogFilePath` (`:22`) is the rolling Serilog file, skipped in Production; `ModuleAssemblies` (`:28`) lists only the host's own module assemblies so peer modules are never loaded; `ConfigurePipeline` (`:35`) is the application-pipeline body ([ADR-014](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/014-cqrs-decorator-pipeline.html)), typically `pipeline.Register(moduleHost.RegisterModules)` plus gRPC clients and `AddBrokerMessaging`. Defaulted values: `Protocols` is `HttpProtocols.Http2` (`:42`; `Http1AndHttp2` for the [ADR-012](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/012-grpc-host-transport.html) mixed profile), `RedeclareCleartextEndpoint` is `true` (`:49`; `false` for a host that declares endpoints under `Kestrel:Endpoints`), `Authentication` is `ForwardedJwtBearer` (`:52`), `UseRedisOutputCaching` is `false` (`:55`). The optional callbacks, by the point they run: `ConfigureConfigurationSources` first of all, before any configuration is read (`:66`); `AfterServiceDefaults` right after `AddServiceDefaults()`, the place for vendor exporters and the shared DataProtection key ring (`:72`); `ConfigureOutputCache` after the always-present no-cache base policy (`:60`); `ConfigureServices` after `AddAPI` (`:78`); `RegisterBrokerTransports` immediately before the pipeline, because `AddBrokerMessaging` picks its transport while it registers (`:84`).
- **Why it's built this way**: vendor calls (Key Vault, the Azure Monitor exporter, a cloud broker transport) arrive only as callbacks, so the package stays cloud-neutral ([ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html)); the options type is `sealed` with `init`-only members, so a host cannot change it after the call.
- **Where it's used**: passed as `new() { ... }` to `AddMmcaServiceHost` by each ADC service host (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Service/Program.cs:58`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Notification.Service/Program.cs:58`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:79`).

### ServiceHostBuilderExtensions
> MMCA.Common.ServiceHost · `MMCA.Common.ServiceHost` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.ServiceHost/ServiceHostBuilderExtensions.cs:30` · Level 5 · static class

- **What it is**: the composed service-host registration, one `extension(WebApplicationBuilder)` method, `AddMmcaServiceHost(MmcaServiceHostOptions)` (`ServiceHostBuilderExtensions.cs:65`), that holds the start-up list every extracted service host would otherwise repeat, in the one order that is correct for all of them (`:20-25`).
- **Depends on**: [MmcaServiceHostOptions](#mmcaservicehostoptions), [ServiceHostAuthentication](#servicehostauthentication), [ServiceHostOpenApi](#servicehostopenapi), [MmcaServiceHost](#mmcaservicehost), [ModuleHostExtensions](#modulehostextensions), [ModuleLoader](group-14-module-system-composition.md#moduleloader), [SerilogHostExtensions](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#seriloghostextensions), [JwtAudience](#jwtaudience), [JwtAuthorityExtensions](#jwtauthorityextensions), [MiniProfilerExtensions](#miniprofilerextensions); it spans the API, Aspire, Grpc and Infrastructure packages (`:6-16`).
- **Concept**: C# `extension(T)` blocks are taught in the [primer](00-primer.md#c-extensiont-types--read-this-once). `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]` assesses composition at the edge: this is the composition root for a service host, and the only place that knows the order. The `CA1708` suppression (`:26-29`) is justified inline as an `extension(T)` false positive `[Rubric §15, Best Practices]`.
- **Walkthrough**: guards first: null builder or options, and an empty `ModuleAssemblies` throws `ArgumentException` (`:67-74`). Then, in order: `ConfigureConfigurationSources` (`:79`) so a vault value is seen by every eager bind below; Kestrel endpoints with the health-probe listener (`:81`); `AddCommonSerilog` then `AddServiceDefaults()` then `AfterServiceDefaults` (`:84-86`); `AddRedisCaching`, the Redis output cache when asked for, and the hybrid cache when Redis is configured (`:91-97`); a bootstrap logger factory and `AddModuleHost` (`:101-104`); infrastructure health checks with `requireDatabase: true` (`:106`); CORS, API versioning and rate limiting (`:108-110`); the output cache with a no-cache base policy then the host's policies (`:112-116`); response compression (`:118`); the OpenAPI 3.1 pin as named options on the `"v1"` document, then `AddCommonOpenApi()` (`:127-130`), so a bare `AddOpenApi()` made before or after still emits 3.1 on both framework lines ([ADR-141](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/141-two-framework-lines-from-one-tree.html)); token validation by `Authentication` (`:132-143`); exception handlers, `AddInfrastructure`, `AddAPI` (`:145-147`); `AddMiniProfilerIfEnabled` (`:151`); `ConfigureServices` (`:153`); `RegisterBrokerTransports` (`:156`); `AddMmcaApplicationPipeline` with the host's `ConfigurePipeline` as its body (`:158`); `AddApplicationProfiling` only when `ApplicationSettings:UseMiniProfiler` is true, after the pipeline because `TryDecorate` wraps only what is registered (`:162-165`); `AddGrpcServiceDefaults` (`:167`); and last the per-module health checks, which can only see modules discovery found (`:170`). It returns a new [MmcaServiceHost](#mmcaservicehost) (`:172`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the doc comment (`:49-59`) records two contracts. The host keeps its own `builder.Services.AddOpenApi()` line, because XML-comment summaries attach only at a call site compiled in the host project (enforced at startup by [ServiceHostOpenApi](#servicehostopenapi)). And after the call the application pipeline is sealed, so anything registered afterwards (gRPC endpoint services, a service-token client, MCP) must not register command or query handlers. Composition order and module contract: [ADR-059](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/059-module-contract-and-composition.html).
- **Where it's used**: every ADC service host (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:82`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:79`, and the Engagement and Notification hosts at `Program.cs:58`); its layer placement is pinned by Common's `LayerDependencyTests` and `CloudVendorIsolationTests`.

### ServiceHostProfiling
> MMCA.Common.ServiceHost · `MMCA.Common.ServiceHost` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.ServiceHost/ServiceHostProfiling.cs:11` · Level 11 · static class (internal)

- **What it is**: the internal helper that inserts a `"MiniProfiler"` step (`StepName`, `ServiceHostProfiling.cs:14`) into the shared middleware pipeline.
- **Depends on**: [MiddlewarePipelineBuilder](#middlewarepipelinebuilder), [MiddlewarePipelineStep](#middlewarepipelinestep), [MiddlewarePipelineStepNames](#middlewarepipelinestepnames); MiniProfiler's `UseMiniProfiler` (external).
- **Walkthrough**: `InsertMiddleware` (`:24`) calls `pipeline.InsertAfter(MiddlewarePipelineStepNames.TenantResolution, ...)` with a step that runs `app.UseMiniProfiler()` (`:25-27`). Placing it right after tenant resolution means `HttpContext.User` is populated when the profiler answers its own result routes, and the controllers, the CQRS pipeline and EF Core all run inside the profiled request (`:16-22`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the named-step builder ([ADR-079](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/079-shared-http-middleware-pipeline.html)) lets the host add an optional step at a stable position without restating the whole pipeline. Who may read the results is decided by `MiniProfilerOptions.ResultsAuthorize`, which `AddMiniProfilerIfEnabled` limits to Development (`:20-21`) `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`.
- **Where it's used**: only by [ServiceHostApplicationExtensions](#servicehostapplicationextensions) when `ApplicationSettings:UseMiniProfiler` is true (`ServiceHostApplicationExtensions.cs:57-60`).

### ServiceHostApplicationExtensions
> MMCA.Common.ServiceHost · `MMCA.Common.ServiceHost` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.ServiceHost/ServiceHostApplicationExtensions.cs:19` · Level 12 · static class

- **What it is**: the application-phase half of the composed service host: one `extension(WebApplication)` method, `UseMmcaServiceHostAsync(MmcaServiceHost, CancellationToken)` (`ServiceHostApplicationExtensions.cs:44`).
- **Depends on**: [MmcaServiceHost](#mmcaservicehost), [ServiceHostOpenApi](#servicehostopenapi), [ServiceHostProfiling](#servicehostprofiling), [DatabaseInitializationExtensions](#databaseinitializationextensions), [WebApplicationExtensions](#webapplicationextensions), [OpenApiEndpointExtensions](#openapiendpointextensions).
- **Walkthrough**: after null guards (`:46-47`), in order: the OpenAPI registration check, before the database is touched or anything mapped (`:51`); database initialization (migrate and seed, skipped only for the Development design-time OpenAPI run) so nothing is mapped against an unmigrated schema (`:54`, [ADR-030](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/030-startup-sole-migrator.html)); `MapDefaultEndpoints` (`:56`); the shared middleware pipeline, with the MiniProfiler step inserted when `UseMiniProfiler` is true (`:57-64`); `MapCommonOpenApi` (`:66`); in Development only, the gRPC reflection service marked `AllowAnonymous` for the fallback authorization policy (`:68-73`, [ADR-122](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/122-dev-only-relaxations-fail-closed.html)); and finally registering disposal of the bootstrap logger factory on `ApplicationStopped` (`:77`).
- **Why it's built this way**: it mirrors [ServiceHostBuilderExtensions](#servicehostbuilderextensions) so the two halves of startup are each one call, and every fail-fast check runs before side effects `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]`: a misconfigured host refuses to start rather than serving a degraded document or an unmigrated database.
- **Where it's used**: every ADC service host ends with `await app.UseMmcaServiceHostAsync(host)` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:376`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:316`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Service/Program.cs:248`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Notification.Service/Program.cs:145`).

### FeatureFlagLifetime
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagLifetime.cs:9` · Level 0 · enum

- **What it is**: the two-value lifetime a feature flag declares itself as, `Permanent` or `Temporary` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagLifetime.cs:9-22`).
- **Depends on**: nothing; consumed by [FeatureFlagAttribute](#featureflagattribute), [FeatureFlagDescriptor](#featureflagdescriptor) and [FeatureFlagStatusResponse](#featureflagstatusresponse).
- **Concept introduced (the flag with a stated exit).** `[Rubric §34, Architecture Governance & Documentation]` assesses whether architectural decisions are enforced by checks rather than by memory. `Permanent` is "a capability a host chooses to run with or without (push notifications, a data-subject export endpoint)" and must NOT carry a removal date (`FeatureFlagLifetime.cs:11-15`); `Temporary` "exists only until a migration, rollout or experiment finishes", must carry a removal date, and the build fails once that date has passed (`:17-21`). The enforcement itself lives on [FeatureFlagAttribute](#featureflagattribute) and the governance architecture rule that reads it.
- **Walkthrough**: two members, `Permanent` (`FeatureFlagLifetime.cs:15`) and `Temporary` (`:21`), each documented inline with the consequence it carries.
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html) is the feature-flag management scheme this lifetime enforces.
- **Where it's used**: the `Lifetime` argument every `[FeatureFlag]` attribute application supplies, across `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Shared/EngagementFeatures.cs`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared/ConferenceFeatures.cs`, `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/Notifications/NotificationFeatures.cs`, and `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/Privacy/PrivacyFeatures.cs`; read back by [FeatureFlagRegistry](#featureflagregistry)`.Describe` into each [FeatureFlagDescriptor](#featureflagdescriptor)`.Lifetime`, surfaced to operators as [FeatureFlagStatusResponse](#featureflagstatusresponse)`.Lifetime`, and checked by the governance architecture rule that fails a `Temporary` flag past its `RemoveBy` date (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing.Architecture/Rules/Governance/ArchitectureRules.FeatureFlags.cs:70`). That rule compares the lifetime by its name string (`ArchitectureRules.FeatureFlags.cs:13-14`), not through this enum, because the architecture-testing package takes no framework reference (`:7-9`).

### FeatureFlagOverrideMode
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideMode.cs:10` · Level 0 · enum

- **What it is**: what a stored, operator-set override does to a declared flag: `Off`, `On`, `Percentage`, or `Targeting` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideMode.cs:10-29`).
- **Depends on**: `System.Text.Json.Serialization.JsonStringEnumConverter<T>` (BCL), applied at `:9`.
- **Concept**: the runtime half of [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html). Where [FeatureFlagLifetime](#featureflaglifetime) describes a flag's declared life cycle, this enum describes what an operator has set it to without a new revision. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether a wire contract can evolve without breaking clients: the type doc says it is "Serialized by name, so a client reads a newer mode as an unknown string rather than a bare number" (`:6-7`), and the explicit numeric values (`= 0` to `= 3`) pin the stored column against a reorder.
- **Walkthrough**: `Off = 0` is the kill switch (`:13`); `On = 1` is on for everyone (`:16`); `Percentage = 2` is on for a stable percentage of users through the targeting filter's default rollout, "so one user keeps the same answer from request to request" (`:18-22`); `Targeting = 3` is on for named users and groups plus an optional percentage of everyone else, and groups are how a tenant-wide override is expressed (`:24-28`).
- **Why it's built this way**: each mode maps one-to-one onto a Microsoft.FeatureManagement definition in [FeatureFlagDefinitions](#featureflagdefinitions)`.ToDefinition` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagDefinitions.cs:33-46`): `On` becomes an always-on filter, `Percentage` and `Targeting` become a targeting filter, `Off` becomes a definition with no filter, and an unknown mode also evaluates to off (`:44-45`), which fails closed.
- **Where it's used**: carried by [SetFeatureFlagOverrideRequest](#setfeatureflagoverriderequest)`.Mode`, [FeatureFlagOverrideResponse](#featureflagoverrideresponse)`.Mode`, and stored on [FeatureFlagOverride](#featureflagoverride)`.Mode`, which also decides which modes carry a percentage or an audience; the admin page `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Pages/Administration/FeatureFlagAdmin.razor.cs` offers the four modes to the operator.

### FeatureFlagValueKind
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagValueKind.cs:10` · Level 0 · enum

- **What it is**: the coarse state a flag definition puts a flag in, as the administration surface reports it: `Off`, `On`, or `Conditional` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagValueKind.cs:5-20`).
- **Depends on**: `JsonStringEnumConverter<T>` (BCL), applied at `:9`; serialized by name like [FeatureFlagOverrideMode](#featureflagoverridemode).
- **Concept**: a summary, not an evaluation. A conditional flag's answer depends on the caller, so the admin surface cannot say "on" or "off" for it; this enum names that third state honestly instead of guessing. The concept of a stored override is introduced at [FeatureFlagOverrideMode](#featureflagoverridemode).
- **Walkthrough**: `Off = 0` covers "everyone, including a flag the configuration never mentions" (`:12-13`); `On = 1` is on for everyone (`:15-16`); `Conditional = 2` is "decided per evaluation by one or more feature filters" (`:18-19`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [FeatureFlagDefinitions](#featureflagdefinitions)`.Describe` is the one place that collapses a definition into this enum (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagDefinitions.cs:51-75`): a missing or disabled definition is `Off` (`:53-56`), a definition with no named filter is `Off` (`:63-66`), a definition whose filters are all always-on (or any always-on under `RequirementType.Any`) is `On` (`:68-72`), and anything else is `Conditional` with its filter names (`:74`).
- **Where it's used**: the `Configured` and `Effective` fields of [FeatureFlagStatusResponse](#featureflagstatusresponse), filled by [FeatureFlagAdministrationService](#featureflagadministrationservice) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagAdministrationService.cs:125-130`), and rendered by `FeatureFlagAdmin.razor.cs`.

### SupportedCultures
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.Globalization` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/Globalization/SupportedCultures.cs:9` · Level 0 · class (static)

- **What it is**: the framework-wide allowlist of supported UI cultures ([ADR-027](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/027-multi-locale-i18n.html)). A static class holding the default culture, the full supported set, the Development-only pseudo-localization locale, a closest-match resolver, and two membership tests.
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party. Uses only BCL types (`IReadOnlyList<string>`, `StringComparison`).
- **Concept introduced (internationalization allowlist as one source of truth).** `[Rubric §27, i18n]` assesses whether locale support is centralized, discoverable, and drift-resistant rather than scattered string checks. Every consumer that decides "is this a language we support" reads this one list: the host request-localization setup, the culture switcher, the MAUI head's culture resolution, and the domain guard on a user's preferred culture. Adding a locale means adding a `.<culture>.resx` sibling set plus one entry here, with no other infrastructure change (`SupportedCultures.cs:3-8`).
- **Walkthrough**
  - `Default = "en-US"` (`SupportedCultures.cs:12`) is the fallback used when no cookie, profile, or `Accept-Language` preference resolves.
  - `All` (`SupportedCultures.cs:18`) is the supported set, default first, and today it is exactly `[Default, "es"]`: English and Spanish. Both the request-localization options and the culture switcher iterate it.
  - `PseudoLocale = "qps-Ploc"` (`SupportedCultures.cs:28`) is the Windows-standard pseudo-localization locale, deliberately **not** part of `All` so the translation-completeness fitness gate does not demand a `.qps-Ploc.resx` sibling (`:21-23`). It is wired into request localization, the culture-switch endpoint, and the culture switcher in **Development only**, where it runtime-transforms every resolved resource string (accents, padding, bracket sentinel) to surface hard-coded strings, truncation, and string concatenation without translating anything (`:23-26`).
  - `IsSupported(string?)` (`SupportedCultures.cs:35-37`) returns true for a non-empty culture matched case-insensitively against `All`; `IsPseudoLocale(string?)` (`SupportedCultures.cs:76-77`) tests case-insensitively against `PseudoLocale`. Both take a nullable string so callers can pass an unvalidated cookie, query value, or profile field straight in.
  - `ResolveClosest(string?)` (`SupportedCultures.cs:51`) is the fallback ladder: blank returns `Default` (`:53-56`), an exact case-insensitive `All` match wins (`:58-62`), otherwise the language subtag is matched, so `"es-MX"` resolves to `"es"` (`:64-66`), and anything left over falls to `Default` (`:68`). The subtag split is done by the private `LanguageOf` (`:83-87`), which returns the input unchanged when there is no `-`, avoiding an allocation for an already-neutral culture. Because `PseudoLocale` is not in `All`, this method can never return it (stated at `:42`).
- **Why it's built this way**: a single `const` plus `IReadOnlyList` allowlist keeps the localization middleware, the switcher UI, the fitness gate, and the domain guard from drifting apart; separating `PseudoLocale` from `All` lets a diagnostic locale ship in Development without polluting the production culture set or the resx-completeness gate. `ResolveClosest` exists because web heads get language-level fallback for free from request localization's `Accept-Language` matching, while a head with no request pipeline (the MAUI Blazor Hybrid, which resolves against the device locale) has to do it itself, and the doc comment (`SupportedCultures.cs:43-48`) says explicitly that the point is to keep the two paths from diverging.
- **Where it's used**: [WebApplicationExtensions](#webapplicationextensions)`.UseCommonRequestLocalization` builds the supported list from `All` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Startup/WebApplicationExtensions.cs:76`), appends `PseudoLocale` only in Development (`:80`), and sets `Default` as the default culture (`:85`); the culture-switch endpoint validates the incoming value with `IsSupported` or, when pseudo is allowed, `IsPseudoLocale` (`:107`). [MmcaCultureBootstrap](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#mmcaculturebootstrap) falls back to `Default` when the stored culture is not supported (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Culture/MmcaCultureBootstrap.cs:30`), and [PseudoStringLocalizer](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#pseudostringlocalizer) activates only under `IsPseudoLocale` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Globalization/PseudoStringLocalizer.cs:17`). The MAUI head is the `ResolveClosest` caller: [MauiCultureStore](group-26-device-capability-layer.md#mauiculturestore) resolves the device culture when nothing is stored (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI.Maui/Globalization/MauiCultureStore.cs:41-43`), and [MauiCultureApplier](group-26-device-capability-layer.md#mauicultureapplier) refuses an unsupported culture outright (`.../MauiCultureApplier.cs:32`). The domain guard is [CommonInvariants](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#commoninvariants)`.EnsurePreferredCultureIsValid`, which allows `null` or an `IsSupported` culture (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Domain/Invariants/CommonInvariants.cs:144`).

### FeatureFlagAttribute
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagAttribute.cs:32` · Level 1 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the attribute a `*Features` constant class applies to each flag field, carrying a [FeatureFlagLifetime](#featureflaglifetime), an optional `RemoveBy` date, and an optional `Owner` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagAttribute.cs:32-67`).
- **Depends on**: [FeatureFlagLifetime](#featureflaglifetime); `System.Attribute`, `System.DateOnly`, `System.Globalization.CultureInfo` (BCL).
- **Concept introduced (self-describing metadata a reflection scan can enforce).** `[Rubric §34, Architecture Governance & Documentation]` assesses whether a "temporary" label is actually checkable. `RemoveBy` is a `string?` rather than a `DateOnly?` because "an attribute argument must be a compile-time constant" (`FeatureFlagAttribute.cs:44`), so `TryParseRemoveBy` (`:60-66`) is what turns the constant string back into a real date for the check, parsed strictly against `RemoveByFormat = "yyyy-MM-dd"` (`:35,63`). `Owner` exists purely for the failure message: "Carried into the failure message, so an expired flag names someone" (`:50`), which lets the governance architecture rule point at a person rather than an anonymous build break.
- **Walkthrough**: a primary constructor takes `lifetime` (`:32`), stored read-only as `Lifetime` (`:38`). `RemoveBy` and `Owner` are `init`-only properties (`:46,52`), settable only as named attribute arguments. `TryParseRemoveBy` is the one static method: a thin wrapper over `DateOnly.TryParseExact` with `DateTimeStyles.None` (`:60-66`), so a malformed date fails to parse rather than being silently coerced.
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html) is the flag life cycle this attribute encodes: pairing every flag with an explicit permanent or temporary choice and, for temporary ones, a checkable expiry.
- **Where it's used**: applied to every flag field in the `*Features` constant classes; read by [FeatureFlagRegistry](#featureflagregistry)`.Describe` via `GetCustomAttribute<FeatureFlagAttribute>` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagRegistry.cs:102`). The governance architecture rules `FeatureFlagsDeclareLifetime` and `TemporaryFeatureFlagsAreNotPastRemoveBy` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing.Architecture/Rules/Governance/ArchitectureRules.FeatureFlags.cs:28,70`) do not reference this type: they match it by its full name string (`:10`) and read its properties reflectively (`:95-98`), keeping their own copy of `RemoveByFormat` (`:12`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: because the governance rule duplicates the format and lifetime names as strings (`ArchitectureRules.FeatureFlags.cs:12-14`), a rename here is not a compile error there.

### FeatureFlagDescriptor
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagRegistry.cs:17` · Level 1 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: one row of [FeatureFlagRegistry](#featureflagregistry)`.Describe`'s output: a flag's field name, wire name, and the [FeatureFlagLifetime](#featureflaglifetime), `RemoveBy` and `Owner` its `[FeatureFlag]` attribute carries, if any (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagRegistry.cs:17-22`).
- **Depends on**: [FeatureFlagLifetime](#featureflaglifetime).
- **Concept**: `Lifetime`, `RemoveBy`, and `Owner` are all nullable (`:20-22`) because a flag field compiles without the `[FeatureFlag]` attribute at all: the descriptor still reports the field and its constant value, just with no life-cycle metadata attached. The governance rule is what turns a missing attribute into a build failure; the descriptor itself stays uniform either way.
- **Walkthrough**: five positional parameters and no body (`FeatureFlagRegistry.cs:17-22`): `FieldName` (the CLR field), `FlagName` (the constant's string value), then the three attribute-sourced, nullable fields.
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html) is the scheme this record inventories.
- **Where it's used**: produced by [FeatureFlagRegistry](#featureflagregistry)`.Describe`, one per flag field; cached by [FeatureFlagCatalog](#featureflagcatalog) as the host's declared flag list, de-duplicated case-insensitively by `FlagName` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagCatalog.cs:19-25`) and looked up by `TryFind` (`:31`); projected into a [FeatureFlagStatusResponse](#featureflagstatusresponse) by [FeatureFlagAdministrationService](#featureflagadministrationservice) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagAdministrationService.cs:123-140`).

### FeatureFlagOverrideResponse
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideResponse.cs:14` · Level 1 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: one stored feature-flag override "as the administration surface reports it" (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideResponse.cs:3-5`): the read-side shape of a [FeatureFlagOverride](#featureflagoverride).
- **Depends on**: [FeatureFlagOverrideMode](#featureflagoverridemode); the `UserIdentifierType` alias for `ChangedBy` (`:22`).
- **Concept**: a contract DTO in the Shared layer, so the API, the Blazor admin page and the infrastructure snapshot share one shape (manual mapping per [ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html)). It doubles as the in-memory form the flag evaluator reads: [FeatureFlagDefinitions](#featureflagdefinitions)`.ToDefinition` takes this record, not the entity (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagDefinitions.cs:33`).
- **Walkthrough**: eight positional parameters (`:14-22`): `Name`, `Mode`, `Percentage` (only when the mode carries one, `:8`), `Users` and `Groups` (empty unless the mode is targeting, `:9-10`), `Reason`, then the audit pair `ChangedOn` (UTC instant of the last write, `:12`) and `ChangedBy` (the last writer or the system sentinel, `:13`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the audit pair is flattened from the entity's audit stamps by [EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore](#effeatureflagoverridestore)`.ToResponse`, taking the last-modified stamp and falling back to the created one (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/FeatureFlags/EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore.cs:126-135`), so a client sees who last touched the override without reading audit history.
- **Where it's used**: returned by [IFeatureFlagOverrideStore](#ifeatureflagoverridestore) and [EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore](#effeatureflagoverridestore), held by [IFeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot](#ifeatureflagoverridesnapshot) / [FeatureFlagOverrideSnapshot](#featureflagoverridesnapshot), turned into a feature definition by [StoredOverrideFeatureDefinitionProvider](#storedoverridefeaturedefinitionprovider), and nested as [FeatureFlagStatusResponse](#featureflagstatusresponse)`.Override`.

### SetFeatureFlagOverrideRequest
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/SetFeatureFlagOverrideRequest.cs:18` · Level 1 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: the body of `PUT {flags}/{name}`, the override to store for one declared flag (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/SetFeatureFlagOverrideRequest.cs:3-5,18-23`).
- **Depends on**: [FeatureFlagOverrideMode](#featureflagoverridemode).
- **Concept**: the required `Reason` is the design point. "An override is deliberately not time-boxed, so its reason is what tells the next operator whether it can be cleared" (`:7-10`): where a [FeatureFlagLifetime](#featureflaglifetime)`.Temporary` flag gets a build-enforced expiry, a runtime override gets a mandatory explanation instead.
- **Walkthrough**: `Mode` and `Reason` are required positional parameters (`:19-20`); `Percentage` (`:21`), `Users` (`:22`) and `Groups` (`:23`) default to `null`. `Percentage` is 0 to 100, required for `Percentage` mode, the share of everyone outside the named audience for `Targeting`, and ignored otherwise (`:11-15`). The record itself validates nothing: the rules are enforced by [FeatureFlagOverride](#featureflagoverride)`.Create` and `.Change`.
- **Where it's used**: bound from the body of [FeatureFlagsControllerBase](#featureflagscontrollerbase)`.SetOverrideAsync` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Administration/FeatureFlagsControllerBase.cs:68-76`), passed through [IFeatureFlagAdministrationService](#ifeatureflagadministrationservice) to [EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore](#effeatureflagoverridestore), which spreads its fields into `FeatureFlagOverride.Create` or `existing.Change` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/FeatureFlags/EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore.cs:75-76,87`); the Blazor admin page sends it through `FeatureFlagAdminService`.

### FeatureFlagRegistry
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagRegistry.cs:35` · Level 2 · class (static)

- **What it is**: the reflection scanner that turns a set of `*Features` constant classes into a flat, ordered list of [FeatureFlagDescriptor](#featureflagdescriptor) rows, given the assemblies to scan (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagRegistry.cs:35-124`).
- **Depends on**: [FeatureFlagDescriptor](#featureflagdescriptor), [FeatureFlagAttribute](#featureflagattribute); `System.Reflection` (BCL).
- **Concept introduced (the flag inventory built entirely by convention).** `[Rubric §34, Architecture Governance & Documentation]` assesses whether the set of flags in play is discoverable rather than tribal knowledge. A `*Features` class is recognized purely by shape: `IsFeatureClass` requires a `static, abstract, sealed` type (the C# pattern for a static class) whose name ends in `FeatureClassSuffix = "Features"` (`:38,77,81-82`); `FlagFields` then takes only its `public const string` fields (`:91,95-97`). No registration call, no central list to keep in sync: adding a flag is adding a `const string` field to the module's `*Features` class.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - `Describe(IEnumerable<Assembly>)` (`:46`) scans every loadable type across the given assemblies, filters to feature classes, flattens to their flag fields, maps each to a descriptor, and orders the result by `FlagName` with `StringComparer.Ordinal` (`:50-55`) so the inventory is deterministic regardless of assembly load order.
  - `Describe(Assembly)` (`:64`) is the single-assembly overload, delegating to the collection one (`:68`).
  - `LoadableTypes` (`:112`) guards `Assembly.GetTypes()` against `ReflectionTypeLoadException`, falling back to the subset of types that did load via `ex.Types.OfType<Type>()` (`:118-121`), so one broken type in an assembly does not blank out every flag in it.
  - The private `Describe(FieldInfo)` (`:100`) reads the optional [FeatureFlagAttribute](#featureflagattribute) with `inherit: false` (`:102`) and the field's raw constant value via `GetRawConstantValue()`, defaulting to an empty string (`:106`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html) is the convention this registry executes; scanning by name suffix rather than a marker interface keeps a `*Features` class a plain constant holder with no base type or registration boilerplate.
- **Where it's used**: [FeatureFlagCatalog](#featureflagcatalog) calls `Describe` lazily over every assembly the host passed to `AddReloadableFeatureFlags` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagCatalog.cs:11-22`), which is what limits stored overrides to declared flags; `MMCA.Common/Tests/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared.Tests/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagRegistryTests.cs` exercises `Describe`, `IsFeatureClass`, and `FlagFields` directly.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the governance architecture rule does not call this registry. It re-implements the same shape test and field filter over the `Shared` layer of the architecture map (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing.Architecture/Rules/Governance/ArchitectureRules.FeatureFlags.cs:87-93`) with its own `FeatureClassSuffix` constant (`:11`), because that package takes no framework reference (`:7-9`); the two copies must be kept in step by hand. `FeatureClassSuffix` is a public constant (`"Features"`), but nothing enforces that every constant class ending in that suffix is meant to hold flags: a coincidentally named class would be scanned as one.

### FeatureFlagStatusResponse
> MMCA.Common.Shared · `MMCA.Common.Shared.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagStatusResponse.cs:15` · Level 2 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: one declared flag as the administration surface lists it: "what it is, what configuration says, what (if anything) a stored override says, and which of the two is in force" (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagStatusResponse.cs:3-6`).
- **Depends on**: [FeatureFlagLifetime](#featureflaglifetime), [FeatureFlagValueKind](#featureflagvaluekind), [FeatureFlagOverrideResponse](#featureflagoverrideresponse).
- **Concept**: `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` assesses whether an operator can see the state a system is actually in. Reporting `Configured` and `Effective` side by side, with the `Override` between them, answers "why is this flag on" in one row instead of leaving the operator to diff configuration against the database.
- **Walkthrough**: eight positional parameters (`:15-23`). `Name`, `Lifetime`, `Owner` and `RemoveBy` (`:16-19`) are the declared metadata, `null` when undeclared (`:8-10`); `Configured` and `ConfiguredFilters` (`:20-21`) are configuration's state and, when conditional, its filter names; `Override` (`:22`) is `null` when configuration is in force; `Effective` (`:23`) is the override's state when there is one, otherwise configuration's (`:14`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [FeatureFlagAdministrationService](#featureflagadministrationservice)`.DescribeAsync` builds it from a [FeatureFlagDescriptor](#featureflagdescriptor) plus the stored override: `Configured` comes from the configuration provider's definition, and `Effective` reuses the same [FeatureFlagDefinitions](#featureflagdefinitions)`.Describe` over the override's definition, so both columns are summarized by one rule (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagAdministrationService.cs:123-140`).
- **Where it's used**: returned by [FeatureFlagsControllerBase](#featureflagscontrollerbase)`.GetAllAsync` and `.SetOverrideAsync` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Administration/FeatureFlagsControllerBase.cs:53,74`), declared on [IFeatureFlagAdministrationService](#ifeatureflagadministrationservice), and consumed by the Blazor `FeatureFlagAdminService` and `FeatureFlagAdmin.razor.cs` page in `MMCA.Common.UI`.

### FeatureFlagOverride
> MMCA.Common.Domain · `MMCA.Common.Domain.FeatureFlags` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Domain/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverride.cs:24` · Level 4 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: one stored override of a declared feature flag, "the data half of a reloadable flag", laid over configuration so an operator can flip a kill switch without a new revision (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Domain/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverride.cs:7-10`).
- **Depends on**: [AuditableBaseEntity<TIdentifierType>](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#auditablebaseentitytidentifiertype) keyed by `string` (`:24`), [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error), [FeatureFlagOverrideMode](#featureflagoverridemode).
- **Concept introduced (the flag name is the identity, and clearing is a soft delete).** `[Rubric §4, DDD]` assesses whether an entity guards its own invariants. There is at most one override per flag in the owning service's database, so `Name => Id` (`:49`); clearing soft-deletes the row so the audit stamps keep who cleared it, and setting the flag again revives the same row (`:12-15`). Deriving from the auditable base means every write is stamped and carries the row-version token; the required `Reason` "is the half of the audit no interceptor can supply" (`:17-21`). `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: the kill switch is data, so it moves without a deployment.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - Limits: `NameMaxLength = 128` (`:27`), `ReasonMaxLength = 500` (`:30`), `TargetMaxLength = 128` (`:33`), `TargetListMaxLength = 4000` (`:36`), `MaxPercentage = 100` (`:39`), and `TargetSeparator = ','` (`:42`), which is also refused inside a name.
  - State: `Mode`, `Percentage`, `TargetUsers`, `TargetGroups` and `Reason` have private setters (`:52-64`); the target lists are stored as one joined string, and `Users` / `Groups` split them back on read (`:67,70`, `Split` at `:136-137`).
  - `Create` (`:82`) refuses a blank or over-long trimmed name (`:90-96`), constructs the entity with the trimmed name as `Id` (`:98`), and runs `Apply` (`:99`), returning its failure or the entity.
  - `Change` (`:116`) runs the same `Apply`, then revives a cleared override with `Undelete()` (`:124-129`). `Clear` (`:134`) is `Delete()`.
  - `Refusal` (`:152`) checks the scalar half in order: an undefined mode (`:154`), a blank or over-long reason (`:162`), then `PercentageFits` (`:146-149`), which requires 0 to 100 for `Percentage`, allows a null or 0 to 100 for `Targeting`, and ignores it otherwise.
  - `Audience` (`:179`) applies only to `Targeting` and requires at least one user or group (`:193-198`); `NormalizeTargets` (`:205`) drops blanks, trims, de-duplicates ordinally (`:210`), and refuses an over-long list, an over-long name, or a name containing the separator (`:214-215`).
  - `Apply` (`:230`) validates everything first (`:237-248`) and only then writes (`:250-254`), so "a refused change never leaves the override half-updated" (`:226-228`); a percentage is kept only for a mode that carries one (`:251`).
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping the override as an ordinary auditable entity (rather than a configuration file write) gives it the database-per-service home, the audit trail, and the concurrency token every aggregate already has. [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html) is the feature-flag scheme it extends.
- **Where it's used**: mapped by [FeatureFlagOverrideModelBuilderExtensions](#featureflagoverridemodelbuilderextensions) into each owning service's database (ADC ships an `AddFeatureFlagOverrides` migration in both its Conference and Engagement migration projects, for example `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosting/MMCA.ADC.Migrations.SqlServer.Conference/Migrations/20261108145459_AddFeatureFlagOverrides.Designer.cs`); created, changed and cleared by [EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore](#effeatureflagoverridestore) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/FeatureFlags/EFFeatureFlagOverrideStore.cs:75,87,116`) and projected to [FeatureFlagOverrideResponse](#featureflagoverrideresponse) there (`:126`). Unit coverage is `MMCA.Common/Tests/Core/MMCA.Common.Domain.Tests/FeatureFlags/FeatureFlagOverrideTests.cs`.

### IEntityDTOMapper<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>
> MMCA.Common.Application · `MMCA.Common.Application.Interfaces.Mapping` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Interfaces/Mapping/IEntityDTOMapper.cs:14` · Level 4 · interface
