- **What it is**: the contract for mapping a domain entity to its DTO. One required method, `MapToDTO(entity)`, plus a default `MapToDTOs(collection)` that fans the single map over a collection.
- **Depends on**: [AuditableBaseEntity<TIdentifierType>](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#auditablebaseentitytidentifiertype) and [IBaseDTO<TIdentifierType>](#ibasedtotidentifiertype) as generic constraints (`IEntityDTOMapper.cs:15-17`), and the file's usings pull in the Domain entities and the Shared DTO namespace (`:1-3`).
- **Concept introduced (manual DTO mapping as a first-class contract).** `[Rubric §15, Best Practices & Code Quality]` assesses whether a change is caught where it happens: [ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html) chose hand-written mappers over a convention-based mapping library precisely so that adding a DTO property that nobody maps is a **compile error**, not a silently null field at runtime, and so that "where is this field filled in" is a single go-to-definition. `[Rubric §1, SOLID]` shows up twice: the interface has one job (ISP), and every generic read path depends on this abstraction rather than on any concrete mapper (DIP). `[Rubric §2, Design Patterns]`: the C# **interface default method** at `:27-32` is the code-sharing mechanism, so a concrete mapper inherits collection mapping for free and overrides it only when batch mapping needs something smarter.
- **Walkthrough**
  - Three generic parameters with matched constraints (`:15-17`): `TEntity : AuditableBaseEntity<TIdentifierType>`, `TEntityDTO : IBaseDTO<TIdentifierType>`, `TIdentifierType : notnull`. The shared `TIdentifierType` is what makes a mismatched entity/DTO pair fail to compile.
  - `TEntityDTO MapToDTO(TEntity entity)` (`:22`) is the single member an implementer must write; it is synchronous, because mapping is a pure in-memory shape change.
  - `IReadOnlyCollection<TEntityDTO> MapToDTOs(IReadOnlyCollection<TEntity> entityCollection)` (`:27`) is the default implementation: guard (`:29`), then `[.. entityCollection.Select(MapToDTO)]` (`:31`), a collection expression that materializes once into a read-only collection.
- **Why it's built this way**: see [ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html). The default method is what keeps the hand-written cost to one method per entity: without it, every mapper would repeat the same `Select`. Registration is by convention rather than by hand: the module registration scans each module assembly for implementations and registers them `AsSelfWithInterfaces` with a scoped lifetime (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/DependencyInjection.ModuleScanning.cs:65-69`), so a new mapper class is wired by existing.
- **Where it's used**: it is a constructor dependency of the whole generic write and read stack: [EntityQueryService<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](group-03-querying-specifications.md#entityqueryservicetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) takes one and re-exposes it as its `DTOMapper` property (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/EntityQueryService.cs:36`, `:90`, declared on the interface at `Interfaces/IEntityQueryService.cs:25`), and it is injected into [CreateEntityHandlerBase<TCreateRequest, TEntity, TIdentifierType, TEntityDTO>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#createentityhandlerbasetcreaterequest-tentity-tidentifiertype-tentitydto) (`UseCases/CreateEntityHandlerBase.cs:44`), [UpdateEntityHandler<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType, TUpdateRequest>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#updateentityhandlertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype-tupdaterequest) (`UseCases/UpdateEntityHandler.cs:51`), and [MutateEntityHandlerBase<TCommand, TEntity, TIdentifierType>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#mutateentityhandlerbasetcommand-tentity-tidentifiertype) (`UseCases/MutateEntityHandlerBase.cs:344`). Implementations are one per entity in every app (for example `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sponsors/DTOs/SponsorDTOMapper.cs:14`), plus the framework's own [PushNotificationDTOMapper](group-10-notifications.md#pushnotificationdtomapper) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Notifications/PushNotifications/DTOs/PushNotificationDTOMapper.cs:13`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: mapping is not always the read path. An entity may also have an opt-in [IEntityDTOProjector<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#ientitydtoprojectortentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype), scanned beside the mappers (`DependencyInjection.ModuleScanning.cs:74-78`), which pushes the same shaping into SQL for list reads; an entity with no projector keeps materialize-then-map through this interface.

### IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>
> MMCA.Common.Application · `MMCA.Common.Application.Interfaces.Mapping` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Interfaces/Mapping/IEntityDTOMapper.cs:42` · Level 4 · interface

- **What it is**: the create-side mapper. It turns an incoming create request into a new domain entity by calling that entity's factory method, and returns a [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) so a refused invariant travels back as a failure rather than an exception.
- **Depends on**: [AuditableBaseEntity<TIdentifierType>](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#auditablebaseentitytidentifiertype), the [ICreateRequest](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icreaterequest) marker, and [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) (`IEntityDTOMapper.cs:43-45`, `:54`). It shares a file with [IEntityDTOMapper<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype), because the file covers both mapping directions.
- **Concept (the entity factory is the only construction path).** The manual-mapping rationale is introduced at [IEntityDTOMapper](#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype); what this interface adds is a boundary rule. `[Rubric §4, DDD]` assesses whether aggregates are constructed through guarded factories that enforce invariants: the mapper does not `new` an entity, it calls `Entity.Create(...)` and hands the resulting `Result<TEntity>` straight back, so no code path can produce an invalid aggregate. `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: separating request-to-entity creation from entity-to-DTO reading keeps each mapper single-purpose. The signature is `Task<Result<TEntity>>` (`:54`) even though most implementations are synchronous, because creation may need a database round trip first (a uniqueness check, for example, as the doc says at `:37`).
- **Walkthrough**: three constrained generic parameters (`:43-45`), with `TCreateRequest : ICreateRequest` as the type-system guard that stops an update request being passed to the create path. The single member is `Task<Result<TEntity>> CreateEntityAsync(TCreateRequest request, CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)` (`:54`). A representative implementation is one method: guard the request, then `Task.FromResult(Sponsor.Create(...))` with the request's fields spread into the factory (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sponsors/UseCases/Create/SponsorCreateRequestMapper.cs:15-31`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html) again. Because the mapper is a named contract rather than inline handler code, the framework can ship one generic create handler for every aggregate: [CreateEntityHandlerBase](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#createentityhandlerbasetcreaterequest-tentity-tidentifiertype-tentitydto) injects it (`UseCases/CreateEntityHandlerBase.cs:43`) and awaits `requestMapper.CreateEntityAsync(request, cancellationToken)`, short-circuiting on failure (`:89-90`), while the module supplies only the per-entity mapping. Registration is the same Scrutor scan as the DTO mappers, `AsSelfWithInterfaces` and scoped (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/DependencyInjection.ModuleScanning.cs:80-84`).
- **Where it's used**: implemented once per creatable aggregate across the apps (for example `SponsorCreateRequestMapper.cs:11`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Questions/UseCases/Create/QuestionCreateRequestMapper.cs:12`), and consumed by the generic create handlers (`UseCases/CreateEntityHandlerBase.cs:43`, `UseCases/CreateEntityHandler.cs:30`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: this interface has a write-side twin declared in the same file, [IEntityUpdateApplier<TEntity, TUpdateRequest, TIdentifierType>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#ientityupdateappliertentity-tupdaterequest-tidentifiertype) (`IEntityDTOMapper.cs:79-91`), which applies a request onto an already-loaded aggregate and returns a bare `Result` because the instance handed in is the tracked one (`:70-74`). Do not reach for the create mapper on the update path.

### CsvWriter
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Export` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Export/CsvWriter.cs:44` · Level 5 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: a hand-written, minimal RFC 4180 CSV writer. It writes into a caller-supplied `TextWriter` one record at a time, so the generic `/export` endpoint can stream a file straight to the response body without ever holding the whole result set in memory.
- **Depends on**: [Money](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#money) and [Currency](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#currency) for their value-form cells. From the BCL: `TextWriter`, `SearchValues<char>` (`System.Buffers`), `UTF8Encoding` (`System.Text`), `ConcurrentDictionary`, `System.Text.Json` (`JsonSerializer`, `JsonSerializerOptions.Web`), reflection, and `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` (`System.Globalization`).
- **Concept introduced: a framework declines dependencies its consumers cannot decline.** `[Rubric §32, Dependency & Supply-Chain]` assesses what a package drags into every downstream application; because MMCA.Common ships under lockstep versioning, a CsvHelper reference here would become a pin in ADC, Store, and Helpdesk that none of them chose. The type doc makes the trade explicit (`CsvWriter.cs:19-24`): the framework needs exactly three behaviors (quote when required, escape embedded quotes, terminate with CRLF), and that is a page of code with full in-repo test coverage. `[Rubric §15, Best Practices & Code Quality]`: every formatting decision is invariant, so the same row produces the same bytes on every machine (`CsvWriter.cs:25-36`). See [ADR-078](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/078-csv-export-endpoint.html).
- **Walkthrough**
  - `Utf8ByteOrderMark` (`CsvWriter.cs:55`) and `LineEnding` (`CsvWriter.cs:58`, the literal `"\r\n"` regardless of host OS) are the two format constants. `Utf8NoPreamble` (`CsvWriter.cs:65`) is a `UTF8Encoding` constructed with `encoderShouldEmitUTF8Identifier: false`, so the `StreamWriter` an export runs through emits no preamble of its own and the BOM decision lives in exactly one place.
  - `MustQuote` (`CsvWriter.cs:71`) is a `SearchValues<char>` over `,"\r\n`: the four characters RFC 4180 section 2 says force quoting. `SearchValues` is the vectorized-lookup type, so the per-field scan is a span search rather than four `IndexOf` passes (`[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`). `NoValueTextTypes` (`CsvWriter.cs:74`) is a `ConcurrentDictionary<Type, bool>` caching, per type, whether `ToString` is debugging text, so reflection runs once per type.
  - `WriteByteOrderMark(writer)` (`CsvWriter.cs:82`) writes the BOM unconditionally. The remarks say why (`CsvWriter.cs:49-54`): Excel reads a BOM-less UTF-8 CSV in the machine's ANSI code page, turning every accented character into mojibake, and three bytes is cheaper than a setting nobody finds in time.
  - `WriteHeader(columns, writer)` (`CsvWriter.cs:94`) and `WriteRow(cells, writer)` (`CsvWriter.cs:118`) are the same loop: a comma between fields (`CsvWriter.cs:101-104` and `CsvWriter.cs:125-128`), then `LineEnding` (`CsvWriter.cs:109` and `CsvWriter.cs:133`). The header escapes a column name exactly like a data field (`CsvWriter.cs:106`), so a column named `full,name` cannot break the record.
  - `FormatCell(value)` (`CsvWriter.cs:141-150`) is the type switch that fixes the value contract: null writes empty, `string` writes verbatim, `bool` writes lowercase `true`/`false` to match the JSON the sibling endpoints emit rather than .NET's capitalized `ToString`, `DateTime` and `DateTimeOffset` write ISO 8601 round-trip `"O"` (chosen over the sortable `"s"` because `"O"` keeps sub-second precision and the offset, so a parsed value equals the exported one), and anything else `IFormattable` formats invariantly. Everything left falls through to `FormatObject`.
  - `FormatObject(value)` (`CsvWriter.cs:159-165`) handles the non-primitive, non-formattable values: `Money` goes to `FormatMoney`, `Currency` writes its code, a type for which `HasNoValueText` is true writes compact JSON via `JsonSerializer.Serialize(..., JsonSerializerOptions.Web)` (the same shape the sibling JSON endpoints return), and the last fallback is `Convert.ToString` with the invariant culture.
  - `FormatMoney(money)` (`CsvWriter.cs:173-177`) writes the invariant amount followed by the ISO 4217 code (`"49.99 USD"`), or the amount alone when the currency code is empty (the zero sentinel).
  - `HasNoValueText(type)` (`CsvWriter.cs:187-195`) decides, once per type, that `ToString` is not a value: the public parameterless `ToString` is missing, declared on `object` or `ValueType`, or marked `CompilerGenerated` (the record's `Name { Member = ... }` form).
  - `WriteField(field, writer)` (`CsvWriter.cs:203`) is the only quoting member: a clean field is written as-is (`CsvWriter.cs:205-209`), and a field containing any `MustQuote` character is wrapped in quotes with embedded quotes doubled (`CsvWriter.cs:211-213`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the writer takes a `TextWriter` rather than returning a string, because the whole point of the export endpoint is that no full result set exists in memory at any moment. Keeping the type `internal static` means it is a framework implementation detail, not a public surface a consumer can bind to and then be broken by. Rendering money and records as values (and not as `Money { ... }` debugging text) keeps a cell something a reader can use.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the writer deliberately does **not** neutralize spreadsheet formula injection. A cell whose value opens with `=`, `+`, `-`, or `@` is written verbatim, and the type doc records the reasoning (`CsvWriter.cs:37-42`): CSV is treated as a data-faithful format here and prefixing would corrupt legitimate negative numbers, so a host that opens untrusted exports in a spreadsheet is expected to import them as text. [ADR-078](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/078-csv-export-endpoint.html) records the same decision in the same direction, and names the price (a known spreadsheet risk carried by every consumer).
- **Where it's used**: only by [EntityCsvExporter<TEntityDTO>](#entitycsvexportertentitydto)`.WriteAsync`, which takes the encoding at `EntityCsvExporter.cs:135`, writes the BOM and header at `EntityCsvExporter.cs:161-162`, and each data row via `WritePage` (`EntityCsvExporter.cs:272`), reached in turn by [EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype)`.ExportAsync` (`EntityControllerBase.cs:280`).

### EntityCsvExporter<TEntityDTO>
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Export` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Export/EntityCsvExporter.cs:17` · Level 6 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: the page-looping CSV export engine [EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype)`.ExportAsync` delegates to: it validates a `fields=` request, walks the paged query service page by page, and streams RFC 4180 CSV to a caller-supplied stream. Closed over the DTO type alone (`EntityCsvExporter.cs:17`), so its exclusion lists and column resolution are computed once per DTO shape without needing the entity or identifier type at all.
- **Depends on**: [CsvWriter](#csvwriter) (byte order mark, header, rows), [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error), [PagedCollectionResult<T>](group-01-result-error-handling.md#pagedcollectionresultt), [PaginationMetadata](group-01-result-error-handling.md#paginationmetadata) (`TotalItemCount`, the last-page and cap-boundary arbiter), [QueryFieldService](group-03-querying-specifications.md#queryfieldservice) (`ShapeData`, the shaped-row fallback), `System.Reflection`, `System.Text.Json` (`JsonNamingPolicy.CamelCase`), and `System.Globalization`.
- **Concept introduced: extracting a generic algorithm out of the controller that only invokes it.** `[Rubric §1, SOLID]` covers Single Responsibility: the page loop, the column resolution, and the exclusion lists are export mechanics independent of HTTP, so hoisting them out of `EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>` lets `ExportAsync` shrink to validate, delegate, and translate the result (class doc, `EntityCsvExporter.cs:10-14`). `[Rubric §15, Best Practices & Code Quality]`: `WriteAsync` takes the fetch as a delegate (`Func<int, CancellationToken, Task<Result<PagedCollectionResult<object>>>>`) rather than the query service itself, so the exporter carries no dependency on `IEntityQueryService<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>` and could stream any paged source a caller adapts to that shape.
- **Walkthrough**
  - The three exclusion sets, `UnexportablePropertyNames` (`EntityCsvExporter.cs:31-37`), `UnexportableColumns` (`:43-44`), and `UnexportableFields` (`:50-51`), are `static readonly` fields computed once per closed `EntityCsvExporter<TEntityDTO>`, since a DTO's shape cannot change at runtime: the property names that cannot render a faithful scalar CSV cell (binary concurrency tokens and every collection type except `string`, `IsExportableType` at `:238`), their camelCase column-name form, and a case-insensitive lookup set for `fields=` matching. Value objects and other class-typed properties are deliberately not dropped: `CsvWriter` renders `Money` as amount plus code, `Currency` as its code, and any other record as compact JSON (remarks, `:26-29`).
  - `ValidateFields(fields)` (`:61`) rejects a `fields=` request naming one of those properties as an `Error.InvalidEntityField` failure (`:70`), before a single byte of the body is written; an empty exclusion set short-circuits to `null`.
  - `WriteAsync(body, fetchPageAsync, pageSize, maxExportRows, fields, beginResponse, onFailureAfterStart, cancellationToken)` (`:116`) is the page loop. The CSV writer is synchronous, so it never writes to `body` directly: it renders into an in-memory `MemoryStream` (`:132`) through a `StreamWriter` (`:135`), and `DrainAsync` (`:209`) copies each page to the body with awaited `WriteAsync`/`FlushAsync` and empties the buffer. The type doc gives the reason: a `StreamWriter` over the body itself would flush synchronously whenever its buffer filled, which Kestrel rejects, and any export over about 1 KB aborted. Each fetched page is unwrapped with `result.TryGetValue(out var page)` (`:142`), so the failure branch and the page variable come from one test. Nothing reaches the body before the first page succeeds, so a first-page failure still returns `Result.Failure` with nothing written (`:145`) for the caller to turn into Problem Details. On the first successful page it resolves columns, calls `beginResponse()` (`:160`), and writes the BOM and header (`:161-162`); each page is written by `WritePage` (`:166`) and drained (`:169`). Three termination conditions follow: a page that stopped mid-page marks the export truncated (`:172-176`), `IsLastPage` ends it on the last page (`:178-179`), and a cap landing exactly on a page boundary consults `PaginationMetadata.TotalItemCount` rather than issuing a wasted page (`:183-187`). A mid-stream failure after the header started calls `onFailureAfterStart` and writes the incomplete marker instead of returning failure (`:150-152`). A final drain follows the optional truncation marker (`:192-197`).
  - `IsLastPage(itemCount, pageSize, rowsWritten, metadata)` (`:229`) does not trust a short page on its own: an empty page ends the export, but a short non-empty page ends it only when `rowsWritten` has reached `TotalItemCount`. The query pipeline clamps every page to its own ceiling (`EntityQueryPipeline.MaxUnboundedResultLimit = 1000`, `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/Query/EntityQueryPipeline.cs:23`), so a full clamped page can come back shorter than a larger requested page size, and ending there would cut the export short with no marker line.
  - `ResolveColumns` (`:293`) takes the shaped keys of the first row when there is one, or falls back to the DTO's own properties in declaration order, filtered by `fields`, for an empty result, so an export of nothing still owes a header row.
  - `ShapeRow` (`:323`) and `BuildCells` (`:334`) normalize a row (typed DTO or already-shaped dynamic object) to a camelCase-keyed dictionary and then project it onto the resolved column order, substituting null for a column a row does not carry.
  - `TruncationMarker` (`:356`) and `IncompleteMarker` (`:362`) format the two trailing marker lines with `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`. `ParseFields` (`:348`) splits a `fields=` value into a case-insensitive set.
- **Why it's built this way**: generic over `TEntityDTO` alone, because export needs only the DTO shape to compute its exclusion lists and resolve columns; the fetch delegate carries everything else the caller's specific entity/query pipeline needs. `internal static` keeps it a framework implementation detail behind `ExportAsync`, not a public surface a consumer can bind to. The last-page test reads the total rather than the page length because the page length is a property of the pipeline's clamp, not of the data. Buffering a page in memory before an awaited copy trades one page of memory for compatibility with default Kestrel synchronous-I/O rules.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: this type has no HTTP awareness of its own; the authorization symmetry, the fail-closed row scoping, and the no-output-caching caveats belong to [EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype), which is the only caller.
- **Where it's used**: only by [EntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>](#entitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype)`.ExportAsync`, which calls `ValidateFields` (`EntityControllerBase.cs:266`) and `WriteAsync` (`EntityControllerBase.cs:280`). Unit coverage is `EntityCsvExporterTests` and `EntityCsvExporterValueObjectTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Export/`).

### AuditHistoryControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.Administration` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Administration/AuditHistoryControllerBase.cs:48` · Level 6 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller that serves the recorded change history of one entity: a single GET at `{entity}/{key}`, paged, newest change first. A subclass names the audited entity types it serves and mounts the route; the base owns the action, the entity lookup, and the refusals.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base, for `HandleFailure`), [IAuditTrailReader](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#iaudittrailreader) re-exposed as the protected `Reader` property (`AuditHistoryControllerBase.cs:79`), [IAuditedEntity](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#iauditedentity) (the marker every served type must implement, checked at `:142`), [AuditHistoryEntryDTO](group-08-auth.md#audithistoryentrydto) on the wire, [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) for the two refusals, and [HasPermissionAttribute](group-08-auth.md#haspermissionattribute) with [AdministrationPermissions](group-08-auth.md#administrationpermissions)`.ViewAuditTrail` on the class (`:47`). Externals: ASP.NET Core MVC and `System.ComponentModel.DataAnnotations`' `[Range]`.
- **Concept (a closed entity vocabulary on an open route).** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether endpoints have a stable, predictable shape and error contract: the `{entity}` segment is not a free-form type name but a key into a map built once at construction, so an unknown name answers 404 with the stable code `AuditHistory.UnknownEntity` (`:51`, returned at `:106-113`) and a blank key answers 400 with `AuditHistory.KeyRequired` (`:54`, returned at `:115-122`), both as Problem Details through `HandleFailure`. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: the class-level `[HasPermission(AdministrationPermissions.ViewAuditTrail)]` (`:47`) gates the action on a capability rather than a role name, and only the types a subclass lists are reachable, so the route cannot be used to probe arbitrary tables.
- **Walkthrough**
  - The constructor (`:69-76`) takes the reader and a `params Type[]` of served types, null-guards both, and builds `AuditedEntityTypes` (`:82`), a dictionary keyed by simple type name with `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase` (`:136`).
  - `BuildAliasMap` (`:131-150`) fails construction fast: it throws `ArgumentException` for an empty list (`:134`), a null entry (`:140`), a type that does not implement `IAuditedEntity` (`:143`), and two types sharing a simple name, which would make the route ambiguous (`:146`).
  - `GetHistoryAsync(entity, key, page, pageSize, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet("{entity}/{key}")]` at `:93`, method at `:99-128`) takes `page` as `[Range(1, int.MaxValue)]` defaulting to 1 (`:102`) and `pageSize` as `[Range(1, 500)]` defaulting to 50 (`:103`). After the two refusals it calls `Reader.GetForEntityAsync(entityType.FullName!, key, page, pageSize, cancellationToken)` (`:124-125`): the URL carries the short name, the reader is keyed by the full type name. It returns the entries as a bare array with `Ok(entries)` (`:127`), declared as `IReadOnlyList<AuditHistoryEntryDTO>` (`:94`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-075](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/075-audit-trail.html) records the audit trail; this base is its read surface. Taking the served types as constructor arguments keeps the knowledge of which entities a module audits in the module's own subclass, while the framework owns the HTTP shape and the validation once. Validating at construction turns a misconfigured list into a startup failure instead of a request-time 404.
- **Where it's used**: ADC derives one subclass per module: Conference (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/AuditHistory/AuditHistoryController.cs`), Engagement (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.API/Controllers/AuditHistoryController.cs`) and Identity (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/AuditHistory/AuditHistoryController.cs`), each with its own test class. The UI reads it through `IAuditHistoryUIService` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Auditing/IAuditHistoryUIService.cs`); the framework's coverage is `AuditHistoryControllerBaseTests`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the `[Range]` bounds are enforced by MVC model validation; whether `IAuditTrailReader` clamps the page size again is outside the cited span and not determinable from the lines read here.

### CorrelationContext
> MMCA.Common.Infrastructure · `MMCA.Common.Infrastructure.Context` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Context/CorrelationContext.cs:10` · Level 6 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the scoped service that holds the correlation ID for the current request, defaulting to the enclosing background hop's correlation ID when one is being delivered, and otherwise to a fresh GUID, until middleware or a restore sets it.
- **Depends on**: [ICorrelationContext](#icorrelationcontext) (Level 0, `MMCA.Common.Application.Interfaces`), the abstraction it implements (`CorrelationContext.cs:1`, `:9`), and [AmbientOrigin](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ambientorigin) (same namespace), whose `Current?.CorrelationId` seeds the default (`CorrelationContext.cs:13`). Uses BCL `Guid`.
- **Concept (where the correlation value actually lives).** The pattern itself is introduced at [ICorrelationContext](#icorrelationcontext); this is its one shipped implementation. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: the eager default is what makes correlation always-on, so a log line emitted from a path with no HTTP request still carries an ID, and a handler delivered from the outbox or a consumer inherits the originating request's ID instead of minting a disconnected one. `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]`: the concrete holder sits in Infrastructure while every consumer depends on the Application interface.
- **Walkthrough**: `CorrelationId` (`CorrelationContext.cs:13`) is `{ get; private set; }`, initialized eagerly to `AmbientOrigin.Current?.CorrelationId ?? Guid.NewGuid().ToString("N")` so a value always exists even if no middleware runs (a background processor, a test path, a gRPC call). `SetCorrelationId(string)` (`CorrelationContext.cs:16-20`) overwrites it, guarding the input with `ArgumentException.ThrowIfNullOrWhiteSpace` (`:18`) so a blank header can never wipe the ID. The private setter means the only write path is that one guarded method.
- **Why it's built this way**: a scoped holder with an eager default keeps correlation cheap and unconditional: every code path has an ID without a null check, and inbound requests still adopt the caller's ID for cross-service tracing. The ambient-origin default lets a restored background hop continue the trace it came from. The `"N"` GUID format (32 hex digits, no hyphens) keeps the fallback compact in log lines and headers. Registration is `TryAddScoped` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/DependencyInjection.cs:342`): scoped, so one instance lives per request, and `TryAdd`, so a host that wants its own implementation can register it first and win.
- **Where it's used**: [CorrelationIdMiddleware](#correlationidmiddleware) resolves it per request and calls `SetCorrelationId` with the inbound `X-Correlation-ID` header, falling back to the current `Activity` trace ID and then to `HttpContext.TraceIdentifier` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Middleware/CorrelationIdMiddleware.cs:34-38`), then echoes the value back on the response through `OnStarting` (`:37-41`). Downstream, [LoggingCommandDecorator<TCommand, TResult>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#loggingcommanddecoratortcommand-tresult) and [LoggingQueryDecorator<TQuery, TResult>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#loggingquerydecoratortquery-tresult) take it as a constructor dependency and wrap the ID into their log scope for the full pipeline duration. The outbox and consumer restore paths read and set it through `AmbientOrigin` (`AmbientOrigin.cs:276`, `:184`).

### FeatureFlagsControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.Administration` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Administration/FeatureFlagsControllerBase.cs:38` · Level 6 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for runtime feature-flag administration: list every declared flag with its configured, stored and effective value, set one flag's stored override, and clear it again. A subclass mounts the route; the base owns the three actions.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base, for `HandleFailure`), [IFeatureFlagAdministrationService](#ifeatureflagadministrationservice), taken as the primary-constructor parameter (`FeatureFlagsControllerBase.cs:38`) and re-exposed as the protected `Administration` property (`:41`), [FeatureFlagStatusResponse](#featureflagstatusresponse) and [SetFeatureFlagOverrideRequest](#setfeatureflagoverriderequest) on the wire, and [HasPermissionAttribute](group-08-auth.md#haspermissionattribute) with [AdministrationPermissions](group-08-auth.md#administrationpermissions)`.ManageFeatures` on the class (`:37`).
- **Concept (a thin admin surface over the override store).** `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: like the other admin bases in this group, every action is a one-line translation from HTTP to `IFeatureFlagAdministrationService` (DIP), mapping a failed `Result` through `HandleFailure` and a success to the response, so no flag rule lives in the controller. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: the class-level `[HasPermission(AdministrationPermissions.ManageFeatures)]` (`:37`) gates all three actions on a capability, so turning a flag on in production is an authorized, attributable act rather than a configuration edit.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `GetAllAsync(cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet]` at `:49`, method at `:53-59`) returns `IReadOnlyList<FeatureFlagStatusResponse>` from `Administration.ListAsync`: per the XML summary, each flag's kind, owner, configured value, stored override and effective value (`:44-45`).
  - `SetOverrideAsync(name, request, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPut("{name}")]` at `:68`, method at `:74-84`) null-guards the body (`:79`) and calls `Administration.SetOverrideAsync(name, request, cancellationToken)` (`:81`), returning the flag's state afterwards. The request carries a required reason (`:65`); 400 and 404 are declared (`:70`, `:73`).
  - `ClearOverrideAsync(name, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpDelete("{name}")]` at `:93`, method at `:98-103`) removes the stored override so configuration is in force again and answers 204; clearing a flag that has no override succeeds (`:87-88`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html) governs feature-flag management. Shipping the HTTP shape once means every module that hosts flags exposes the same three routes, which is what lets one shared admin page drive them. All actions are `virtual`, so a subclass can override one without re-declaring routing.
- **Where it's used**: ADC mounts it in two modules, Conference (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/FeatureFlags/AdminFeatureFlagsController.cs`) and Engagement (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.API/Controllers/AdminFeatureFlagsController.cs`). The UI client is `IFeatureFlagAdminUIService` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Administration/IFeatureFlagAdminUIService.cs`); the framework's coverage is `FeatureFlagsControllerBaseTests`.

### MachineClientsControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.MachineClients` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/MachineClients/MachineClientsControllerBase.cs:39` · Level 6 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for administering machine clients (OAuth client-credentials callers): list them, create one with its secret, rotate and promote a secondary secret, and revoke a client. A subclass mounts the route; the base owns the actions.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base, for `HandleFailure`), [IMachineClientService](group-08-auth.md#imachineclientservice) resolved per request (`MachineClientsControllerBase.cs:42-43`), [CreateMachineClientRequest](group-08-auth.md#createmachineclientrequest), [MachineClientResponse](group-08-auth.md#machineclientresponse) and [MachineClientSecretResponse](group-08-auth.md#machineclientsecretresponse) on the wire, [IdempotentAttribute](#idempotentattribute) and [NonIdempotentAttribute](#nonidempotentattribute) on the write actions, and [HasPermissionAttribute](group-08-auth.md#haspermissionattribute) with [AdministrationPermissions](group-08-auth.md#administrationpermissions)`.ManageClients` on the class (`:38`).
- **Concept (a secret is never a cacheable response).** `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses how credentials and sensitive data are handled end to end: `CreateAsync` and `RotateAsync` return the only copy of a client secret, so both are marked `[NonIdempotent]` (`:69`, `:94`) with reasons stating that a replay would leave the secret at rest in the idempotency replay cache, while `PromoteAsync` and `RevokeAsync`, whose responses carry no secret and whose effect is the same on a retry, are `[Idempotent]` (`:116`, `:141`). `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: the controller takes no constructor dependency; the private `EnabledMachineClients` property (`:42-43`) resolves the service with `GetService` and keeps it only when `IsEnabled` is true, so on a host where the feature is off every action answers a plain 404 rather than failing to construct.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `GetAllAsync(cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet]` at `:48`, method at `:53-62`) lists every client, revoked ones included, via `ListAsync`.
  - `CreateAsync(request, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPost]` at `:68`, method at `:75-87`) null-guards the body (`:79`) and creates the client and its service account; the `MachineClientSecretResponse` carries the secret once.
  - `RotateAsync(id, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPost("{id:guid}/rotate")]` at `:93`, method at `:100-109`) issues a secondary secret; both secrets work until it is promoted, and a second rotation replaces the waiting secret (`:94`). 409 is declared (`:99`).
  - `PromoteAsync(id, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPost("{id:guid}/promote")]` at `:115`, method at `:122-131`) makes the secondary secret primary; the old primary stops working.
  - `RevokeAsync(id, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPost("{id:guid}/revoke")]` at `:140`, method at `:146-155`) stops the secrets working and, per the summary, makes every service reject the tokens the client holds; revoking a revoked client succeeds (`:134-135`).
  - Every action opens with the same `EnabledMachineClients is not { } machineClients` guard returning `NotFound()` and closes with `result.IsFailure ? HandleFailure(result.Errors) : Ok(result.Value)`.
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-140](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/140-machine-clients-oauth-client-credentials.html) introduces machine clients as an opt-in capability; resolving the service per action keeps the controller mountable on a host that has not opted in. The rotate-then-promote pair gives a zero-downtime secret rollover: the caller deploys the new secret while the old one still works, then promotes. The idempotency split follows [ADR-017](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/017-request-idempotency.html).
- **Where it's used**: ADC's `AdminMachineClientsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/MachineClients/AdminMachineClientsController.cs`); the UI client is `IMachineClientAdminUIService` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Administration/IMachineClientAdminUIService.cs`). The framework tests mount it through `MachineClientTestHost`.

### MachineTokenControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.MachineClients` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/MachineClients/MachineTokenControllerBase.cs:40` · Level 6 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for the OAuth 2.0 client-credentials token endpoint: one anonymous, form-encoded POST that issues an access token to a machine client and speaks RFC 6749's response and error format rather than Problem Details.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base), [IMachineClientService](group-08-auth.md#imachineclientservice) resolved per request (`MachineTokenControllerBase.cs:57`), [MachineTokenResponse](group-08-auth.md#machinetokenresponse) and [MachineTokenErrorResponse](group-08-auth.md#machinetokenerrorresponse) on the wire, [MachineClientErrors](group-08-auth.md#machineclienterrors) for the error-code mapping, [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error), [NonIdempotentAttribute](#nonidempotentattribute), and the `RateLimitPolicyAuthIp` policy name on [WebApplicationBuilderExtensions](#webapplicationbuilderextensions) (`:48`). Externals: `System.Net.Http.Headers.AuthenticationHeaderValue`, `Convert.FromBase64String`, `Uri.UnescapeDataString`.
- **Concept (the one endpoint that does not answer in Problem Details).** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: a token endpoint is consumed by generic OAuth client libraries, so it answers in the protocol's own shape: RFC 6749 error bodies (`invalid_client`, `unsupported_grant_type`, `invalid_request`) through `MachineTokenErrorResponse` (`:120-121`), 401 with a `WWW-Authenticate: Basic realm="token"` header (`:129`), and `Cache-Control: no-store` plus `Pragma: no-cache` on every response, success or refusal (`:62-64`). `[Rubric §11, Security]`: the endpoint is `[AllowAnonymous]` (`:47`) because the credentials are the request, so it is bounded instead by `[EnableRateLimiting(RateLimitPolicyAuthIp)]` (`:48`), and it is `[NonIdempotent]` (`:46`) so no bearer token is ever held at rest in the replay cache or handed to a retry minted for an earlier request.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `IssueTokenAsync(cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPost]` at `:45`, `[Consumes("application/x-www-form-urlencoded")]` at `:49`, method at `:55-76`) answers 404 when the service is missing or disabled (`:57-60`), sets the no-store headers (`:63-64`), reads the form when the request has form content and otherwise uses `FormCollection.Empty` (`:66-68`), extracts the credentials, and calls `IssueTokenAsync(form["grant_type"], clientId, clientSecret, ...)` (`:71-73`). Success is `Ok(result.Value)`; a failure goes to `TokenError` (`:75`).
  - `ReadClientCredentials(form)` (`:85-108`) prefers HTTP Basic (RFC 6749 section 2.3.1): it base64-decodes the parameter, returns `(null, null)` on a `FormatException` (`:96-99`) or when there is no `:` separator, and otherwise form-decodes each half through `FormDecode` (`:110`, `+` to space, then `Uri.UnescapeDataString`). Without a Basic header it reads the `client_id` and `client_secret` form fields (`:107`).
  - `TokenError(errors)` (`:115-124`) switches on the first error's code: `MachineClientErrors.UnsupportedGrantTypeCode` becomes 400 `unsupported_grant_type`, `InvalidRequestCode` becomes 400 `invalid_request`, and anything else (including an empty list, which defaults to `InvalidClientCode`) becomes `InvalidClient()` (`:126-131`), a 401 `invalid_client`.
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-140](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/140-machine-clients-oauth-client-credentials.html) adopts the standard client-credentials grant so callers can use stock OAuth clients; speaking the RFC's wire format is what makes that work. The per-IP limit follows [ADR-019](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/019-rate-limiting.html) and the brute-force posture of [ADR-029](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/029-authentication-brute-force-protection.html).
- **Where it's used**: ADC's `MachineTokenController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/MachineClients/MachineTokenController.cs`). `AnonymousEndpointTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Architecture/MMCA.Common.Architecture.Tests/Api/AnonymousEndpointTests.cs`) lists it among the framework's anonymous endpoints, and `MachineClientTestHost` exercises it.

### SignInLinkControllerBase<TSendCommand, TRedeemCommand>
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.SignInLinks` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/SignInLinks/SignInLinkControllerBase.cs:35` · Level 6 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for passwordless email sign-in: one anonymous POST that emails a single-use sign-in link, and one that redeems it for a token pair. A subclass supplies the two app command types, their factories, and the route.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base, for `HandleFailure`), two [ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) instances taken as primary-constructor parameters (`SignInLinkControllerBase.cs:35-37`) and re-exposed as `SendHandler` (`:42`) and `RedeemHandler` (`:45`), the constraints [ICommandWithRequest<out TRequest>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandwithrequestout-trequest) over [SendSignInLinkRequest](group-08-auth.md#sendsigninlinkrequest) (`:38`) and [IRedeemSignInLinkCommand](group-14-module-system-composition.md#iredeemsigninlinkcommand) (`:39`), [RedeemSignInLinkRequest](group-08-auth.md#redeemsigninlinkrequest) and [AuthenticationResponse](group-08-auth.md#authenticationresponse) on the wire, and [IdempotentAttribute](#idempotentattribute) / [NonIdempotentAttribute](#nonidempotentattribute).
- **Concept (an anonymous credential endpoint that reveals nothing).** `[Rubric §11, Security]`: the send action answers 202 Accepted on any well-formed request "whether or not the address holds an account" (`:74-75`, `Accepted()` at `:97`), so it cannot be used to enumerate accounts; both actions are `[AllowAnonymous]` and bounded by `[EnableRateLimiting(RateLimitPolicyAuthIp)]` (`:82-83`, `:110-111`); and the link carries the address and token in the URI fragment, which the page reads client-side and posts, so neither reaches a server log or a Referer header (`:101-103`). The controller follows the same generic command-factory shape as [EmailConfirmationControllerBase<TSendCommand, TConfirmCommand>](#emailconfirmationcontrollerbasetsendcommand-tconfirmcommand): the app owns its command types, the base dispatches them through the decorator pipeline.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `ClientIpAddress` (`:51`) reads `Connection.RemoteIpAddress`, which behind a proxy is the forwarded value only when the host configured `UseForwardedHeaders` (`:48-50`); `ClientUserAgent` (`:54`) reads the User-Agent header. Both are recorded on the session the redeem opens.
  - `CreateSendCommand(request)` (`:61`) and `CreateRedeemCommand(request, ipAddress, userAgent)` (`:71`) are the abstract factories; the XML docs give the expected one-line `=> new(...)` bodies.
  - `SendSignInLinkAsync(request, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPost("sign-in-link")]` at `:80`, `[Idempotent]` at `:81`, method at `:87-98`) dispatches the send command and answers `Accepted()` or a Problem Details failure for a malformed payload.
  - `RedeemSignInLinkAsync(request, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPost("sign-in-link/redeem")]` at `:108`, method at `:116-127`) dispatches the redeem command with the caller's IP and user agent and returns the `AuthenticationResponse`. It is `[NonIdempotent]` (`:109`): a replay would hand a retrying client tokens minted for an earlier call, while the single-use token makes a genuine retry fail, which is the correct answer.
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-116](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/116-identity-completions-opt-in.html) makes the identity completions opt-in; shipping them as abstract bases lets an app adopt the HTTP shape without its commands leaving its own Application assembly. The per-IP limiting follows [ADR-019](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/019-rate-limiting.html) and [ADR-029](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/029-authentication-brute-force-protection.html).
- **Where it's used**: ADC's `SignInLinkController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/SignInLinkController.cs`); `AnonymousEndpointTests` lists both actions among the framework's anonymous endpoints.

### ViewAsGrantsAdminControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.Administration` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Administration/ViewAsGrantsAdminControllerBase.cs:40` · Level 6 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for the view-as audit list: one GET that pages through the read-only support-access grants staff have opened, newest first, with each grant's status derived at read time. A subclass mounts the route.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base), [IViewAsGrantStore](group-08-auth.md#iviewasgrantstore), `IOptions<`[ViewAsSettings](group-08-auth.md#viewassettings)`>` and BCL `TimeProvider`, all resolved per action with `[FromServices]` (`ViewAsGrantsAdminControllerBase.cs:60-62`), [ViewAsGrantListRequest](group-08-auth.md#viewasgrantlistrequest) bound from the query string (`:59`), [ViewAsGrantSummaryResponse](group-08-auth.md#viewasgrantsummaryresponse) inside a [PagedCollectionResult<T>](group-01-result-error-handling.md#pagedcollectionresultt) with [PaginationMetadata](group-01-result-error-handling.md#paginationmetadata), and [HasPermissionAttribute](group-08-auth.md#haspermissionattribute) with [AdministrationPermissions](group-08-auth.md#administrationpermissions)`.ManageUsers` on the class (`:39`).
- **Concept (status derived from a clock, not stored).** `[Rubric §14, Testability]` assesses whether behavior can be exercised deterministically: the status of each grant is computed by `g.StatusAt(now)` (`:90`) against a `now` taken from the injected `TimeProvider` (`:75`), so a test pins the clock instead of waiting for a grant to expire. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: the list is the accountability half of view-as support access; it is gated on `ManageUsers` (`:39`) and dark (404) unless both `ViewAs:Enabled` and `HostsGrantStore` are true (`:70-73`).
- **Walkthrough**
  - `ListAsync(request, settings, grants, timeProvider, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet]` at `:53`, method at `:58-100`) null-guards its four inputs (`:65-68`), returns 404 on a host without the grant store (`:70-73`), then calls `grants.ListAsync(request, now, cancellationToken)` for a `(items, total)` pair (`:76`).
  - It projects each grant to a `ViewAsGrantSummaryResponse` carrying the staff and target ids and display names, the reason, the granted, expiry and end times, and the derived status (`:78-91`).
  - The metadata reports the page actually served: `Math.Clamp(request.PageSize, 1, ViewAsGrantListRequest.MaxPageSize)` and `Math.Max(request.PageNumber, 1)` (`:94-97`), with a comment noting the store clamps the same way (`:93`). It returns `Ok(new PagedCollectionResult<...>(rows, metadata))` (`:99`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-137](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/137-read-only-view-as-support-access.html) makes every view-as session a recorded grant; this endpoint is how an administrator reviews them. Resolving everything per action leaves the class with no constructor, so a subclass is a route and nothing else.
- **Where it's used**: ADC's `AdminViewAsGrantsController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/AdminViewAsGrantsController.cs`); the UI client is `IViewAsGrantsUIService` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Administration/IViewAsGrantsUIService.cs`); coverage is `ViewAsGrantsAdminEndpointTests`. The grants themselves are opened by [UsersAdminControllerBase<TUserDto>](#usersadmincontrollerbasetuserdto)`.StartViewAsAsync`.

### PasskeysControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.Passkeys` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Passkeys/PasskeysControllerBase.cs:41` · Level 8 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for WebAuthn passkeys: the signed-in user registers, lists, renames and deletes their passkeys, and an anonymous caller signs in with one. A subclass mounts the route; the base owns all seven actions.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base, for `HandleFailure`), [IPasskeyService](group-08-auth.md#ipasskeyservice) resolved per request (`PasskeysControllerBase.cs:53-54`), [PasskeyStepUp](group-08-auth.md#passkeystepup) and [PasskeyErrors](group-08-auth.md#passkeyerrors) for the recent-sign-in rule, [PasskeyOptionsResponse](group-08-auth.md#passkeyoptionsresponse) and [AuthenticationResponse](group-08-auth.md#authenticationresponse) on the wire, [NonIdempotentAttribute](#nonidempotentattribute), and BCL `TimeProvider` (falling back to `TimeProvider.System`, `:56-57`).
- **Concept (two-step ceremonies over single-use challenges).** A WebAuthn registration or sign-in is two requests: an `options` call that stores a fresh challenge and returns it, and a completion that proves possession by signing that challenge, which burns it. `[Rubric §11, Security]` shows in three places: every ceremony action is `[NonIdempotent]` (`:63`, `:111`, `:230`, `:255`) because a replayed response would hand back a challenge or tokens that are already spent; registration additionally requires a recent sign-in through `PasskeyStepUp.IsSatisfiedBy(User, UtcNow)` (`:81`, `:132`), refused with `PasskeyErrors.StepUpRequired`; and the anonymous sign-in pair is bounded by `[EnableRateLimiting(RateLimitPolicyAuthIp)]` (`:232`, `:257`). The controller has no constructor dependency: `EnabledPasskeys` keeps the service only when `IsEnabled` is true, so on a host that has not opted in every action answers 404.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `BeginRegistrationAsync` (`[HttpPost("register/options")]` at `:62`, `[Authorize]` at `:64`, method at `:69-89`) checks enabled (404), caller id (401), and step-up, then names the account through `DescribeCaller` (`:86`) and returns the creation options.
  - `DescribeCaller(userId)` (`:97-104`) takes the account name from `ClaimTypes.Email` or the raw `email` claim, falling back to the user id, and the display name from `ClaimTypes.Name` or `name`, falling back to the account name, so it works with mapped and unmapped JWT claims.
  - `CompleteRegistrationAsync(request, ...)` (`[HttpPost("register")]` at `:110`, method at `:118-139`) repeats the three checks and stores the passkey.
  - `ListAsync` (`[HttpGet]` at `:144`, method at `:149-163`) returns the caller's passkeys, oldest first. `RenameAsync(id, request, ...)` (`[HttpPatch("{id:guid}")]` at `:170`, method at `:176-194`) and `DeleteAsync(id, ...)` (`[HttpDelete("{id:guid}")]` at `:204`, method at `:210-224`) answer 404 for another account's passkey; the last passkey can be deleted only while the account still has a password or an external login, otherwise 409 `Passkeys.LastPasskey` (`:197-199`).
  - `BeginSignInAsync` (`[HttpPost("signin/options")]` at `:229`, `[AllowAnonymous]` at `:231`, method at `:236-245`) returns the request options. `CompleteSignInAsync(request, ...)` (`[HttpPost("signin")]` at `:254`, method at `:263-276`) passes `ClientIpAddress` (`:47`) and `ClientUserAgent` (`:50`) to the service (`:273`) and returns a token pair whose access token carries `mfa=passkey` (`:248-249`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-139](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/139-passkeys-webauthn-opt-in-sign-in.html) makes passkeys an opt-in sign-in method; resolving the service per request is what lets the controller ship in the framework and stay inert until a host opts in. Rate limiting follows [ADR-019](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/019-rate-limiting.html) and [ADR-029](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/029-authentication-brute-force-protection.html).
- **Where it's used**: ADC's `PasskeysController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/Passkeys/PasskeysController.cs`) with `PasskeysControllerTests`; registration support in `PasskeysServiceCollectionExtensions` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Passkeys/PasskeysServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`); framework coverage in `PasskeysControllerBaseTests` and `PasskeyTestHost`; `AnonymousEndpointTests` lists the two sign-in actions.

### TwoFactorControllerBase<TBeginCommand, TConfirmCommand, TDisableCommand, TRegenerateCommand>
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.TwoFactor` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/TwoFactor/TwoFactorControllerBase.cs:60` · Level 8 · class (abstract, partial)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for authenticator-app (TOTP) two-factor authentication on the signed-in account: read the status, begin and confirm an enrollment, regenerate recovery codes, and turn the factor off. A subclass supplies the four app command types, their factories, and the route.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base, for `HandleFailure`), four [ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) instances and an [ITwoFactorStore](group-08-auth.md#itwofactorstore), all primary-constructor parameters (`TwoFactorControllerBase.cs:60-65`) re-exposed as `BeginHandler`, `ConfirmHandler`, `DisableHandler`, `RegenerateHandler` and `Store` (`:72-84`); the constraints [IUserScopedRequest](group-14-module-system-composition.md#iuserscopedrequest) and [IUserScopedCommand<out TRequest>](group-14-module-system-composition.md#iuserscopedcommandout-trequest) over [TwoFactorCodeRequest](group-08-auth.md#twofactorcoderequest) (`:66-69`); [PasskeyStepUp](group-08-auth.md#passkeystepup) and [TwoFactorErrors](group-08-auth.md#twofactorerrors); [IAuthenticationService](group-08-auth.md#iauthenticationservice), resolved optionally to end sessions; and [RefuseMachineClientsAttribute](group-08-auth.md#refusemachineclientsattribute) on the class (`:59`).
- **Concept (a factor change invalidates every session signed in under the old state).** `[Rubric §11, Security]`: after a confirmed enrollment (`:181`) and after a disable (`:244`), `EndSessionsAsync` revokes every refresh session of the account (`:285-308`), so a session opened before the change cannot keep refreshing. The change is already saved at that point, so a failed revocation is caught (any exception except `OperationCanceledException`, `:299`) and logged rather than failing the request. The secret-bearing responses (enrollment, confirm, recovery codes) carry `[ResponseCache(NoStore = true, Location = ResponseCacheLocation.None)]` (`:122`, `:153`, `:193`) and every write is `[NonIdempotent]` (`:121`, `:152`, `:192`, `:221`), so neither an HTTP cache nor the replay cache holds a secret or a set of recovery codes. Beginning and confirming an enrollment require a recent sign-in through `PasskeyStepUp.IsSatisfiedBy(User, UtcNow)` (`:134`, `:168`), refused with `TwoFactorErrors.RecentSignInRequired`. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: the failure line is a source-generated `[LoggerMessage]` at Warning (`:272-273`) that names the user id, the change and the error codes or exception type, and no address.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `GetStatusAsync` (`[HttpGet]` at `:92`, method at `:98-114`) answers 401 without a caller id, 404 when the store holds no state for the account (`:106-109`), and otherwise whether the factor is on and how many recovery codes remain, reported as 0 while the factor is off (`:111-113`).
  - `BeginEnrollmentAsync` (`[HttpPost("enrollment")]` at `:119`, method at `:127-144`) checks the caller and the step-up, then dispatches `CreateBeginEnrollmentCommand(userId)` and returns the shared secret as a key and a provisioning URI.
  - `ConfirmEnrollmentAsync(request, ...)` (`[HttpPost("enrollment/confirm")]` at `:150`, method at `:159-184`) checks the caller and the step-up, dispatches the confirm command, ends the sessions, and returns the single-use recovery codes once.
  - `RegenerateRecoveryCodesAsync(request, ...)` (`[HttpPost("recovery-codes")]` at `:190`, method at `:199-213`) replaces the recovery codes after a code verifies; it applies no step-up check and ends no sessions.
  - `DisableAsync(request, ...)` (`[HttpPost("disable")]` at `:219`, method at `:227-247`) dispatches the disable command, ends the sessions, and answers 204.
  - The four abstract factories `CreateBeginEnrollmentCommand`, `CreateConfirmEnrollmentCommand`, `CreateDisableCommand` and `CreateRegenerateRecoveryCodesCommand` (`:252`, `:258`, `:264`, `:270`) each take the authenticated user id (and the presented code), so the account a command targets always comes from the token, never the body.
  - `EndSessionsAsync` (`:285-308`) returns early on a host without the framework's `IAuthenticationService` (`:288`), otherwise calls `RevokeAllSessionsAsync` (`:296`) and logs any failure (`:304-307`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the same generic command-factory shape as [SignInLinkControllerBase<TSendCommand, TRedeemCommand>](#signinlinkcontrollerbasetsendcommand-tredeemcommand): the app owns its commands and their handlers run through the decorator pipeline, while the HTTP shape, the step-up rule and the session revocation ship once in the framework.
- **Where it's used**: in the usage sites read here only by the framework's tests, `TwoFactorControllerBaseTests` and `TwoFactorControllerTestHost` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Controllers/TwoFactor/`). [UsersAdminControllerBase<TUserDto>](#usersadmincontrollerbasetuserdto) reads the same `ITwoFactorStore` to require enrollment before a listed role is granted.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: no ADR mentions this type; no ADC subclass appears among its usage sites, so whether an app mounts it is not determinable from source read here.

### UsersAdminControllerBase<TUserDto>
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.Administration` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Administration/UsersAdminControllerBase.cs:68` · Level 8 · class (abstract, partial)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for account administration: a single-account read, view-only lists of the account's live sessions and its sign-in history, the start of a read-only view-as session, lock/unlock, and a full role replacement. The write actions are guarded by a self-administration refusal, a privilege ceiling and, for roles, a second-factor enrollment check. A subclass supplies the app's `TUserDto` and mounts the route; the base owns every action.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base, for `HandleFailure`), [IUserAdministrationService<TUserDto>](group-08-auth.md#iuseradministrationservicetuserdto) taken as the primary-constructor parameter (`UsersAdminControllerBase.cs:68-69`) and re-exposed as the protected `Administration` property (`:72`), `UserIdentifierType`, the per-action-resolved [IUserSessionsAdministrationService](group-08-auth.md#iusersessionsadministrationservice) with its [RefreshSessionSummaryResponse](group-08-auth.md#refreshsessionsummaryresponse) and [SignInSummaryResponse](group-08-auth.md#signinsummaryresponse) items, the per-action-resolved [IViewAsService](group-08-auth.md#iviewasservice) and `IOptions<`[ViewAsSettings](group-08-auth.md#viewassettings)`>` with [StartViewAsRequest](group-08-auth.md#startviewasrequest) and [ViewAsGrantResponse](group-08-auth.md#viewasgrantresponse), the request type [SetUserRolesRequest](group-08-auth.md#setuserrolesrequest), and, resolved optionally from the request's services, [IPermissionRegistry](group-08-auth.md#ipermissionregistry) (`:456`), [ITwoFactorStore](group-08-auth.md#itwofactorstore) (`:433`) and `IOptions<`[MultiFactorAuthorizationSettings](group-08-auth.md#multifactorauthorizationsettings)`>` (`:451`). The ceiling evaluates permissions through [PermissionAuthorizationHandler](group-08-auth.md#permissionauthorizationhandler)`.Holds` (`:412`). The class carries [HasPermissionAttribute](group-08-auth.md#haspermissionattribute) with [AdministrationPermissions](group-08-auth.md#administrationpermissions)`.ManageUsers` (`:67`).
- **Concept (a thin admin surface with guards no service can forget).** `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: the controller depends on `IUserAdministrationService<TUserDto>` (DIP), not a concrete service, and every read is a thin translation from HTTP to that interface or to the framework's session and view-as services. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: the write actions add three refusals in a fixed order before the service runs. First, an administrator cannot lock, unlock or re-role their own account (`IsCaller` at `:485-487`), answered 403 with `UserAdministration.Self` (`:322-326`), because an administrator who locks themselves out cannot sign back in to undo it (`:206-208`). Second, the privilege ceiling: when the host registers an `IPermissionRegistry`, a request is refused 403 with `UserAdministration.PrivilegeCeiling` (`:333-337`) if the requested role, or the target's current role, grants a permission the caller does not hold; the refusal names no permission, and a role-only host with no registry applies no ceiling (class remarks at `:49-55`). Third, for roles only, a role listed under `Authorization:MultiFactor:RequiredForRoles` goes only to an account with two-factor authentication on, otherwise 409 `UserAdministration.SecondFactorNotEnrolled` (`:345-349`, remarks at `:58-62`). Every optional service is resolved per action or per request, so a host that never opts into sessions, view-as, permissions or two-factor does not need them registered. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: each successful write logs one source-generated Information line naming the action, the target and the acting administrator (`LogAdministered`, `:351-352`).
- **Walkthrough**
  - `GetAsync(userId, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet("{userId}")]` at `:80`, method at `:85-92`) reads one account through `Administration.GetAsync`.
  - `GetSessionsAsync(userId, sessions, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet("{userId}/sessions")]` at `:101`, method at `:105-115`) returns the account's live sessions, newest first, view only, as `IReadOnlyList<RefreshSessionSummaryResponse>`; it null-guards `sessions` (`:110`) and maps failures through `HandleFailure` (`:114`).
  - `GetSignInsAsync(userId, sessions, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet("{userId}/sign-ins")]` at `:125`, method at `:129-139`) returns the sign-in history through `sessions.GetSignInsAsync` (`:136`): one row per sign-in, ended ones included with how they ended, newest first, capped at the newest 50 per the XML summary (`:118-119`).
  - `StartViewAsAsync(userId, request, settings, viewAs, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPost("{userId}/view-as")]` at `:160`, method at `:168-193`) additionally requires `[HasPermission(AdministrationPermissions.ViewAsUsers)]` (`:161`) and is `[NonIdempotent]` (`:162`) because each call writes a new grant row, the audit record of the access. It answers 404 unless `ViewAs:Enabled` and `HostsGrantStore` are both true (`:178-181`), 401 without a caller id (`:183-186`), and otherwise calls `viewAs.StartAsync(staffUserId, userId, request?.Reason, cancellationToken)` (`:188-190`). The XML summary lists the refusals the service applies: the caller's own account, a missing, deleted, locked or machine-client account, and an account that itself holds `users:view-as` (`:142-146`).
  - `LockAsync` and `UnlockAsync` (`[HttpPost("{userId}/lock")]` at `:214` and `[HttpPost("{userId}/unlock")]` at `:246`, methods at `:220-238` and `:252-270`) run the self refusal (`:224`, `:256`), then `CheckTargetCeilingAsync` (`:229`, `:261`), then `Administration.SetLockedAsync(userId, locked, cancellationToken)` (`:235`, `:267`), and finish through `Completed` (`:237`, `:269`). Both are `[NonIdempotent]` (`:215`, `:247`): a lock is a state setter, and a cached 204 would answer a later unlock-then-lock cycle with a state the account is not in. The remarks on `LockAsync` (`:198-210`) state that a service derived from [UserAdministrationServiceBase<TUser, TUserDto>](group-08-auth.md#useradministrationservicebasetuser-tuserdto) makes the lock take effect at once by writing the soft-deleted user marker, so every host refuses the access tokens the account already holds, and by revoking its refresh sessions; a service that only revokes sessions leaves each issued access token working until it expires.
  - `SetRolesAsync(userId, request, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPut("{userId}/roles")]` at `:280`, method at `:286-315`) null-guards the request (`:291`), runs the self refusal (`:293`), `CheckRoleChangeCeilingAsync` (`:298`) and `CheckSecondFactorEnrollmentAsync` (`:304`), then replaces the account's complete role set through `Administration.SetRolesAsync(userId, request.Roles, cancellationToken)` (`:310-312`): it is a replace, not a merge.
  - `CheckRoleChangeCeilingAsync` (`:363-376`) acts only when exactly one role is requested (`roles is not [var requestedRole]`, `:368`); zero or several pass untouched so the service's exactly-one-role refusal is what the caller receives (`:355-357`). It checks the requested role, then the target's current role through `CheckTargetCeilingAsync` (`:386-402`), which returns success with no registry and otherwise loads `Administration.GetRoleAsync` (`:393`), passing through the service's not-found failure for an unknown account. `CallerHoldsEveryPermissionOf` (`:410-412`) is the shared predicate.
  - `CheckSecondFactorEnrollmentAsync` (`:425-443`) passes without a load when there are no request services, no requested role is listed (`RequestsAListedRole`, `:449-453`, case-insensitive with blank entries ignored), or no `ITwoFactorStore` is registered; otherwise it requires `state?.IsTwoFactorEnabled == true` (`:440-442`).
  - `Completed(result, action, userId)` (`:466-480`) maps a failure through `HandleFailure`, and on success logs the administered line and returns 204.
- **Why it's built this way**: like the other admin and auth controller bases in this group, the HTTP shape (routes, status codes, `ProblemDetails` on every failure path) ships once in the framework and an app supplies only its `TUserDto` and its `IUserAdministrationService<TUserDto>` implementation, so the account-management surface stays identical across apps. Putting the self, ceiling and enrollment refusals in the base rather than in the app service means no app can ship an administration surface without them. View-as follows [ADR-137](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/137-read-only-view-as-support-access.html), the session views follow [ADR-097](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/097-multi-device-refresh-sessions.html), and the idempotency markings follow [ADR-017](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/017-request-idempotency.html).
- **Where it's used**: ADC's `UsersAdminController` is the current subclass (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/UsersAdminController.cs`), backed by `UserAdministrationService` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Application/Users/Administration/UserAdministrationService.cs`) implementing `IUserAdministrationService<TUserDto>` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Auth/Administration/IUserAdministrationService.cs`). On the web host the same-origin proxy (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI.Web/SameOriginProxy/SameOriginApiProxyEndpoint.cs`) moves the view-as token into its overlay cookie, leaving the admin's own session untouched (`:151-152`), and the UI starts it from `ViewAsButton` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Components/Auth/ViewAsButton.razor.cs`). The framework's coverage is `AdministrationControllerBaseTests`, `UsersAdminSessionsEndpointTests`, `UsersAdminSignInsEndpointTests` and `ViewAsEndpointTests`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: [ADR-116](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/116-identity-completions-opt-in.html) mentions this surface, but the opt-in mechanics it governs live outside the cited span. The 15-minute view-as lifetime and the refusal list are stated in the XML summary (`:142-146`) and enforced inside `IViewAsService`, outside this file. Whether ADC registers an `IPermissionRegistry` or an `ITwoFactorStore` (and so applies the ceiling and the enrollment check) is not determinable from the lines read here.

### DataExportControllerBase<TQuery>
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.Privacy` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Privacy/DataExportControllerBase.cs:61` · Level 10 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for the data-subject export endpoint (`GET {route}/{userId}/export`, the GDPR/CCPA access and portability request). A subclass supplies its app's query type and a route; the base owns the action, the authorization posture, the feature gate, and the file-download delivery.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base, for `HandleFailure` at `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/ApiControllerBase.cs:49`), [IQueryHandler<in TQuery, TResult>](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#iqueryhandlerin-tquery-tresult) closed over [UserDataExportDTO](group-08-auth.md#userdataexportdto), [ICurrentUserService](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice), [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error), [PrivacyFeatures](group-08-auth.md#privacyfeatures), and the constraint [IUserOwnedRequest](group-14-module-system-composition.md#iuserownedrequest) on `TQuery` (`DataExportControllerBase.cs:61-64`). Externals: ASP.NET Core MVC (including its `IOptions<JsonOptions>`), `System.Text.Json`, `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection` and `Microsoft.Extensions.Options` (`:6-7`), and `Microsoft.FeatureManagement.Mvc`'s `[FeatureGate]`.
- **Concept (a privacy endpoint shipped as a base class, not a registered controller).** `[Rubric §30, Compliance/Privacy/Data Governance]` assesses whether legal obligations such as subject access, portability, and erasure are first-class code rather than manual operations: the access/portability half of a DSAR is framework code here, so an app gets it by subclassing rather than by re-implementing the dispatch, the ownership posture, and the delivery format. `[Rubric §11, Security]` shows up as **defence in depth**: the class-level `[Authorize]` (`:59`) demands an authenticated caller, while the handler independently enforces owner-or-privileged-role, so the endpoint cannot leak another subject's data even if a subclass is mounted without its own `[Authorize]` (`:52-55`). `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven Design]` covers the `[FeatureGate(PrivacyFeatures.DataExport)]` (`:60`, the flag string is `"Privacy.DataExport"` at `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/Privacy/PrivacyFeatures.cs:12`): the whole surface stays off, answered by [DisabledFeatureHandler](#disabledfeaturehandler)'s 404, until a host deliberately turns the flag on.
- **Walkthrough**
  - The primary constructor takes the app's query handler and [ICurrentUserService](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) (`:61-63`); the latter is re-exposed as the protected `CurrentUserService` property (`:70`) so a subclass can reuse it. `ExportContentType = "application/json"` (`:67`) is the one media type the package is served as.
  - `ExportAsync(UserIdentifierType userId, CancellationToken)` (`:84-86`) is routed by the action-level `[HttpGet("{userId}/export")]` (`:79`) only: the route prefix lives on the subclass, so a controller routed at `Users` serves `/Users/{userId}/export` (`:41-43`). The declared responses are 200 with a `UserDataExportDTO`, plus 401/403/404 as `ProblemDetails` (`:80-83`).
  - It reads `CurrentUserService.UserId` first and, when there is none, short-circuits through `HandleFailure` with `Error.Unauthorized("Privacy.Unauthorized", ...)` (`:88-92`), so even the unauthenticated case comes back as RFC 9457 Problem Details rather than a bare status.
  - It then builds the app's query through the abstract `CreateQuery(userId, currentUserId, CurrentUserService.Role)` factory (`:94`, declared at `:126-129`), awaits the handler (`:95-96`), and unwraps the [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) with `result.TryGetValue(out var export)`, mapping a failure through `HandleFailure(result.Errors)` (`:98-101`), which is what turns the handler's own ownership refusal into a 403.
  - The role passed to `CreateQuery` is the caller's FIRST role claim (`ICurrentUserService.Role`), which the parameter doc notes is the only one on the single-role tokens `TokenService` mints; a host issuing multi-role tokens overrides `CreateQuery` to pass its privileged role when `CurrentUserService.IsInRole` holds, as `OwnershipHelper.IsAdmin` does (`:119-124`).
  - On success it serializes the package itself with `JsonSerializer.SerializeToUtf8Bytes(export, json)` (`:109`) and returns `File(payload, ExportContentType, BuildFileName(...))` (`:111`). The options are the host's MVC JSON options, resolved per request as `HttpContext.RequestServices?.GetService<IOptions<JsonOptions>>()?.Value.JsonSerializerOptions`, with `JsonSerializerOptions.Web` only as the fallback when none resolve (`:107-108`). The inline comment (`:103-106`) is explicit about why: `Ok(export)` would content-negotiate and render inline, and this document exists to be saved, while reusing the MVC options (the host's converters included) keeps the payload byte-shape identical to every other response the API produces.
  - `BuildFileName` (`:140-141`) composes `user-data-{userId}-{yyyyMMdd}.json` with `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`, taking the date from the package's own `UserDataExportDTO.GeneratedOn` so the file name and the document always agree, and so a saved file sorts and parses the same in every locale (`:131-136`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-076](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/076-data-subject-export.html) hoists the export idiom that ADC and Store each wrote by hand. It ships as an **abstract base with a `CreateQuery` factory** rather than as a concrete controller added through an application part because the query type is app-owned (each app's `ExportUserDataQuery` lives in its own Application assembly), and a concrete controller could not construct a type it cannot see (`DataExportControllerBase.cs:46-49`). The route staying on the subclass follows the [AuthControllerBase](#authcontrollerbase) precedent: the app owns its URL space (`:41-43`).
- **Where it's used**: both full apps now derive from it, and each subclass is nothing but a route plus a query factory: ADC's `UsersDataExportController` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/UsersDataExportController.cs:26-35`) and Store's (`MMCA.Store/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.Store.Identity.API/Controllers/UsersDataExportController.cs:19-29`). Both are routed at the literal `Users` rather than `[controller]`, and both say why: `[Route("[controller]")]` would resolve to `UsersDataExport` and move the published path (ADC `:19-22`, Store `:9-13`). The framework's own coverage is `DataExportControllerBaseTests`, whose `TestDataExportController` is the third subclass in source (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API.Tests/Controllers/Privacy/DataExportControllerBaseTests.cs:267`).

### RolesAdminControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.Administration` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Administration/RolesAdminControllerBase.cs:61` · Level 10 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for role-permission administration: list every role with its compiled and stored permissions, read the role/permission catalog, read one role, and replace one role's stored grants.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base, for `HandleFailure`), the app-supplied `IRoleAdministrationService` re-exposed as the protected `Administration` property (`:66`), and `ICurrentUserService`, re-exposed as `CurrentUserService` (`:69`), taken together as primary-constructor parameters (`:61-63`).
- **Concept (a closed-set catalog endpoint routed ahead of its own parameterized sibling).** `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: like [UsersAdminControllerBase<TUserDto>](#usersadmincontrollerbasetuserdto), the controller has no rule of its own, only translation to `IRoleAdministrationService` (DIP).
