The routing choice on `GetCatalogAsync` is the notable design point: `[HttpGet("catalog")]` (`:98`) is a literal segment that ASP.NET Core routing matches before the `[HttpGet("{role}")]` template (`:116`) below it, and the XML remarks on the method say so explicitly (`:93-95`), which is also why no role can be named "catalog": it would be unreachable through the `{role}` route. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: `SetPermissionsAsync` records who made the change through `ResolveChangedBy()` (`:150-152`, method at `:162-163`), which defaults to the caller's own `CurrentUserService.UserId` and is virtual so an app can substitute a display name instead.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `GetAllAsync(cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet]` at `:76`, method at `:80-84`) lists every role via `Administration.ListRolesAsync` (`:83`), returning `IReadOnlyList<RolePermissionsResponse>`.
  - `GetCatalogAsync(cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet("catalog")]` at `:98`, method at `:102-105`) returns the closed sets a role editor needs: the roles this surface lists and the permissions a stored grant may name, via `Administration.GetCatalogAsync` (`:105`).
  - `GetAsync(role, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet("{role}")]` at `:116`, method at `:121-125`) reads one role's compiled and stored permissions via `Administration.GetRoleAsync` (`:125`).
  - `SetPermissionsAsync(role, request, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPut("{role}/permissions")]` at `:137`, method at `:143-152`) guards the request (`:148`), then replaces the role's stored permission grants via `Administration.SetStoredPermissionsAsync(role, request.Permissions, ResolveChangedBy(), cancellationToken)` (`:150-152`), returning the role's post-edit state.
  - `ResolveChangedBy()` (`:162-163`) is `CurrentUserService.UserId?.ToString(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture)`, or `null` when the caller cannot be identified.
- **Why it's built this way**: the same admin-base pattern as [UsersAdminControllerBase<TUserDto>](#usersadmincontrollerbasetuserdto): the HTTP shape ships once in the framework, and an app supplies its `IRoleAdministrationService` implementation. Keeping `ResolveChangedBy` virtual rather than hardcoding the user identifier lets an app record a friendlier audit label without touching the write path itself.
- **Where it's used**: ADC's `AdminRolesController` is the current subclass (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/AdminRolesController.cs`), consumed from the UI at `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.UI/Pages/Roles/RoleList.razor.cs`. The framework's own coverage is in `AdministrationControllerBaseTests`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: [ADR-116](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/116-identity-completions-opt-in.html) mentions this surface, but the opt-in mechanics it governs live outside the cited span; not determinable from the lines read here.

### LegalAcceptanceControllerBase
> MMCA.Common.API · `MMCA.Common.API.Controllers.Legal` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.API/Controllers/Legal/LegalAcceptanceControllerBase.cs:45` · Level 10 · class (abstract)

- **What it is**: the shipped base controller for the signed-in user's Terms of Service standing: one GET that reads whether the user has accepted the configured current version, and one POST that records the acceptance. An app subclass supplies only the route prefix.
- **Depends on**: [ApiControllerBase](#apicontrollerbase) (its base, for `HandleFailure`), [ILegalAcceptanceService](group-08-auth.md#ilegalacceptanceservice), [ICurrentUserService](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) (re-exposed as the protected `CurrentUserService`, `LegalAcceptanceControllerBase.cs:51`), and `IOptions<LegalAcceptanceOptions>` ([LegalAcceptanceOptions](group-08-auth.md#legalacceptanceoptions)), all taken as primary-constructor parameters (`:45-48`). It maps through [LegalAcceptancePolicy](group-08-auth.md#legalacceptancepolicy) and exchanges [LegalAcceptanceDTO](group-08-auth.md#legalacceptancedto) and [AcceptLegalTermsRequest](group-08-auth.md#acceptlegaltermsrequest) on the wire, at the route constant [LegalAcceptanceRoutes](group-08-auth.md#legalacceptanceroutes)`.Action` (`me/legal-acceptance`, `LegalAcceptanceRoutes.cs:13`).
- **Concept (the configured version is the only truth the controller trusts).** `[Rubric §11, Security]`: the POST does not store what the client sent. `LegalAcceptancePolicy.EnsureAcceptsCurrentVersion` (`:110-111`) proves the request names the configured current version, and then the service is called with the configured value (`:119-120`), with a comment recording that this keeps the stored version byte-identical to configuration (`:117-118`). `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: like the other admin and account bases, the controller holds no rule of its own, only translation to the service and policy (DIP).
- **Walkthrough**
  - `CurrentTermsVersion` (`:54`) is `LegalAcceptancePolicy.ResolveCurrentVersion(options.Value)`, `null` when no version is configured.
  - `GetLegalAcceptanceAsync(cancellationToken)` (`[HttpGet(LegalAcceptanceRoutes.Action)]` at `:59`, method at `:63`) returns `Ok(LegalAcceptanceDTO.Evaluate(null, null, null))` when no version is configured (`:66-69`), a 401 Problem Details failure (`Legal.Unauthorized`) when `UserId` is null, (`:71-75`), and otherwise asks `GetForCurrentUserAsync` (`:77`), unwraps the result with `result.TryGetValue(out var acceptance)` (`:81`), and returns the standing normalized against the current version through `LegalAcceptancePolicy.Normalize` (`:83`).
  - `AcceptLegalTermsAsync(request, cancellationToken)` (`[HttpPost]` plus `[Idempotent]` at `:93-94`, method at `:99`) applies the same 401 guard, then the version check described above, then `AcceptForCurrentUserAsync` with the configured version (`:119-121`), and returns the normalized standing through the same `TryGetValue` unwrap (`:123-125`). A version mismatch comes back as a failure through `HandleFailure`, surfaced as a 400 per the declared `ProducesResponseType`.
- **Why it's built this way**: the HTTP shape ships once in the framework and an app contributes its `ILegalAcceptanceService` implementation, the same pattern as the other base controllers in this group. `[Idempotent]` on the POST ([IdempotentAttribute](#idempotentattribute)) makes a retried acceptance replay the first response rather than run twice (see [ADR-017](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/017-request-idempotency.html)). Both actions are `virtual`, so an app can override one without re-declaring routing.
- **Where it's used**: ADC's `LegalAcceptanceController` is the current subclass and supplies only the route (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/LegalAcceptanceController.cs:24-28`); the Common coverage is `LegalAcceptanceControllerBaseTests`. [ADR-116](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/116-identity-completions-opt-in.html) mentions the surface.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: how the opt-in registration of this controller is wired is outside the cited span and not determinable from the lines read here.
